# 🏰 Rise of Git — Game Guide

An ambient, zero-maintenance kingdom game for teams using **Git**. Every developer rules a kingdom; every repository they work on becomes a building in their city. Nobody has to click anything — the realm grows from real Git activity.

---

## 1. The Realm at a Glance

| Thing in the game | Comes from |
|---|---|
| Kingdom | A Git user (ghost accounts are skipped) |
| Building | A repo the developer committed to or owns |
| Building level | Contribution XP earned in that repo (plus sealed 🪶 deed reports, §17) |
| Castle (town hall) level | Sum of all building levels |
| Building health / weeds | Days since the last commit to the repo |
| Townsfolk walking around | Commits in the past week |
| How lively the townsfolk are | Commit streak (days with commits; up to 2 days off in a row are forgiven) |
| Resources (gold, iron, …) | Share of a fixed pool every 15 min, weighted by past-week XP |
| Day and night, the season | Each harvest alternates day / night; the season turns weekly (§7 *Day, night & seasons*) |

Three background services run on the server (there is no manual sync button):

* **Git live refresh** — every `GIT_SYNC_INTERVAL_HOURS` (default **1 hour**, minimum 1) and once at server start, re-reads users, repos, PRs, reviews, and commits within a rolling **3-day window** (`LIVE_SYNC_LOOKBACK_DAYS` / `GIT_LOOKBACK_DAYS`, default 3) safety-capped at **5,000 per repo per sync** (`GIT_MAX_COMMITS_PER_REPO`). Never pays resources.
* **Historical backfill worker** ([backfillService.ts](server/src/backfillService.ts)) — runs gently in the background across all repositories starting from `BACKFILL_START_YEAR` (default **2020**) with a gentle pause between repos (`BACKFILL_DELAY_MS`, default **500 ms**), saving checkpoints in SQLite (`repositories.backfilled_at`) so complete repository history is indexed without stalling server startup or overloading Git.
* **Harvest** — every `SYNC_INTERVAL_MINUTES` (default **15**), pays out resources. Its last run time is saved in SQLite, so a restart never causes an extra harvest, and after downtime it catches up with a single harvest.

---

## 2. Where the Data Comes From

Server: [realmBuilder.ts](server/src/realmBuilder.ts), [gitFetch.ts](server/src/gitFetch.ts), [backfillService.ts](server/src/backfillService.ts)

1. **Users & emails** — every Git user, plus every primary and secondary email (`/admin/emails`, needs an admin token; falls back to public user search). The first time a login is seen, its Git account number is remembered, so sign-in can tell if the login later passes to someone else (§11).
2. **Repositories** — every repo that isn't empty or archived.
3. **Commits** — default-branch commits from a rolling **3-day window** (`LIVE_SYNC_LOOKBACK_DAYS` / `GIT_LOOKBACK_DAYS`, default 3) for live head sync, safety-capped at **5,000 per repo per sync** (`GIT_MAX_COMMITS_PER_REPO`), with line stats. A background backfill worker simultaneously indexes all historical commits back to **2020** (`BACKFILL_START_YEAR`). If the default branch doesn't exist (renamed / never pushed) the newest branch is read instead.
4. **Pull requests** — the 50 most recently updated per repo.
5. **Reviews** — fetched only for PRs touched in the last 7 days (keeps Git load low).
6. **Subscriptions** — repos each user watches.

Everything is upserted into SQLite (`server/data/realm.db`). Commits are never deleted, so history keeps accumulating past the lookback window.

### Who gets credit for a commit

Each commit is matched to a developer in this order:

1. An administrator's **per-repo match** of its email in its repo (a shared email, see *Developer Emails* below)
2. The Git account linked to the commit (`author.login`)
3. The commit email → any primary, secondary, or previously learned email
4. The author name → a user's full name or username, in either order (`"Jane Doe"` matches a Git name of `"Doe, Jane"` and the other way round)
5. Otherwise → **nobody**: the commit stays unassigned (it is never credited to the repo owner)

Once a commit email is matched it is remembered for that developer on every future sync — except an email an administrator matches per repo, which is never learned as a whole.

> 💡 If your commits show up under someone else (or not at all), add your commit email to your Git account (**Settings → Account → Email Addresses**). It is picked up on the next sync — or immediately when an administrator presses **🔄 Re-check Commits & Repos** in your developer profile, which re-reads your emails from Git, re-attributes matching commits (by email, and by name for unassigned ones; a name match never takes an email another developer holds) and rebuilds your buildings, levels and stats on the spot.

> 🛠️ **Administrators: 📧 Developer Emails** (Realm Administration → **Open**). Commits made with an email Git doesn't know (a laptop's default like `admin@desktop-…` or `Jane Doe@desktop-…`, which may contain spaces, a personal gmail, a GitHub `noreply` address) stay unassigned. The **Unmatched** tab lists every such email with its author names, commit count and last commit, and a collapsed list of the repos its commits are in (**▸ N repos** opens it; the search also finds repos); pick the developer on a row and press **Match**: every commit under that email becomes theirs at once (buildings, levels, titles and stats are rebuilt), and every future sync keeps the link. An email another developer held moves over with its commits; a developer's own Git email can't be moved. **Shared emails** (one address on commits by different people, e.g. a tool's `noreply` address): open the repo list, tick the repos that are this developer's, and **Match N repos** gives them only that email's commits in those repos; every future sync keeps attributing (email + repo) to them, before any other rule. The email's other repos stay on the list to be matched to someone else; once an email is matched per repo it can only be matched per repo (and an email already linked as a whole must be unassigned before it can be split). The **By developer** tab shows a developer's linked emails, per-repo matches with their repo (*only in …*); **✕** unassigns one and sends its commits back to Unmatched.

The **👤 developer profile** shows your Git activity: repositories, past-week commits and PRs, code volume, activity score, your **🎖️ Sovereign Titles** (§13) and a GitHub-style contribution calendar (at least a full year, more on wide screens). Game information — treasury, population, army, census — lives in the **👥 Kingdom** panel instead.

---

## 3. Contribution XP

Server: [scoring.ts](server/src/scoring.ts)

XP rewards meaningful work, not raw commit count. One ordinary commit ≈ **1 XP**.

| Action | XP |
|---|---|
| Commit (size-scaled by meaningful lines) | 0.5 – 1.0 |
| Big commit (500+ lines) | +0.5 / +1.0 / +1.5 bonus (see below) |
| Commit with no meaningful lines (empty, whitespace, comment-only, reformat, lockfile…) | 0 |
| Merge commit | 0 (the PR gets the credit) |
| Merged pull request | +3 |
| Open / unmerged pull request | +0.5 |
| Review on someone else's PR | +1.5 (once per PR per reviewer) |
| 🪶 Sealed deed report (work that isn't a commit, §17) | +0.5 / +1.0 / +1.5, at most 5 a day |

**Meaningful lines** ([diffQuality.ts](server/src/diffQuality.ts)): each new commit from the last 30 days has its diff fetched from Git once (stored in SQLite, up to 600 per sync). A changed line only counts if it is not:
* blank or whitespace-only,
* a comment (recognised per language: `//`, `/* */`, `#`, `--`, `<!-- -->`),
* in a lockfile (`package-lock.json`, `yarn.lock`, `composer.lock`, …), a minified/source-map file, or build output / vendored code (`dist/`, `build/`, `vendor/`, `node_modules/`, …),
* a pure reformat, re-indent or move (lines that match once whitespace is ignored cancel out).

Commits older than 30 days, or whose diff can't be fetched or is over 2 MB, use the raw line count. Commits with no meaningful lines earn 0 XP and don't use up the daily quota below.

**Commit size factor:** `0.5 + 0.5 × min(1, log10(1 + lines) / 2)` on meaningful lines — a one-line fix is ≈ 0.6 XP, 100+ changed lines is 1 XP.

**Big commit bonus:** added to the daily multiplier below, on every commit that day — a big commit past the daily quota keeps its full bonus.

| Counted lines | Bonus |
|---|---|
| 500 – 1000 | +0.5 |
| 1001 – 2000 | +1.0 |
| 2001+ | +1.5 (cap) |

So a 3000-line commit is worth 2.5 XP as one of the first five that day, 2.0 XP as the 6th–10th and 1.7 XP after that.

**Daily diminishing returns** (per developer, across *all* repos, so spreading spam over repos doesn't help):

| Commit # that day | Multiplier |
|---|---|
| 1 – 5 | × 1 |
| 6 – 10 | × 0.5 |
| 11+ | × 0.2 |

XP per commit = (daily multiplier + big commit bonus) × size factor. XP is credited to the repo the commit, PR or review belongs to.

Deed reports (§17) have their own daily cap (5 XP a developer a day) and skip the commit quota above: a deed never uses up a commit's place in the day, and commits never shrink a deed. The 👤 developer profile shows **XP by source**: ⌨️ commits, 🔀 PRs, 👁️ reviews and 🪶 deeds.

---

## 4. Building Your City

Server: [db.ts](server/src/db.ts) `getAllKingdomsFromDB`, [kingdomUtils.ts](server/src/kingdomUtils.ts)

### Which repos become buildings

| Repo | Result |
|---|---|
| You have commits in it | ✅ Building |
| You have a sealed 🪶 deed report on it (§17) | ✅ Building (a deed is work in that repo, like a commit) |
| You own it, watch it or are subscribed to it, but none of its commits are yours | ❌ Listed as *unbuilt* — "no commits" |
| Beyond your top **100** (`GIT_MAX_BUILDINGS`) | ❌ Listed as *unbuilt* — "over cap" |

Repos are ranked by `total lines changed + commits × 40`; the biggest come first.

### A developer with no commits

* No commits → an empty city with a Lv1 castle; owned and subscribed repos show only in the unbuilt list. Owning a repo is not enough: only a commit matched to you (§2) builds it.
* The first commit to any repo immediately turns it into a building on the next sync.

### Districts

Every building belongs to one of four districts, each occupying a quadrant of the city:

| District | Share | Quadrant | Townsfolk |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🏠 Houses (residential & commercial) | 35% | front-left | villagers |
| ⚔️ Soldier buildings | 15% | front-right (by the gate) | soldiers on patrol |
| 🌾 Food fields | 25% | back-right | farmers |
| 🐄 Animal fields | 25% | back-left | villagers + livestock |

* **First build:** repos are ranked biggest-first and dealt out — houses, then pastures, then farms, then barracks — using the 35/25/25/15 split. Small kingdoms: 1 repo → house; 2 → house + farm; 3 → house + pasture + farm. This plan is saved.
* **Later syncs:** existing buildings never move. A new repo joins whichever district is furthest below its quota and takes over a vacant plot there if there is one, otherwise the lowest free plot.
* **Removed repos** leave a **vacant plot** until a new repo takes it over.
* **Swap plots** (owner only): from a building's details, the owner can swap it with any other building. The two trade plots and districts — e.g. a Lv13 house swapped with a Lv1 barracks becomes a grand barracks, and the small repo moves into the house plot. Both keep their level and XP and are redrawn in their new district's style. District sizes never change, and swapping is free.

Slot 0 of each district sits closest to the central plaza; plots pack outward in rows along streets.

---

## 5. Building Levels & Designs

### Level from XP

`XP needed for level n = 5 + 0.8 × (n − 1)²` (Lv1 = 0 XP), capped at **Lv25**.

| Level | XP | Level | XP |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2 | 6 | 15 | 162 |
| 5 | 18 | 20 | 294 |
| 10 | 70 | 25 | 466 |

### Prestige (XP beyond the Lv25 cap)

Past Lv25 a building earns ★ prestige stars — **one per 200 XP** over the Lv25 threshold, with **no cap**. Lv25 is the grand structure; prestige adds **effects only** and the structures stay the same. Stars show as ★ up to ★★★★; from the 5th star on, the row becomes **one red ★ and the real total** (red ★ 7 = seven stars, 1,666+ XP), in the city labels, the City Directory and the building panel. The red ★ is the **top class** for a repo: the plot gets the legendary effects of the rarity ladder (§10 *Rarity*).

| Prestige | Effect on the plot |
|---|---|
| ★ (466+ XP) | Banners on poles at the plot entrance |
| ★★ (666+ XP) | Glowing gold border around the plot |
| ★★★ (866+ XP) | Floating sparkles over the plot |
| ★★★★ (1,066+ XP) | **Epic-class**: a low curtain of light round the plot's edge and motes orbiting it |
| red ★ (5+ stars, 1,266+ XP) | **Legendary-class**: a tall golden curtain of rising light round the whole plot, rising embers and orbiting motes |

No building plot gets a pillar of light: the curtain stands round the plot's edge, so a maxed city (an Overseer's, every plot ★★★★) reads as glowing plots, not a forest of pillars. Only the town hall keeps its beam (§6).

**Red-star plots in HD** (§10) are dressed as the legendary plots they are, before the curtain: the prestige banners become grand standards (a gilt pole on a stone foot, the civilization's cloth streaming with a gilt hem, a gilt crown with a pulsing gem and sparks rising off it), the glowing border becomes a gilt kerb round the pad set with pulsing gems (left open at the gate), and royal pennants stream from gilt rods on the corner towers' roofs.

### Structure tiers

Each district has four structure tiers, from small to grand:

| District | Tier 0 | Tier 1 | Tier 2 | Tier 3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 🏠 Houses | Hut | House | Manor | Great Hall |
| 🌾 Food | Crop patch | Field | Granary | Windmill |
| 🐄 Animal | Pen | Stable | Big barn | Ranch |
| ⚔️ Soldier | Tent | Barracks | Watchtower | Keep |

What stands on the plot at each level (counts of tier 0 / 1 / 2 / 3):

| Lv | Structures | Lv | Structures | Lv | Structures |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 1·0·0·0 | 10 | 0·3·0·0 | 19 | 1·3·2·0 |
| 2 | 2·0·0·0 | 11 | 1·3·0·0 | 20 | 0·2·0·1 |
| 3 | 3·0·0·0 | 12 | 0·1·1·0 | 21 | 0·3·0·1 |
| 4 | 0·1·0·0 | 13 | 0·2·1·0 | 22 | 0·2·1·1 |
| 5 | 1·1·0·0 | 14 | 1·2·1·0 | 23 | 0·3·1·1 |
| 6 | 2·1·0·0 | 15 | 0·3·1·0 | 24 | 0·2·2·1 |
| 7 | 0·2·0·0 | 16 | 1·3·1·0 | 25 | 0·3·2·1 |
| 8 | 1·2·0·0 | 17 | 0·2·2·0 | | |
| 9 | 2·2·0·0 | 18 | 0·3·2·0 | | |

So the plot evolves: huts → houses (Lv4) → first manor (Lv12) → great hall (Lv20).

**The tiers climb the rarity ladder** (§10 *Rarity*) in every civilization's own style: tier 0 is **common** (plain and matte, nothing lit), tier 1 **uncommon** (the civilization's one accent and a small prop), tier 2 **rare** (metal or glowing trim, lit windows and one looping effect: chimney smoke, a turning vane or crystal, a streaming banner, a smoking fire pit), tier 3 **epic** (a bigger silhouette, glowing or gilded details and something circling it):

| Civilization | Tier 0 | Tier 1 | Tier 2 | Tier 3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 🏰 Valoria | Thatched cottage, crop patch, pen, canvas tent | Half-timbered house with gold window boxes and sign, a field with a gold-scarfed scarecrow, a stable with a gold horseshoe and lantern, barracks with gold shields and a weapon rack | Stone manor with a smoking chimney, a granary whose copper silo turns its vane, a big barn with a vane on its cupola, a round watchtower with a brazier and a streaming banner | Great hall under a gilded dome, a windmill with a lantern-lit gallery, a ranch with a gilded silo, a keep behind a torch-lit gatehouse; doves or hawks circling |
| 🌿 Sylvandale | Round leaf hut, herb patch, pen, leaf pavilion | Two-storey round house with a cyan banner, a hedged field with a lantern post, a pale stable with a cyan sign, barracks with cyan shields and a bow rack | Willow manor of silver-ringed towers, a storehouse with a swaying silo tree, a round barn, a watch spire, each with a streaming pennant | Moon hall under a tall white spire, a great tree mill with leaf sails, a glade ranch, a three-spire keep; moonstones glowing, wisps circling |
| ⚒️ Ironforge | Turf dugout, crop patch, pen, dugout guard post | Squat stone house with an orange door band and an anvil, a walled field with a keg, a stone stable, a bunker with an axe rack | Iron-banded forge hall with a smoking chimney, a storehouse with a turning vane, a stone barn with a smoking forge chimney, a square tower with a brazier and banner | Bastion hall with a forge-lit gate and molten channels, a forge mill with a turning gear, a hold ranch, a bastion with a hammer crest; embers circling |
| 🪓 Drak'mar | Hide yurt, gourd patch or drying rack, pen, hide war tent | Log hut with a red war-paint hand and a skull totem, a staked field with a war drum, a stable with red stall doors, barracks with a red war shield | Iron-spiked longhouse with a smoking fire pit, a smokehouse, a beast hall with a fire pit, a log watchtower with a streaming banner | Great war hall with a burning-eyed skull gate and braziers, a war mill with a beacon, a warg ranch, a war fort behind a palisade; crows circling |
| 🌌 Nethergarde | Dull obsidian hut with a dark crystal, crystal or lotus patch (unlit), pen, canopy | Obsidian house with a violet banner and an unlit crystal lantern, a field with a violet-runed ward stone, a stable with violet-banded doors, barracks with violet shields | Silver-trimmed rune manor, a crystal vault and silo, a rune barn, a watch spire, each with a crystal turning over it | Obsidian spire hall with rune bands and a great crystal, a crystal tower with turning rings, a star ranch, a void citadel; shards orbiting |

Turning vanes and circling birds cast no shadow (the shadow map is drawn once).

**HD structures**: with HD graphics (§10) every structure and the dressing round it is drawn in its HD version, on the same ladder. Everything shows what it is made of: plaster and painted walls, dressed stone in courses, log and plank walls with their grain, roofs in rows of tiles or slates (Valoria's blue tiles and slate, Ironforge's rust tiles, Nethergarde's violet tiles), thatch and straw in strands, Sylvandale's leaf roofs fringed with leafy clumps round their eaves, Drak'mar's hide roofs and tents, glass in the windows, crystals that catch the light. Windows get frames, sills and glazing bars (battens across closed shutters), doors a frame and lintel, chimneys a cap (and a clay pot on a smoking one), vanes an arrow, tail fin and compass cross, circling birds a body, head, tail and wings held out in a glide, hay bales their twine, lamp posts a frame round their lantern, pennants a crossbar and finial. Tiers add detail: a **common** stays plain but well made (the cottage's bound thatch ridge, corner posts and split logs; the leaf hut's leafy eave; a dugout's lintel and stones; the yurt's ropes and smoke ring; the obsidian hut's crystal socket); an **uncommon** its accent in a real material and small props (flowers in Valoria's window boxes, joists under the jetty and a gold horseshoe; Sylvandale's leaf-shaped sea-glass shields and real bows with strings; Ironforge's hooped kegs and an anvil with its horn and a hammer; Drak'mar's real skulls lashed to the totems and a laced war drum; Nethergarde's kite shields in violet crystal and obsidian lantern frames); a **rare** its metal (iron-banded doors with a ring pull, iron rivets, silver frames and bands in Sylvandale and Nethergarde, a brazier's rim over glowing coals, dressed quoins and a stone step on the manor, white X-braced barn doors); an **epic** the most (gilt ribs over Valoria's dome and gilt cornices on its towers, a portcullis grid under a stone arch in the keep, the windmill's railed gallery and gilt lanterns; silver railings and leafy eaves on every elf spire, roots at the living trees' feet; Ironforge's gate arch, kerbed molten channels and brass bands; Drak'mar's horned great skull with teeth and bone-tipped banners; Nethergarde's silver cradles under the floating crystals, ribs over the vault's dome and merlons round the citadel). The plot round them too: pads and flagstone paths, trees with root-flared trunks and leafy crowns (Ironforge's layered pines), crops in leaves and glossy gourds, troughs with water and a bucket, a well with its winch, rope and bucket, spear racks with iron heads, training dummies bound in rope, archery butts with straw rings and arrows, statues in stone or bronze on moulded plinths, iron lantern frames on the lamps and gate lanterns, fence posts with caps, plot walls with coping and corner piers, banners on rods, and the small props: flowers with a heart and a leaf, crates with battens and corner posts, barrels with iron hoops, sacks tied at the neck, carts on spoked wheels with shafts and rails, a blanket in the empty cradles. Celestial plots have their own mythic-class HD version (below).

**Tier 4 "Celestial" (Lv26–30, the Archon of the Realm only).** Mortal buildings stop at Lv25; only the Archon's buildings stand at Lv30 (§9). From Lv26 every plot raises one Celestial structure at its heart, on top of the usual tiers (Lv26 0·2·1·1, Lv27 0·2·2·1, Lv28 0·3·1·1, Lv29–30 0·3·2·1):

| District | Tier 4 |
|---|---|
| 🏠 Houses | **Sky Palace**: marble terraces raised on pillars, crowned with gold domes and a crimson spire |
| 🌾 Food | **Hanging Gardens**: three planted tiers with vines and waterfalls down the front |
| 🐄 Animal | **Griffin Roost**: a crag with a gilded perch and nest of golden eggs; griffins rest on the perches and circle overhead |
| ⚔️ Soldier | **Seraph Bastion**: a white fortress with a crimson-roofed tower, winged gold statues by the gate and red-gold banners |

On top of the ★1–★3 prestige effects, a Celestial plot has a **crimson sigil** of glowing marks on the ground and **slow crimson-gold embers** drifting up, and it is **mythic-class** on the rarity ladder: a tall crimson curtain of light with a prismatic shimmer round the plot, a crimson beam, embers and orbiting shards round its edge (no beam). The rarity effects are drawn instanced (one draw call per effect kind for the whole city), and the griffins are drawn with the city's animals (in Normal its flying bird, gilded and grown; in HD griffins of their own, below), so neither costs extra draw calls.

**Celestial structures in HD** (§10): they are built in the Empyrean's palette (marble in dressed courses, crimson roof tiles, gilt fittings, leaded stained glass in crimson and gold under pointed gold heads, in gilt frames on marble sills), so the Archon's city reads as one place. The **Sky Palace** stands on a grand stair and a colonnade of ten pillars with moulded bases and gilt capitals, a balustrade with gilt finials round its terrace, lancets on every face of the hall round a crimson portal, gilt string courses, ribbed gold domes with lanterns, gilt ribs up its crimson spire and crimson banners with gilt hems hanging between the front pillars. The **Hanging Gardens** get copings and balustrades round each tier, leafy clumps and flowers along the edges, strands of vine swaying down the walls, the falls as moving water pouring over each coping into a basin on the tier below (foam where they land), and a gold pavilion on top: six gilt columns under a ribbed dome with a lantern and a star. The **Griffin Roost**'s crag is banded with strata, grass on its ledges and rocks jutting from it and heaped at its foot; the nest is woven of twigs with sticks poking out and down caught in it, with a third egg in real gold; the perches stand on stones with gilt bands, struts and gilt knobs; and its griffins are real griffins (an eagle's head, breast, wings and taloned forelegs on a lion's body, hind legs and tufted tail, with a gilt beak, collar and primaries). The **Seraph Bastion** gets a moulded plinth course, cross-shaped arrow slits, gilt caps on its merlons, a marble arch round the gate with the Archon's star in it and an iron portcullis with gilt spikes, lancets round the tower under a gilt band, dormers on the tiled roof, seraph statues with fanned gilt wings, a halo and a sword held point down, and its banners in cloth streaming from gilt poles. The **sigil**'s marks are inlaid in marble slabs with a gilt stud at each end; the embers and the mythic curtain stay as they are.

### Land features unlocked by level

| Level | Unlocks |
|---|---|
| 8 | Fence (wooden; palisade for soldier plots) |
| 9 | Path from the gate to the centre |
| 10 | An extra tree |
| 11 | Well / centerpiece |
| 13 | Garden bed |
| 16 | Street lamps |
| 19 | Statue |
| 20 | Stone wall replaces the fence |
| 22 | 2 corner towers |
| 24 | 4 corner towers |

District extras: soldier plots get a training dummy (Lv2) and archery target (Lv6); house plots keep small birds/pets from Lv5; animal plots hold `1 + level/2` animals (max 12).

**Plot size** grows with level: 5 (Lv1–3) → 6 (4–6) → 7.5 (7–9) → 9 (10–14) → 10.5 (15–19) → 12.5 (20–25) → 14 (the Archon's Lv26–30).

### Life, health and decay

* **Townsfolk** — `ceil(commits this week / 2)` people, capped at `2 + level/4`.
* **Commit streak** — the number of days with at least one meaningful commit (not a merge, and at least one meaningful line — see §3), counting back from today. Up to **2 days off in a row** (a weekend, or e.g. Wednesday + Thursday) are forgiven and don't add to the streak; a 3rd empty day in a row ends it. Today never counts against you while it's in progress. Days are calendar days in UTC.
* **Liveliness (commit streak)** — the streak sets a liveliness of `streak / 14` (full at a two-week streak). Townsfolk walk at 0.7× pace with no streak, rising to 1.5× at full liveliness. Each 2 streak days adds a walker on the main roads (main-road walkers: `2 + commits this week / 3 + streak / 2`, max 32), and from a 3-day streak a crowd of `streak − 2` villagers (max 12) strolls around the plaza fountain.
* **Market shoppers** — with the 🛒 Market Square improvement (any tier), `3 + streak / 2` shoppers (max 10) visit the stalls: half browse between two stalls on one side of the plaza, the rest walk from beside the fountain to a stall counter and back.
* **Children at play** (by day; at night they are asleep, §7 *Day, night & seasons*) — `1 + ⌈births last harvest / 5⌉` children, at least 2 (no cap), small villagers in the civilization's clothes. They play on the plaza in groups of up to 4 (`⌈children / 4⌉` groups, split evenly so no child plays alone: 5 children play as 3 + 2), each group at its own random clear spot (in front of the castle while there is room) playing one of three games: **tag** (chasing round a little ring, each child at its own pace, so they keep catching and overtaking each other), **catch** (standing in a circle throwing a ball from child to child, hopping up to catch it) or **running about** at random. Children being children, now and then one trips while running about or playing tag, falls flat on their face, kicks their legs for a moment and gets back up (the others run on and lap them), and in catch one sometimes fumbles the ball and topples over backwards while it flies on round the circle. When the plaza is full, groups play round the fountain. A **Special Overseer's** (and the **Archon's**) plaza always has 12 children in 3 groups, one playing each game.
* **Children walking with grown-ups** — every villager or farmer walking back and forth (roads, plaza, market, plots) has a **20%** chance of a child walking beside them. None under a **Low birth rate** punishment. Like other townsfolk they disappear when you zoom far out. A kingdom punished with **Low birth rate** (§15) has none.
* **Landmark visitors** — no extra people: with the 🐉 Dragon Statue, 🔮 Mana Spire or an upgraded ⛲ Plaza Fountain, two existing townsfolk per landmark (taken from the plaza crowd, then shoppers, then road walkers) walk up to it, pause with good manners — bowing to the dragon and the Hero Monument, leaning back to look up at the spire, looking around to admire the Grand Fountain — then walk back, rest and return.
* **Health** — `100 − days since last commit`, floored at 40. A repo with no indexed commits counts its days from the repo's last Git update instead (no date at all → treated as dormant, health 40).
* **Flowers** bloom when health ≥ 80 (fewer at 60–79). **Weeds** appear below 60.
* **Active bonus** (a commit within the last 90 days) — the plot flies a banner in the civilization's accent colour by its gate, flanked by two glowing gate lanterns (these replace the front pair of path lamps).
* **Dormant** (no commit for 90+ days — or, for a repo with no indexed commits, no Git update for 90+ days) — the plot keeps its full colours and level design; it just loses the banner and lanterns. One new commit raises them again.
* **Props** (crates, barrels, sacks, carts) scale with lines of code, up to 8.
* **Royal Decrees** (§15) show on the city too: round a 🛡️ shielded city a translucent curtain of energy rises from just outside the outer walls, tracing their outline (square walls, a square aura) with flat faceted sides, a glowing seam along the ground, faint hexagons rippling upwards and flickering, and a soft fade towards the top (in HD it is richer: scan lines climb it, cells flare, a pulse runs round the seam, it turns violet up high and motes of light drift up it); under a ⛈️ punishment the sky darkens, heavy slanted rain splashes over the streets, ragged storm clouds hang over the countryside round the city and forked lightning strikes and flashes the sky. **Low birth rate** wilts every plot's flowers grey and puts empty cradles by the houses; **High death rate** adds fresh graves outside the walls, crows circling over the plaza and a grey-green miasma drifting through the streets.

---

## 6. The Castle (Town Hall)

Castle level = `1 + √(sum of all building levels)`, capped at **Lv25**. The square root rewards a well-rounded city over one giant repo. Only the Archon of the Realm's castle goes past it, to a fixed **Lv30** (§9).

A building past Lv25 keeps counting: its prestige XP adds to the sum at the Lv25 rate, `25 × XP ÷ 466` (a Lv25 repo with 932 XP counts as 50, one with 3,866 XP (18★) as 207). Work poured into a few deep repos weighs the same as the same XP spread over many; below Lv25 a building counts its level.

*Examples: 1 hut → Lv2 · ten Lv5 buildings → Lv8 · twenty Lv20 buildings → Lv21 · two repos at 3,866 XP → Lv21 · three → Lv25.*

The castle is rebuilt bigger every 5 levels; each level inside a stage adds a little height:

| Castle Lv | Stage | Look |
|---|---|---|
| 1 – 5 | **Keep** (common) | Small hall, one tower, wooden fence |
| 6 – 10 | **Castle** (uncommon) | Bigger hall, two towers, twin banners |
| 11 – 15 | **Citadel** (rare) | Curtain walls, four corner towers, central keep spire; **glints** twinkle round it |
| 16 – 20 | **Palace** (epic) | Second hall tier, gatehouse towers, lit windows, flags on every tower; a **low curtain of light** round it and orbiting motes |
| 21 – 25 | **Wonder** | Gold-banded towers, golden crown, orbiting crystals; **legendary-class** effects (below); in HD a grander castle of its own (*HD Wonders* below) |
| 26 – 30 | **Empyrean** (the Archon only) | A floating citadel over a broken rock base |

**Every stage climbs the rarity ladder**, the same in both views. On the world map each Citadel gains one looping detail and each Palace something circling it: Valoria lights its keep windows and turns a gold vane, then raises a tall spire with doves circling it (the Wonder's gold crest sits on top of that spire); Sylvandale's moonstone circles its spire tip, then a wisp orbits the canopy; Ironforge turns a brass gear on its bastion, then an ember orbits the forge; Drak'mar turns a skull totem on its roof, then a crow circles the war hall; Nethergarde circles a crystal round its light column, then a shard orbits its hovering citadel.

**Wonder is top class.** A Wonder is the top mortal castle and gets the **legendary effects** of the rarity ladder (§10 *Rarity*) in both views, as grand as a legendary landmark: a tall golden curtain of rising light round the castle, a beam of light, rising embers and orbiting motes.

**Empyrean** — the Archon of the Realm's castle. A marble citadel ringed in gold floats over a broken rock base with jagged stones and a crimson sigil burnt into it (eight rays round a molten vent). Crimson-gold spires fly red banners with gold hems, a halo turns slowly round the great crimson spire and three shards (two crimson, one gold) orbit the citadel. It is **mythic-class** in both views, one step above a Wonder: a crimson curtain of light with a prismatic shimmer round it, a crimson beam, gold embers and orbiting shards. On the world map it is its own instanced model (one draw per part), standing at the heart of the Voidrift under the Eye (§9); in the city it is merged like the other castles, the floating citadel and the shards within their own moving groups.

**HD Empyrean**: with HD graphics (§10) the Empyrean is one hand-built model everywhere it stands (the world map, the Archon's city as its town hall, and the Codex): a floating holy city held down to its rock by **four gold chains** made fast to marble obelisks. On its lower terrace a crenellated **curtain wall** with turrets rings the city, broken by a **gatehouse** (crimson doors in a gilt pointed arch, the Archon's star over it, braziers burning either side) and by **four great towers of different heights** (balconies on corbels, lancet windows, corbelled parapets, tiled roofs with dormers and gilt finials, each flying a banner over the wall); **seraph statues** with raised wings, halos and swords stand by the gate and at the back, behind a balustrade round the edge, and **water pours off the terrace** in three falls that thin into mist before they reach the ground. On the upper terrace stands a **cathedral**: a nave with a great door and a rose window, transepts with rose windows, a choir ending in an apse, flying buttresses with pinnacles, **stained-glass lancets** (crimson, gold, azure and violet, glowing as if lit by a fire inside) and a **crossing tower** that climbs through a columned gallery and a lantern to a gold-ribbed needle under the gold star. Marble is laid in dressed courses (flagstones on the terraces), roofs in rows of crimson tiles, the trim in mirror gold; under the city the spur breaks into crags and crimson crystals with rubble drifting below. The halo, the three shards, the fluttering banners and the mythic beam, curtain and embers finish it.

**World Map Level Progression**: On the world map, every kingdom's castle renders dynamically according to its castle stage (Keep, Castle, Citadel, Palace, Wonder). Lower-level keeps appear as rustic halls with two front watchtowers or early cottages. As kingdoms level up, they gain corner towers, curtain walls, high spires, bell towers, and forge chimneys, culminating in stage-4 wonders featuring civilization signatures (mammoth tusks, molten sluices & anvils, living tree spires, floating mana crystals, and orbiting shards). This visual progression is achieved with zero extra draw calls through instanced matrix zero-scaling.

**HD castles**: with HD graphics (§10) every map castle from Keep to Palace is drawn in its HD version, climbing the same ladder. Walls are dressed stone in courses, roofs laid in rows of tiles, and each stage adds its tier's detail: the **Keep** stays plain (Valoria's hipped slate roof inside a parapet, a door up a flight of steps and a timber cottage with its chimney smoking; Sylvandale's roots and bark under a leafy canopy; Ironforge's buttresses, stone gate and ore cart on its rails; Drak'mar's hide-roofed war hall with horns, a log gate under a skull and sharpened stakes; Nethergarde's obsidian footing ringed with shards of black glass); the **Castle** adds the civilization's colour in cloth (banners and flags rippling in the wind, war banners, hanging pennants) and small props; the **Citadel** real metal (gilt finials and bands, iron or brass fittings, silver filigree) and lit windows; the **Palace** the richest detail and glowing parts (Valoria's lit spire with a streaming pennant and doves with wings, Sylvandale's runes and drifting petals, Ironforge's carved guardian with burning eyes over runes on its battlements, Drak'mar's spiked battlements and war runes, Nethergarde's silver-ringed citadel with rock hanging under it). The **city town hall** gets the same treatment in the city view: dressed-stone courses on its walls and round towers, tiled roofs, a wooden door, banners in cloth, and its own detail by stage: the **Keep** dull iron bands on the door, arch stones round it, steps up to it, sills and arched heads on the windows and rails along the fence; the **Castle** the civilization's banners hanging by the door; the **Citadel** iron bands and studs on the door, a portcullis drawn up in the curtain gate and lit lanterns on the wall; the **Palace** gilt fittings, a gold rim round the crest, a glowing rose window and lanterns on the upper hall.

**HD Wonders**: with HD graphics the Wonder is legendary-class in its model, not only in its effects: two steps above the Palace, with a bigger silhouette, the civilization's richest materials and parts that move, before the legendary curtain and beam finish it. On the world map: **Valoria** raises its four corner towers a storey (lit drums under gold bands, tall tiled spires with gilt finials and pennants), links them with a crenellated curtain, wears a gilt moulding and gilt cresting on the keep, royal banners with a gold sun down its sides, gold ribs and crockets and a glowing rose window on the great spire, a **gilt crown turning** round the spire's tip with sparks rising, and lamp standards burning by the steps; **Sylvandale**'s tree blooms (a third crown of leaves with **blossom glowing** in it, lantern-fruit hanging on silver threads), its silver spires rise a storey under crescent moons with silver bridges to the trunk, the mana crystal floats over the crown in a silver cradle and a **ring of leaves and motes turns** round the tree in place of the Palace's flat ring of light; **Ironforge** stands four square bastions at its corners with braziers burning, raises a great **forge stack** with glowing vents, smoke and embers from its copper roof, pours **lava falls** from the battlements either side of the gate, lights gold runes on the battlements' sides and sets its masterwork anvil (glowing metal, a hammer leaning on it, sparks flying) on a plinth by the gate; **Drak'mar** mounts a **mammoth skull** over the gate between its iron-banded tusks, raises totems of stacked skulls and tall tattered war banners, rings its foundation with bone and iron spikes and burns a **bonfire** in an iron cauldron over its roof; **Nethergarde** rings itself with tall obsidian monoliths with violet runes pulsing on them, raises a crystal spire to a void orb from its hovering citadel and turns **two great silver rings** round it, one tilted, like a gyroscope. In the **city** the town hall's Wonder takes moulded gilt rings in place of its flat gold bands, lancets lit round every tower, gilt (silver for Sylvandale and Nethergarde) ribs and crockets up its roofs, statues of the civilization's champion either side of the gate (gilt, silver for Sylvandale and Nethergarde, bone for Drak'mar), flying buttresses and gilt pinnacles on the upper hall, a jewelled crown (a velvet cap, gems, pearls and Valoria's orb and cross), gilt rings round its orbiting crystals, and its signature rebuilt: Sylvandale's tree with roots, leafy clumps, blossom, lantern-fruit and a silver cradle round its crystal; Ironforge's hammer with gilt bands, runes burning on its faces and a wrapped grip, and braziers on the roof; Drak'mar's tusks banded in iron, a mammoth skull on the roof and braziers; Nethergarde's monolith with a tilted silver ring turning with it. The Empyrean hall in the city is the Empyrean's hand-built model.

**Castle prestige** — past raw level 25, one tier per 3 extra levels (max 4), effects only:

| Prestige | Effect |
|---|---|
| ★ | Gold aura ring on the ground (HD: a ring of gilt light-standards with a low curtain of light round the castle) |
| ★★ | Light beacon into the sky (HD: brightest down its middle, fading as it climbs, with bands of light rising) |
| ★★★ | Golden halo above the crown (HD: a gilt halo with rays and jewels, a second ring of light turning the other way) |
| ★★★★ | Ring of orbiting sparkles (aura glows brighter) (HD: sparkles rising round the castle and twinkling) |

## 7. Resources & the Harvest

Server: `executeResourceAllocation` in [db.ts](server/src/db.ts), [syncService.ts](server/src/syncService.ts)

Every kingdom starts at **0**. Every 15 minutes (96 harvests/day) a fixed pool is shared across all kingdoms:

| Resource | Pool per harvest | Guaranteed minimum per kingdom |
|---|---|---|
| 🪙 Gold | 1,000 | 1 |
| ⚙️ Iron | 25,000 | 50 |
| 🪨 Stone | 50,000 | 100 |
| 🪵 Lumber | 100,000 | 200 |
| 🌾 Food | 250,000 | 500 |
| 💎 One random scarce drop | see below | 1 |

Iron/stone/lumber/food minimums shrink automatically if the realm is so large that they'd use up more than half the pool. A kingdom under a **⛈️ Royal Punishment** (§15) gets nothing from the harvest at all (no pool share, no gem drop, no building harvest, no workforce yield, no title bonus) and is left out of the split, so the others share its part; its people still live (births and deaths, scaled by the punishment) and its garrison still eats. Special Overseers (`SPECIAL_USERS`) are excluded from recurring harvest distributions (100% of the harvest pool goes to active contributors); instead their treasury, people and garrison reset to their preset every harvest (§13).

**How the rest is split:**

1. Every kingdom receives its minimum.
2. The remaining pool is shared by **weight = 10 × √activity score + 1** (rounded). The activity score is **2 × past-week XP** (commits with diminishing returns, PRs, reviews and sealed 🪶 deed reports (§17) across *all* your repos — built or not), raised by your **🔥 play streak** (below). Deed XP counts by the day it was written for. The square root keeps the order but softens the gap: **4× the activity earns 2× the share**, so one very busy week can't take the pool. The +1 means quiet kingdoms still get a share.
3. **10% cap** — no kingdom takes more than **10%** of a pool (minimum + share, for each resource and the gem drop). What a capped kingdom would have had past it goes to the others by their weights. The ledger line says when a harvest was capped (`Weight: 170, capped at 10% of the pool`). In a realm of fewer than 10 kingdoms the cap can't hold and is lifted.
4. Leftover units from rounding are handed out round-robin; the starting kingdom rotates every harvest so nobody is always first.

**On top of the pool:**

* **Building harvest** — every building yields per building level each harvest: 0.2 gold, 2 iron, 4 stone, 4 lumber, 10 food (summed over the kingdom, rounded down).
* **🔥 Active Surge** — a repo with a commit or a merged PR in the last **48 hours** surges: its building harvests **double** (+100%), the kingdom's share of the scarce gem drop is weighted **2×**, and the plot burns with a flame aura and rising embers in the city view.
* **Civilian workforce** — civilians add their civ's yield per 100 (§13); elves gather mana and gems. A 🎉 Festival adds +10% to it for its day (§13 *Royal Proclamations*).
* **Gathering** — on top of the harvest, armies can work the resource nodes (the day's and each roving window's fresh wave) and claim roving treasure (§13 Gathering).
* **🎺 Game Activity title** — +10% / +20% / +30% of this harvest's base resources (pool share + building harvest), paid on top of the pool. It is **capped**: the bonus is worked out on at most **2× the realm's average harvest** per ruler, so a very high activity score can't turn it into a flood. It shows in the ledger as its own line (`title_harvest_bonus`).

**🔥 Play streak.** A **play day** is a UTC day on which you made at least **5 game actions** while signed in (marches, scouts, rallies, recalls, sending troops home, resource caravans, swaps, civilization changes, mobilizing or summoning, forging Armory sets, improvements, Royal Proclamations, moving buildings). Admin tools, royal decrees and sign-in don't count, a mobilize that leaves your garrison as it was doesn't either, and neither does anything that happens without you: harvests, admin resets and refunds, royal gifts, or battles resolving on their own. Only what you send counts (for war: marches, scouts, rallies and joins, recalls, sending reinforcements home). Consecutive play days form your play streak (today counts once you reach 5; a day still in progress never breaks it). Each streak day adds **+2% to your activity score**, up to **+20%** at a 10-day streak. The developer profile shows it next to the commit streak (🎮 N-day play streak) and on the Activity Score card (🎮 +X% play streak, +0% when there is no streak).

### Scarce resources (gems)

Each harvest picks **one** at random (on a day harvest the sun gems, on a night harvest the moon gems are 3× as likely; see *Day, night & seasons* below). Its pool (or the number of kingdoms, if larger; ×1.25 at night) is shared the same way — 1 guaranteed, the rest by weight (×2 for a kingdom in Active Surge), capped at 10% of the drop:

| Gem | Pool | Rarity |
|---|---|---|
| 🔮 Arcane Mana | 2,500 | rare |
| 🌌 Volcanic Obsidian | 500 | rare |
| 🪙 Dwarven Mithril | 300 | rare |
| ⚡ Aether Shard | 250 | epic |
| 📜 Titan Rune | 150 | rare |
| 💎 Flawless Diamond | 100 | epic |
| 🔴 Blood Ruby | 100 | epic |
| 🟢 Sylvan Emerald | 100 | epic |
| 🔷 Tide Sapphire | 100 | epic |
| 📜 Titan Tablet | 80 | legendary |
| 🐉 Molten Dragon Scale | 60 | legendary |
| 🪶 Phoenix Feather | 50 | legendary |

### Day, night & seasons

Server: [realmTime.ts](server/src/realmTime.ts) · Client: [realmTime.ts](src/utils/realmTime.ts), [RealmSky.tsx](src/components/3d/RealmSky.tsx), [SeasonWeather.tsx](src/components/3d/SeasonWeather.tsx)

**Day and night take turns with the harvest.** Every harvest window (from one harvest to the next, `SYNC_INTERVAL_MINUTES`, 15 minutes by default) is either ☀️ **day** or 🌙 **night**, alternately, so a realm day is two harvests: 15 minutes of day, then 15 minutes of night. Which one is running follows the number of harvests so far (even = day, odd = night), so it never drifts. The harvest that ends a window pays it:

| Harvest | Base harvest (pool share, buildings, workforce) | Gem drop |
|---|---|---|
| ☀️ Day | full | ☀️ sun gems 3× as likely: 🔴 ruby, 🌌 obsidian, 🐉 dragon scale |
| 🌙 Night | **×0.85** | 🌙 moon gems 3× as likely: 🔮 mana, ⚡ aether, 🔷 sapphire; the drop's pool **×1.25** |

**The realm clock and the sun.** A day window runs the realm clock from 05:00 to 19:00 and a night window from 19:00 to 05:00, in every season. The sky goes through eight parts of the day: 🌄 dawn, 🌅 early morning, ☀️ day, 🌇 late afternoon, 🌆 dusk, 🌗 nightfall, 🌙 deep night and 🌠 the small hours. Sunrise and sunset **move with the season**, but the harvest windows don't:

| Season | Sunrise | Sunset | Daylight | What it looks like |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 🌸 Spring | 05:00 | 19:00 | 14 h | sunrise and sunset fall on the harvests |
| ☀️ Summer | 03:30 | 20:30 | 17 h | the light lingers into the night window: it opens in late afternoon and dusk, and dawn comes before it ends |
| 🍂 Autumn | 05:00 | 19:00 | 14 h | as spring |
| ❄️ Winter | 07:00 | 17:00 | 10 h | the dark reaches into the day window: it opens in the small hours and ends at nightfall |

The parts of the day stretch to fit (dawn is the first 1/14 of the daylight, nightfall the first 2/10 of the dark, and so on). They are only the look of the window: the whole window still pays as day or as night. The day & season panel shows the whole realm day on one bar, with the season's parts of the day over the two harvest windows and where the clock is now.

**Life after dark.** From late dusk (the last half of dusk, so earlier in winter and later in summer) to the first of dawn the realm goes to bed:

* **In the city** the children are asleep (no games on the plaza, no children walking beside their parents) and most townsfolk are indoors: only about **1 in 5** of the day's walkers and workers is still out on the roads and at the plots, walking a little slower. Grazing and pecking animals settle down.
* **The night watch** walks the town: two soldiers up and down each of the four main roads (one on each side) and two along the front of the plaza.
* **Everyone out after dark carries a light**, in their civilization's style, with a soft flickering glow round it: 👑 humans a brass hand lantern, 🏹 elves a floating moon-wisp over the shoulder, ⚒️ dwarves a miner's lamp on the brow and an iron lantern, 🛡️ orcs a pitch torch, 🔮 Nethergarde a hovering void orb.
* **On the world map** every army and gathering party on the road, in battle or at work carries the same lights (each figure its own civilization's), so marches show as moving trails of lanterns and torches.
* **The frontier gates** keep their lights burning: each gate has braziers and wall torches (Valoria), war fires and pitch torches on stakes (Drak'mar), moonstone lanterns and lamp posts (Sylvandale) or rune panels, braziers and iron lanterns on the rock face (Ironforge), and after dark every one of them glows. A burning gate has only its fire.
* **The Voidrift's orbiting obsidian shards** smoulder violet from within at night, each wearing a violet halo with a brighter one round its crystal, so the ring reads against the dark sky (faintly by day).

**Seasons.** One season a week, turning with the other weekly resets on **Sunday 00:00 UTC**, in a four-week cycle. Each season **buffs one civilization and debuffs another**; over the four weeks every mortal civ is buffed once and debuffed once. Nethergarde stands outside the seasons.

| Season | ▲ Buffed | ▼ Debuffed | Story |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🌸 Spring (Bloom) | 🏹 Elf | 🛡️ Orc | the glades wake under the spring rains; the Badlands flood |
| ☀️ Summer (Blaze) | 🛡️ Orc | ⚒️ Dwarf | war season; the forges and halls swelter |
| 🍂 Autumn (Harvest) | 👑 Human | 🏹 Elf | the granaries fill; the glades fall asleep |
| ❄️ Winter (Frost) | ⚒️ Dwarf | 👑 Human | the mountain halls are warm; the fields lie bare |

The buffed civ gets **+10% harvest** (pool share, buildings and workforce), **+10% births** and **+5% march speed**; the debuffed civ the same in reverse. Seasons never touch combat stats. The ledger line of a harvest shows them (`🌙 Night ×0.85`, `[Season ▲ ×1.1]`), and the season change is a herald notice (§14).

**What you see.** In both views the sky, fog and light follow the window: a rose dawn, a low golden early-morning sun, full day, amber late afternoon with longer shadows, an orange-to-violet dusk, and a blue moonlit night. The sun crosses from east to west between the season's sunrise and sunset, and the moon through the dark; windows, lamps and lanterns light up after dusk. The light moves in half-hour steps of the realm day, about every 30 seconds (shadows are redrawn only then). Grass and trees take the season's colours (lush spring green, sun-yellowed summer, gold and red autumn leaves, snow-covered winter), and the weather follows it:

* 🌸 **Spring** — rain showers come and go (about half of each half hour, fading in and out): an ordinary light rain under a few passing grey-white clouds, the light only a little dimmer. Not a storm: no lightning, nothing like a ⛈️ punishment.
* ☀️ **Summer** — clear skies with a few white clouds; fireflies over the fields after dark.
* 🍂 **Autumn** — leaves drift down on the wind.
* ❄️ **Winter** — snow falls, in gusts.

A Voidrift city has no seasons: no tint and no weather. The **day & season button** in the top bar is also the **harvest timer**: it shows ☀️ / 🌙, the countdown to the next harvest (when day turns to night or back) with a thin line filling as the window runs, and on wider screens *Day* / *Night*, framed in the season's colour with the season's emblem on its corner (▲ / ▼ when the season buffs or debuffs your civilization). Tap it for a panel with the window's progress, what a day and a night harvest pay, the season's story and weather, who flourishes, who struggles and who is unchanged (your civilization marked), and the four seasons in turn.

**World disasters** (§10 *World Disasters*) can change a harvest for 4 hours: the 🌾 Great Blight stops the pool's payouts, the ☠️ Wasting Plague kills 1% of the civilians at each harvest, and the 🥀 Barren Spell halves births.

### Ledger

Every harvest and purchase writes a ledger entry with deltas and running balances. View your kingdom's history or your whole civilization's stream in the **Ledger** modal (admins and Special Overseers see the whole realm's; other civilizations' ledgers are private under the fog of war).

---

## 8. City Improvements (spending resources)

Server: [upgrades.ts](server/src/upgrades.ts) · city visuals: [blueprints.ts](src/components/3d/city/blueprints.ts)

Improvements are bought by the kingdom owner (signed in). Every improvement has **5 tiers**, bought in order; each tier is visible in the city and adds a **permanent kingdom bonus**. Tiers 1–2 are cheap; from tier 3 the price climbs steeply (4–5× per tier). A median developer earns roughly 6k gold, 45k iron, 85k stone, 130k lumber and 340k food a day, so saving for one improvement takes about **2–3 hours at tier 1, 7–9 hours at tier 2, 1.5 days at tier 3, 6 days at tier 4 and a month at tier 5** (City Walls about 1.2× that); a very active developer gets there four to eight times faster, a quiet one two to three times slower. Gold matters from tier 3 on, with stone, iron, lumber or food (whichever the improvement uses most).

**Gems follow the rarity ladder.** Tier 3 asks for a rare gem, tier 4 for an epic gem plus half as much of the rare one, and tier 5 for a legendary gem plus half as much of the epic one; amounts are sized so a median developer's gem drops (§7) cover them in about 60% of the tier's saving time.

| Improvement | Rare (tier 3) | Epic (tier 4) | Legendary (tier 5) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🧱 City Walls | mithril | diamond | dragon scale |
| 🛣️ Roads | titan rune | aether | titan tablet |
| 🏮 Lantern Streets | mana | sapphire | phoenix feather |
| 🌷 Royal Gardens | mana | emerald | phoenix feather |
| 🛒 Market Square | obsidian | diamond | titan tablet |
| 🚩 Kingdom Banners | titan rune | ruby | dragon scale |
| ⛲ Plaza Fountain | mithril | diamond | phoenix feather |
| 🐉 Dragon Statue | obsidian | ruby | dragon scale |
| 🔮 Mana Spire | mana (5 already at tier 2) | aether | titan tablet |
| 🦉 Spymaster's Tower | titan rune | sapphire | titan tablet |

**Every improvement climbs the rarity ladder** (§10 *Rarity*): tier 1 common, 2 uncommon, 3 rare, 4 epic, **5 legendary**. Tier 1 is plain (Sylvandale's hedge lanterns and Nethergarde's ward pylons stay dark, garden flowers and market goods unlit, banners plain); from tier 2 the walls fly the civilization's flag on every corner tower; tier 3 brings one looping effect (the flags and banners stream, the fountain throws up spray, mana flames pulse, garden trees sway, traders' pennants stream over the stalls, magical goods and flowers glow); tier 4 brings things circling (glowing water orbs over the fountain, butterflies round the conservatories) and glowing details (the gilded strongboxes gleam, the canals glow). Improvements that stand as one structure (the city gate, the Plaza Fountain, the Dragon Statue, the Mana Spire, the Spymaster's Tower) also wear the ladder's effects: glints at tier 3, a faint curtain of light and orbiting motes at tier 4, and the full **legendary** treatment at tier 5 (a tall golden curtain of light round them, a beam, embers and motes; the fountain's gold orbs and the spire's starlight orbs circle). The ones spread over the whole city (Roads, Lantern Streets, Gardens, Market Square, Banners) show their tiers through their own models only. The Royal Codex marks every tier with its rarity.

**HD improvements**: with HD graphics (§10) every improvement is drawn in its HD version (the districts too: §5 *HD structures*): walls, towers, gate pillars, plinths and the fountain in dressed stone courses, cobbled and paved roads and the plaza in flagstones, wood, cloth, leaves, gems and metal where they are made of them, glossy water. Tiers add detail on the same ladder: arrow slits through stone walls and in the towers, cornices and finials on the towers (dull iron, then iron from tier 3, gold from tier 4), rope lashing and skulls on the palisades, lantern frames round the hedge lanterns and street lamps, splayed legs and coals on the braziers, a portcullis drawn up in the city gate from Stone Walls (iron from tier 3), moulded bases and capitals on the road arches, rolled lips and four spouts pouring into the fountain's basin (gilt from tier 3) with round iron-rimmed shields round it, gilt ribs on the conservatory domes and stone kerbs along the canals, battens on the market crates, the scale's pans and a gold lock on the strongboxes, a dragon with legs gripping its plinth (gilt claws from tier 3), an open jaw with teeth and fire, two eyes and a spade on its tail, moss, stones and pale crystals round the standing stone, a ladder and braces on the spymaster's lookout, an arch, banded door, sills, a finial and a brass rim round the lens on its towers.

**Tier 5 in HD** is the legendary landmark of each improvement, gilded and alive, before its curtain of light:
* **Citadel Ramparts**: obsidian courses framed in steel mouldings, glowing runes pulsing along the mithril vein, steel caps on every merlon, machicolation corbels and a steel band on every tower, tiled roofs, a pennant streaming from each wall tower, beacons as iron cauldrons on legs with a gilt rim, tongues of flame and sparks, the city's sigil over the gate in a steel frame under a gilt finial, and a steel portcullis with gilt spikes.
* **Imperial Highway**: triumphal arches with fluted pillars, gilt bands and astragals, a cornice with gilt cresting, gilt suns (rays turning round the lit balls) and two banners hanging under each span; the glowing runes set in silver frames.
* **Celestial Nightlights**: gilt collars up every post, each starlight orb in a gilt cradle with three gilt stars circling it and a glint rising off it; the crossroads braziers as gilt star-bowls.
* **Paradise of the Realm**: the floating blossoms open into petals turning round their glowing hearts, a gilt trellis arch with flowering vines over every bed, a gilt lantern on each conservatory and glowing blossoms circling it.
* **Sovereign Emporium**: every trade lantern in a gilt frame, a painted sign with a gilt coin on a gilt arm, coins heaped on the strongboxes, bunting on a gilt cord between the poles.
* **Legendary War Standards**: every standard on a stone foot with a gilt band, a gilt crown round its finial, the sigil in a gilt frame with two motes circling it, and what is sewn onto the cloth (hem, crest, runes, tatters) moving with it, a gilt hem along its foot; the two grand standards on gilt-banded plinths.
* **Sovereign Spring**: gilt lion masks on pedestals round the basin pouring arcs of water into it, gilt rims, a gilt crown round the top basin spilling four streams, gilt fluting on the column, rays turning round the gold suns.
* **Mythic Sovereign**: a gilt crown on the dragon's head, gilt bones through its wings, a gilt crest down its spine (where its civilization has none), gilt runes pulsing on the plinth, a hoard round its foot (a heap of gold, spilled coins, gems, goblets and a chest) and the braziers on iron legs with flames and sparks.
* **Astral Apex Spire**: a gilt rim round the pedestal, gilt bands up the civilization's spire, each orbiting orb in a gilt collar, a ring of crystals turning round the spire, motes rising.
* **Eye of the Night Court**: a gilt almond lid round the eye and a slit pupil that narrows and widens, gilt ribs up the cap, gilt bands up the tower, lit slits beside the lens, the Night Court's dark violet banners with gilt hems hanging from the walkway and a gilt crescent moon circling the star.

The army stat bonuses (Dragon Statue, Mana Spire, Plaza Fountain) add to your milestone title buffs and apply to your attacking armies, your home garrison, your reinforcements in allies' castles and your gatherers defending a node. The battle log lists them with your titles (`🎖️ Ser Aldric's troops fight with +3% ATK (🐉 Dragon Statue III)`).

**Tier 0 — the pastoral village.** A kingdom with no improvements still looks finished: packed-earth roads framed by gravel shoulders with stepping cobbles where they meet the plaza, a stone-lipped village well bubbling in the plaza, an ancient moss-covered runic plinth on the Dragon Statue's spot, a wooden bench between flowering shrubs on the Mana Spire's spot, clover and country lawn along the cross road, iron lamp posts every 7 units and the split-rail fence with its gateposts. Buying the Dragon Statue raises the dragon onto the plinth; the Mana Spire replaces the bench.

The Improvements window has a second tab, **📜 Royal Proclamations**: one-off orders with a cooldown (Paid Settlers, Festival), described in §13.

Tiers bought before improvements had 5 tiers keep their tier number (an old tier-1 Dragon Statue is now a Wyrmling Effigy). Administrators can reset a kingdom's improvements with a full refund (§11).

Every city starts ringed by a low wooden split-rail fence with the gate set into it (Wall DEF 1.25×, Wall HP 1.0×); buying City Walls replaces the fence. Walls ring the whole city and get a taller gatehouse, corner bastions, machicolations, and gilt rampart banners at higher tiers; roads change the colour of every street and the plaza.

Kingdom Banners are styled per civilization (in the castle's banner colour): heraldic blue banners with gold crests for humans, teal pennants with glowing orbs for elves, rune-marked banners on iron-banded stone pillars for dwarves, tattered red banners on skull-topped spears for orcs, and glowing runic banners under floating crystals for the arcane. They line all four main roads, two grand standards flank the plaza entrance, and hangings drape the city gate.

---

## 9. Civilizations

Developers are distributed across five civilizations using a balanced quota system:

1. **Special Overseers (`SPECIAL_USERS`)**: Managers, trackers, and leads who do not commit code are permanently assigned to **Nethergarde Voidrift** (`arcane`). They rule as legendary overseers with **maxed kingdoms**:
   - **Castle**: Level 25 (**Wonder**), Prestige 4 (golden crown, orbiting crystals, drifting motes).
   - **City Improvements**: All 10 improvements at tier 5 (Citadel Ramparts, Imperial Highway, Celestial Nightlights, Paradise of the Realm, Sovereign Emporium, Legendary War Standards, Sovereign Spring, Mythic Sovereign, Astral Apex Spire, Eye of the Night Court), with all their bonuses (§8).
   - **Buildings**: All buildings at Level 25, Prestige 4, Health 100, active condition, 8 workers per plot, 12 animals per pasture. If the overseer owns fewer than 20 repositories, 20 honorary executive facilities across all four districts (35/25/25/15 ratio: 7 residential/commercial, 5 animal pastures, 5 food fields, 3 soldier keeps) give the appearance of managing 20 active repositories:
     - *Residential & commercial:* Void Archive, Aether Spire, Chrono Matrix, Astral Observatory, Nether Council Chamber, Eclipse Sanctum, Voidrift Citadel Tower.
     - *Animal pastures:* Astral Stables, Nether Beast Pen, Void Drake Aviary, Shadow Wyrm Menagerie, Phase Chimera Paddock.
     - *Food fields:* Starlight Hydroponics, Crystalline Lotus Beds, Void Mana Orchards, Luminescent Fungus Caverns, Arcane Essence Silos.
     - *Soldier keeps:* Rift Sentinel Bastion, Netherblade Armory, Void Vanguard Fortress.
   - **Liveliness & Crowd**: 365 streak days (maximum 1.5× walking speed), 32 main-road walkers, 12 fountain crowd, 10 market shoppers, and landmark visitors.
   - **Treasury**: Abundant resources (50k gold, 200k iron, 200k stone, 200k lumber, 500k food) and scarce gems (50 mana, 25 aether, 25 diamond, 25 mithril, 10 dragon scale, 10 titan tablet, 10 phoenix feather). It is a **real treasury**: raids plunder it, and every harvest resets it to these amounts (see *Special Overseers* in §13). Special overseers are excluded from recurring harvest distributions so that 100% of the recurring harvest pool is allocated to active contributors.
   - Special overseers cannot change their civilization.

   **👑 The Archon of the Realm (`ARCHON_USERS`)** — the department head, an overseer above the overseers (title **Archon Eternal**; badge the **Archon's Seal**, the overseer's starburst in crimson and gold). Archons are listed in `ARCHON_USERS` only, not also in `SPECIAL_USERS`.
   - **Everything an overseer has**: Nethergarde for good, maxed improvements, the preset treasury reset every harvest, no share of the harvest pool, a fixed title instead of milestone titles, the Royal Decrees powers (§15), 12 children at play, never fogged, never marches.
   - **Untouchable**: can't be attacked, rallied on or scouted (§13), no Royal Shield or Punishment can be laid on them (§15). Not in War Frenzy or titles.
   - **Castle Lv30**, the **Empyrean** (§6), and **50 buildings, all Lv30** with the Tier 4 Celestial structures (§5). When the Archon owns fewer than 50 repos, honorary halls fill the city (35/25/25/15: 18 houses, 12 pastures, 12 fields, 8 garrisons), e.g. *Sky Palace of Dawn*, *Hall of the Crimson Seal*, *Griffin Roost of the Dawn*, *Hanging Gardens of Ascension*, *Seraph Bastion*.
   - **A fixed seat at the heart of the Voidrift**: the Archon's citadel stands at the centre of the crater, where the Nethergarde Core stood. The Core (the Eye) has **risen off the ground and floats above the citadel** like a lodestone, a beam of light running from it down into the citadel's crown, its obsidian monoliths and shards wheeling round both. It keeps its legendary ring, lore and Codex entry; its ring now circles the citadel's base, where robed Nethergarde pilgrims come to kneel. Hovering anywhere round the floating Eye shows its card (it doesn't grow on hover like the world bosses and legendary sites, so it stays under the cursor); the castle below keeps its own hover. No other castle may stand on that seat (anyone there moves to a free plot), and it is not a Voidrift slot. Without an Archon the Core sits on the ground as before.
   - The Archon's Seal shows in the kingdom picker, on the profile button frame (crimson ring with a flickering molten-gold rim), in the developer profile, on the castle badge on the map and in the castle panel.

2. **Civilization Allocation & Open Places**:
   - Given Total Developers $N$ and Special Users $S$:
     $$\text{Non-Special Pool} = N - S$$
     $$B = \lfloor (N - S) / 4 \rfloor$$
   - Each of the **4 mortal civilizations** (Human, Elf, Dwarf, Orc) has a baseline of $B$ places ($4 \times B$ total).
   - **Nethergarde Voidrift** (`arcane`) receives all $S$ special users plus `NETHERGARDE_PLAYER_SLOTS` (default 3) conquerable Voidrift thrones.
   - **Reset world** (admin): the $4 \times B$ mortal places are filled at random, and the leftover $N - S - 4B$ developers (0–3) go to Nethergarde. Administrators (`ADMIN_USERS`, in their listed order) take those leftover places first, then random developers; with no leftover, administrators are placed at random like everyone else. Ordinary syncs never move anyone, and new developers always join a mortal civilization.
   - Non-special developers in Nethergarde Voidrift progress normally via their own commits and pull requests.
   - **Open places & Re-alignment**: When a mortal developer conquers a Voidrift slot and ascends to Nethergarde, they leave an **open place** in their former mortal civilization (`open = places − current members`). Any other mortal developer can **instantly re-align** into a mortal civilization that has an open place (putting their own place up for the next mover) as long as:
     1. Every march of theirs is home (no outbound, fighting, or returning armies),
     2. Their city walls are not burning (> 25% wall HP), and
     3. The two civilizations are not locked in **War Frenzy** this week (triggered as soon as any battle occurs between the two civilizations; resets Sunday 00:00 UTC).
   - Two mortal rulers can also **swap** civilizations by mutual agreement (§13) when both have all marches home, neither is burning, and their civilizations are not in War Frenzy.
   - **New Developer Placement**: When a new developer account is first registered from Git, the server automatically places them into the mortal civilization with the lowest current headcount (filling open places first). Nethergarde Voidrift (`arcane`) is strictly war-conquest only and is never assigned to newly registered developers.
   - **Nethergarde is war-only:** nobody can choose it (it is entered by conquering a Voidrift slot, §13), and a Nethergarde developer can't change or swap civilization — they leave only when their citadel falls.

| Civ | Title | Province | Livestock | Small animals | Crops |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Human | Kingdom | Valorian Heartlands | sheep, cow, pig | chickens | wheat, cabbage, pumpkin |
| Elf | Sanctuary | Sylvandale Glades | deer, horse, rabbit | swans | berries, herbs, orchard |
| Dwarf | Bastion | Ironforge Highlands | goat, boar, pony | geese | mushroom, potato, hops |
| Orc | Stronghold | Badlands of Drak'mar | wolf, boar, lizard | crows | gourd, meat |
| Arcane | Spire | Nethergarde Voidrift | sheep, stag, cat | owls | crystal, lotus |

Each civ has its own colour palette, townsfolk models (knights, hooded elven archers, bearded dwarves, tusked orcs, robed battle-mages), flowers (elf and arcane flowers glow) and castle style.

### Unique Traits

Every civilization has one **Unique Trait**: a legend that explains its standing buffs and debuffs (§13). It changes no numbers. It is shown in the Game Guide's civilization chapters, on the Royal Codex castle entries and in the 👥 Kingdom panel above the ▲ / ▼ chips (hover for the story). The full card also tells why the civilization copes with one world disaster and suffers from another (§10 *World Disasters*).

| Civ | Unique Trait | What it explains |
|---|---|---|
| 🏹 Elf | **Echoes of the Thousand Years**: an elf soldier was already old when the human kingdoms were founded. Elves are so few that every ranger is a veteran of a hundred wars, so one elf is worth five to ten soldiers of the younger races, and they fight hardest in their own glades | Void-Piercing Volleys, Volleys From the Treeline, Cull the Vanguard, Hold the Glade, Armor-Piercing Runes, Glade Ambush *Heartseeker Volleys*: an elven archer knows where a colossus's hide wears thin, so elves land 200% on world bosses (§13). *Moonwell Healers*: no elf is left on a battlefield; healers carry the fallen home to the Moonwell, so half of an elf army's battle losses come home wounded instead of dying (§13 *Moonwell Healers*). |
| 🔮 Nethergarde | **The Unbound Accord**: the Voidrift stands outside the world's laws. Its people are neither born nor age; they are bound to the rift, and it lends its strength to whatever it holds | Universal Harmony: the one buff that needs no reason |
| 👑 Human | **The Banner of Many Hands**: humans win by being many, and by being fed. *Massed Levies*: when the rift first opened every village sent its sons under the Dawn Banner, and facing the void they still march as one host. *Grain-Fed Hosts*: orc warbands live off raiding and starve within weeks; a human army marches behind full grain wagons and outlasts them in the Badlands | Massed Levies, Grain-Fed Hosts |
| ⚒️ Dwarf | **Oath of the Mountain Halls**: matchless behind stone and against the horde, slow and uneasy elsewhere. A thousand years of orc raids taught every clan the shield wall (*Anvil Against the Horde*, *Break the Charge*); in their halls every wall is a weapon (*Stone Ramparts*); they don't leave the mountain gladly (*Slow Advance*); their rune-forged steel was made for this world, and void flesh drinks its edge (*Void-Dulled Steel*) | all five dwarf modifiers *Giant-Felling Runes*: the oldest runes on dwarven steel were cut in the giant wars, so dwarves land 180% on world bosses (§13). |
| 🛡️ Orc | **Blood of Drak'mar**: orcs fight on fury and numbers. A berserker's rage feeds on the enemy's fear and pain; the Void Weavers feel neither, so the frenzy breaks against them; nor does a colossus, whose roar drowns the war-drums | Frenzy Breaks on Void Shields, Frenzy Breaks on Colossi (world bosses) |

### Seasons

On top of the standing modifiers, each week's **season** buffs one mortal civilization and debuffs another (±10% harvest, ±10% births, ±5% march speed; never combat stats): spring favours elves over orcs, summer orcs over dwarves, autumn humans over elves, winter dwarves over humans. Nethergarde is outside the seasons. See §7 *Day, night & seasons*.

**World disasters** (§10) are the other side of a civilization's luck: each civilization copes with one disaster and suffers from another (Dwarves cope with the Great Blight and suffer Tempest Roads; Elves cope with Tempest Roads and suffer the Blood Moon; Orcs cope with the Blood Moon and suffer the Wasting Plague; Nethergarde copes with the Wasting Plague and suffers the Barren Spell; Humans cope with the Barren Spell and suffer the Great Blight).

---

## 10. The World Map & Living Frontier

A persistent map with **five provinces**, one per civilization, separated by natural buffer zones with persistent and dynamic points of interest. Each kingdom's castle is placed in its civilization's province; the map is generated once, saved to disk, and new kingdoms are slotted in without moving anyone. Click a castle to open its actions (enter the city, profile, scout, attack, reinforce, send resources, propose a swap).

### Finding your way

* **Your home beacon**: when you are signed in, a soft golden pillar of light (about as tall as a Wonder's curtain of light) rises from your castle and a thin crest ring pulses round its grounds, so you can spot it on the continent. Your castle's badge is gold and reads **👑 Home Sovereign**.
* **Kingdom picker** (the developer dropdown in the top bar): on the world map, picking a developer glides the camera to their castle, marks it with a silver ring and makes it the current kingdom (the top bar shows it). You stay on the map. Inside a city, picking a developer switches to their city. **My Kingdom** in the main menu works the same way.
* **⏎ Enter**: a castle picked in the kingdom picker shows a bouncing **⏎ Enter** arrow over its badge. Press the **Enter** key to step into that city; any click on the map (or clicking a castle, which opens its actions as usual) dismisses the hint. The **Enter Kingdom** button in the top bar enters the current kingdom too.
* Coming back from a city always shows the whole realm again.

**Where castles stand**: every castle sits in its civilization's province, scattered like towns rather than in rings (the provinces fill loosely from the heart outward; the Voidrift's few castles are scattered round the rift at random), at least **8 units** from any other castle, 6 from the fixed landmarks (bosses, buffer kingdoms, gates, the Nethergarde Core), 4.5 from the frontier gates' border walls and 12 from its province's legendary landmark at the centre, so no two overlap. Resource nodes and roving spawns keep off the walls too. Nethergarde castles and the NPC kingdoms on open Voidrift slots stand scattered in a band round the rift's heart, **10 to 18 units** from the Nethergarde Core: clear of the Core's glowing disc, never drifting off toward the province's edges. With the Archon seated they keep **14 units** from the citadel and never stand straight behind it as the map camera sees it, so the Empyrean and the Eye never hide one. A castle keeps its spot while it follows these rules; a new ruler, a civilization change or a crowded spot gets the first free plot.

**Nethergarde Voidrift is special**: it is the **sole province with an orbiting aerial phenomenon** — towering obsidian shards levitating and circling high above the glowing crystalline crater rift. The other four provinces feature clean open skies, emphasizing the mystical anomaly of the Nethergarde Voidrift.

**The land** is one continent that changes as you cross it, never a grid of tiles: Valoria's farmland is a patchwork of irregular fields with soft hedgerow edges (wheat, young crops, ploughed earth, flower meadows and pasture) between dry and lush meadows; Sylvandale has mossy dells, the Deepwood moss and leaf litter, the highlands pale scree and low moss, the badlands ochre dust and scorched earth, the desert lighter and darker dunes. Gentle swells keep flat land from being perfectly flat. With **HD graphics** (☰ menu → **⚙️ Game settings** → *Graphics*: **1 · Normal** runs on any laptop, **2 · HD** adds bloom, soft reflections on metal, gilding and polished glaze only (matte ground, stone and wood look as in Normal), richer rarity effects, the Archon's Empyrean as its high-graphics citadel, the legendary landmarks, the Nethergarde Core, the legendary resource nodes and the Relic Hoard as their hand-built models (§10 *Legendary landmarks in HD*, *Legendary sites in HD*), the twelve world bosses as hand-built creatures (§10 *World bosses in HD*) and the legendary creatures too (§10 *Legendary creatures in HD*), every creature, band and site in its HD version, every castle (on the map and as the city's town hall), city improvement and district structure in its HD version (§5, §6, §8), the townsfolk, animals and soldiers too (rounder shapes, real materials, soft shading, a relaxed pose and the extra detail its rarity earns: see *Guide Book & Royal Codex* below), and this ground; HD's **HD terrain** switch turns the ground texture and grass off on their own, **HD water** the moving water surface, and **Antialiasing** (off at first) smooths every edge at a real cost on laptop graphics; saved in this browser), the ground gets a texture that shows as you zoom in and fades out as you zoom away, so the overview stays a clean map: grass blades and clumps with bare dirt patches, crop rows running along each field, gravel and mossy flagstone outcrops in the highlands, rock with strata and cracks on every steep slope, wind ripples in the sand, a wet band lapping up and down every shore, glittering snow on the peaks, cracked mud pans with embers glowing in the burnt badlands, and void glitter and faint light veins in the rift. Up close, **grass sways in the wind** round the view: lush tufts in the green lands with flowers in the meadows and glades, sparse dry straw on the steppe and badlands, reeds with cattails along the water's edge, none in the water itself, on castle and landmark grounds or in and beside the lava fissures and forge vents (no tree or rock stands there either); it takes the land's season colours, and in winter lies low and frosted with no flowers.

### Persistent Buffer Zones

Four geographic buffer zones isolate the continental provinces:
* **The Sunken Fjord** (`sea`) — separating Sylvandale and Ironforge
* **The Scorchwind Expanse** (`desert`) — separating Valoria and Drak'mar
* **The Whispering Deepwood** (`forest`) — separating Valoria and Sylvandale
* **The Dragonspine Peaks** (`mountains`) — separating Ironforge and Drak'mar

Each buffer zone keeps a boss, a buffer kingdom and its landmarks, saved on disk:
