1. **Sovereign Boss Monster**: Each zone has 3 unique boss types defined (e.g. Leviathan Kraken Lord, Dagon Tsunami Sovereign, Siren Broodmother in the Fjord; Sandworm, Sphinx, Scorpion Colossus in the desert). One boss is active at a time; the next one in the zone's pool takes over **every Sunday 00:00 UTC**, whatever state the current one is in (see *World bosses* in §13).
2. **Buffer realm**: an NPC kingdom from the zone's pool of **3** (Sea Fjord: Corsair Sovereign Citadel, Port Royale Freebooters, High-Tide Merfolk Enclave · Scorchwind: Nomad Dune Confederacy, Sultanate of the Brass Mirage, Sandstrider Republic · Deepwood: Sylvan Ranger Barony, Dryad Principality, Greenwood Hermit Bastion · Dragonspine: Basalt Forgehold Keep, Thunderpeak Clan Bastion, Dragon-Rider Eyrie). It grows from common to epic over a **28-day cycle**, then leaves, and another of the pool rises somewhere else in the region (see *Buffer realms* below and *NPC kingdoms* in §13). It is a simulated kingdom you can attack.
3. **One epic landmark** out of the region's pool of six, in the region's style, rotating every week (below).

**Buffer realms — the 28-day cycle.** Each zone's realm climbs the rarity ladder one week at a time ([worldMapGenerator.ts](server/src/worldMapGenerator.ts) `cycleRealms`, levels in [warConfig.ts](server/src/warConfig.ts) `REALM_CYCLE`):

| Week | Stage | City Hall | Looks |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Common | 6 → 10 | the realm's core alone, small and matte |
| 2 | Uncommon | 11 → 15 | houses round it and a banner in its colour |
| 3 | Rare | 16 → 20 | more houses, a wall ring with four towers, metal caps and glowing braziers |
| 4 | Epic | 21 → 25 | the full settlement, a great tower with lit windows, banners on every tower, motes orbiting the core and the epic aura |

* **Day 1 of every week it is built up**: the stage's new parts rise in scaffolding over the finished stage before it (week 1: from the ground), with masons at work. All that day it is **shielded**: no attacks or rallies (scouts may look), and armies arriving turn back. It stands at the previous stage's top level (week 1: City Hall 5).
* **Days 2–6 it gains a City Hall level a day** (00:00 UTC), and each level-up rebuilds it **good as new**: full population, garrison and treasury, walls mended. **Day 7** it holds the stage's top level.
* After its epic week it **leaves the realm**, crumbling away for a day where it stood (it can't be attacked), while another realm of the pool (never the same one twice running) is built at a fresh random spot in the region, keeping clear of castles, landmarks and the day's resource nodes.
* **The zones are a week apart**, so every week the map shows one realm of each stage. Weeks start Sunday 00:00 UTC, with the landmark rotation.
* A realm grows on the map with its stage (×0.75 common → ×1.1 epic: from smaller than a landmark to about twice its width), and its **soldiers** (its donor civilization's, §13) patrol round it: 2 at common, 4 uncommon, 6 rare, 8 epic, with townsfolk among the houses. Sea realms stand in the water on a plank **jetty ring** their soldiers walk, with houses on stilts and a stone breakwater for walls.
* The hover card and target panel show the stage, City Hall and day (e.g. *Rare realm · City Hall 16 · day 2 of 7*), when the stage ends and what comes next.
* Maps saved before the cycle keep their realm where it stands, which joins the cycle at the current week (a retired design is swapped for one of the pool).

**Legendary landmarks — the heart of each province.** As the Nethergarde Core is the heart of the Voidrift, each mortal province has one legendary landmark at its centre, in its civilization's style, always standing (never re-rolled): **The Cathedral of the Dawn Banner** (Valoria: a white-stone cathedral under blue slate roofs with gold bands, twin front towers, a glowing rose window and lancet windows, a bell tower crowned by a turning golden sun, a statue of the First King raising the Dawn Banner before its doors, clipped hedges, fountains and blue-and-gold banners; it stands just east of the centre, beside the river), **The Heart-Oak** (Sylvandale: the first tree, with roots holding up the forest floor, a hollow heart glowing green, a swaying layered crown, warden stones and circling wisps), **The Anvil of the Gods** (Ironforge: a keep-sized anvil on a plateau, a god's hammer circling above it, rivers of forge-fire, smoking braziers and rune stones) and **The Bloodfire Throne** (Drak'mar: a throne hewn from a titan's skull on a red mesa, the bones of the first Warchief on it with his war-axe raised, war drums before it, burning skull totems and war banners round it, fire in the cracks of the mesa). Castles keep **12 units** from them (6 from other fixed landmarks), so each province's castles ring its heart. Like the other landmarks they can be scouted for their lore. Five pilgrims of the province's own civilization (human, elf, dwarf or orc folk in their festival colours) walk up to each one to bow, gaze up or admire it, as Nethergarde folk do at the Archon's seat.

**Epic landmarks — the weekly rotation.** Each buffer region has a pool of **six epic landmarks** in its own style, and **one of them stands at a time**:

| Region | Its six epic landmarks |
|---|---|
| 🌊 The Sunken Fjord (sea ruins and wrecks) | Wreck of the Kraken's Folly, Luminescent Coral Trench, Atlantis Sunken Spire, Siren Pearl Lagoon, The Drowned Lighthouse of Saltmere, The Leviathan's Jaw |
| 🏜️ The Scorchwind Expanse (desert temples) | Mirage Palm Oasis, Great Obsidian Pyramid, Titan Beast Skeleton, Sunken Sandstone Canyon, Temple of the Burning Disc, Valley of the Sleeping Kings |
| 🌲 The Whispering Deepwood (forest shrines) | Stonehenge of the Leyline, World Tree Hollow, Fairy Mushroom Megalith, Blackwood Bandit Den, The Moonwell Shrine, The Grove of Whispering Spirits |
| 🏔️ The Dragonspine Peaks (mountain forges) | Mount Ignis Caldera, The Deep Iron Delve, Glacial Wyrm Peak, Basalt Pillar Gorge, The Giants' Forge, The Old King's Skybridge |

* **Rotation**: every **Sunday 00:00 UTC** (with the realm's other weekly resets) every region swaps its standing landmark for another of its pool, picked at random.
* **Rotation day**: for that day each region shows two: the leaving one is **🏚️ crumbling** where it stood (parts tilt, sink and grey, the high stones fall first into rubble heaps, lights go out, orbiting stones drop, dust rises) and the arriving one is **🏗️ rising** at a new spot (only the lower courses stand, cut off flat at a height that climbs through the day, inside scaffolding with a slewing crane, stone blocks and lumber piles, masons hammering and porters hauling). Magical sites (the Coral Trench, Atlantis Spire, Pearl Lagoon, Leviathan's Jaw, Obsidian Pyramid, Stonehenge, Mushroom Megalith, Moonwell, Spirit Grove and Glacial Peak) are **shaped** instead: their missing stones float round the site with a shimmer, robed shapers at work. Both states move on in **10 steps** over the day; on Monday only the new one stands, finished. The hover card and panel read *🏗️ Rising — complete in 6 h* or *🏚️ Falling to ruin — gone in 6 h*, and scouting either one adds a line about what is happening to it. A crumbling landmark loses its epic aura; onlookers and carrion birds gather at it.
* **Placement**: each arriving landmark gets a random spot anywhere in its region (between the two provinces, from outside the Voidrift to the coast), at least **16 units** from every other landmark (the provinces' legendary landmarks, the Nethergarde Core, the landmarks standing and the one still crumbling), **14** from castles, the boss, the buffer kingdom, the frontier gates and the day's resource nodes, and 6 from the border walls. When no spot that far is free, it takes the spot that falls least short. Trees under it are cleared, and a land landmark on open water gets an islet. The day's resource nodes keep to the landmarks as they stood at 00:00 UTC, so an admin reset never moves a node out from under its gatherers.
* **Older saved maps** are upgraded in place on the first start: the region's existing landmark stays where it stands (the old shipwreck becomes the Wreck of the Kraken's Folly model), finished; a region showing more than one keeps only its first; castles, bosses, buffer kingdoms and Voidrift slots don't move. The first rotation day is the next Sunday.
* Admins can start a rotation any time (🗿 **Reset Landmarks**, §11).

Every buffer realm and landmark has its **own 3D model** (e.g. the Corsair Sovereign Citadel on volcanic sea pillars, the Brass Sultanate with domes and minarets, Stonehenge of the Leyline, the fallen World Tree Hollow, the Mount Ignis Caldera); a realm's model grows with its stage (above). Sea-based ones (Corsair Citadel, Port Royale, Merfolk Enclave, the Wreck of the Kraken's Folly, Coral Trench, Atlantis Spire, Pearl Lagoon, the Drowned Lighthouse and the Leviathan's Jaw) float at sea level instead of standing on an island.

**Frontier gates**: one gate stands on the road from the Voidrift into each mortal province, its front facing the Voidrift. From each gatehouse a **border wall** curves away both ways round the province (about 20 units in all, following the lie of the land, with towers along it), long enough to read as a border without walling the province in. Each gate is built in its civilization's style and guarded by three of its soldiers walking the wall: **The Imperial High Gate** (Valoria: twin pale-stone towers under blue cones, a crenellated arch, portcullis, royal crest and hanging blue banners; a crenellated pale-stone wall with blue-roofed round towers), **The Tusked War Pass** (Drak'mar: a sharpened log palisade between two great mammoth tusks, a skull with burning eyes over the gap, war fires smoking; a lashed log palisade with skull-topped watch posts and red banners), **The Moonwood Arch** (Sylvandale: two living trees grown into one arch, swaying crowns, moonstone lanterns and drifting wisps; a white-stone wall crowned with a living hedge, slim white towers with teal roofs and moonstones) and **The Anvilhold Deepgate** (Ironforge: an iron door cut into a rune-carved cliff, two stone dwarves with raised hammers, smoking braziers; a dark stone wall with glowing rune bands and square brazier towers). Each is an NPC kingdom (§13) that only the other civilizations can attack or rally on, and while it stands it **seals its province**. A **burning** gate looks fallen: whatever closed the road (door, portcullis, arch, skull, the joined crown) is gone, the rest is knocked down to charred stumps with the smashed door and rubble on the road, its border wall lies broken for about 12 units either side, fire burns over the wreck and along the broken wall, and its guards are gone (it is whole again once its walls repair). While it stands: no other civilization can attack a city there until the gate burns (§13 *Frontier gates*).

A new world lays each zone out as a triangle: the boss holds the centre of the buffer zone, while the kingdom and the first landmark sit 12 units further out toward the coast, 16 units apart from each other; the second landmark is placed by the rotation's rules. Saved maps from older layouts are re-laid out automatically on the next load (same picks; castles too close to a province's legendary landmark move out to the next free plot once).

### Who is online

Server: `onlineRulerIds` in [broadcasts.ts](server/src/broadcasts.ts) · Client: [presence.ts](src/utils/presence.ts)

A ruler is **online** while they are signed in with the realm open (their page checked in within the last 2.5 minutes). On the world map an online ruler's castle stands in a **white curtain of light**, the storm light of a punishment in white, like a boss rising, and a 🟢 green dot sits next to their name on the castle label (hover: *🟢 Online now*). A punished castle keeps its storm light, a burning one its fire, and a Wonder or the Empyrean its own kit, so for those only the dot shows. Visitors looking around without signing in are never shown.

### Rarity

Rarity belongs to the **race or resource type**: a slime is always common, a drake always epic, a gold mine always rare. Rarer things really are rarer: resource nodes (daily and wave) are picked by the weights below, and roving encounters roll a fixed number of slots per tier (see Dynamic World Spawns):

| Tier | Weight | Ring colour | Size on map |
|---|---|---|---|
| Common | 50 | slate | 0.9× |
| Uncommon | 28 | emerald | 1.0× |
| Rare | 14 | sapphire | 1.1× |
| Epic | 6 | purple | 1.22× |
| Legendary | 2 | gold | 1.36× |

Legendary is otherwise reserved for the world raid bosses, the four provinces' legendary landmarks and the Nethergarde Core. Above it sits **Mythical** (crimson), which never rolls: it belongs only to the Overseers' horror (so far a Codex teaser) and to the Archon's Empyrean and Celestial plots.

**Rarity reads at a glance.** Everything in the realm is styled on one ladder, so how special something is looks the same everywhere: map NPCs, mercenaries, castles, city plots and improvements. Each tier gets its effects from one shared, instanced kit (one draw call per effect kind for the whole view, however many things shine). Nothing is drawn as a disc on the ground: the light stands **round** the structure, like the column over a reviving boss, and creatures (mobs, bandits, mercenaries) get no curtain at all, only the sparkles and, at legendary, the beam:

| Tier | Feel | Model | Effects |
|---|---|---|---|
| Common | plain | matte, small | none |
| Uncommon | ok | one accent colour, a small prop (banner, lantern) | none |
| Rare | good | metal or glow accents, one small looping motion | a few glints twinkling round it |
| Epic | better | bigger silhouette, glowing details, moving parts | a low, faint curtain of light round it and motes orbiting it |
| Legendary | **grand** (two steps above epic) | the richest models | a tall bright curtain of rising light round it, a beam of light, rising embers and orbiting motes |
| Mythical | **grander** | the very top | a tall crimson curtain with a prismatic shimmer, a crimson beam, twice the embers, orbiting shards |

Progression joins the same ladder: a **Wonder** castle (Lv21–25, §6) and a **red-star** building (5+ prestige stars, §5) are legendary-class, the Archon's **Empyrean** and **Celestial** plots mythic-class, and improvements that stand as one structure (the city gate for Walls, the Plaza Fountain, the Dragon Statue, the Mana Spire) are rare at tier 3, epic at tier 4 and **legendary at tier 5** (§8). World bosses keep their own aura and sparks.

**Monsters and bandits wear their tier.** Every roving band has a bigger **leader** in the middle and is built on the ladder: **common** bands (slimes, wolves, bats, rats, spiders, highwaymen, pickpockets, poachers) are plain and matte, with nothing glowing, not even their eyes; **uncommon** bands share **one accent colour** and carry a **small prop** (the goblins' red war-paint and banner, the skeletons' teal shields and banner, the scorpions' amber claws and skull stake, the harpies' crimson crests and nest, the lizardfolk's amber paint and totem, the smugglers' teal scarves and lantern, the river pirates' red sail and mast lantern, the raiders' indigo turbans and pennant); **rare** bands get **metal or glowing trim** and **one looping effect** (the trolls' iron armour and cook-fire smoke, the chimera's burning eyes and smoking nostrils, the werewolves' silver manacles under a pulsing moon, the ogres' iron plates and an ox turning on the spit, the wraiths' spirit lantern, the cultists' gold-hemmed robes and effigy smoke, the corsairs' brass and streaming black flag, the syndicate's glowing eye-slits and smoke bomb); **epic** bands are **bigger and armoured**, with **glowing details** and **things orbiting them** (the magma golem's lava seams and embers, the drake's glowing belly and embers round its eggs, the aberration's blinking eyes and void shards over jutting void crystals, the basilisk's glowing ridge and petrifying motes, the lich's runed robes and soul-wisps, the warlord's rune-axe and crows round his banner, the mercenary commander's glowing visor and ravens, the saboteurs' glowing goggles, lit fuses and sparks).

**Legendary sites look the part**: the legendary resource nodes, the Relic Hoard, the legendary landmarks and the Nethergarde Core are animated models with a **beam of light** rising from them, a pulsing aura and drifting sparks. The Volcanic Dragonstone Core is a volcano whose dragonstone heart beats over a lava mouth, with shards circling it, lava running down its slopes and a burning-eyed dragon skull on its flank. King's Motherlode is a gilded mine with gold veins, a rocking ore cart, coins circling and a crown turning over the entrance. The Relic Hoard is a chest floating over a rune dais, its lid breathing open on a glow of gold, inside two turning void rings. The Nethergarde Core is a void heart beating over a pool of night, ringed by obsidian monoliths, with three rings, shards and boulders wheeling round it.

**Legendary sites in HD.** With Graphics set to HD, the two legendary resource nodes and the Relic Hoard are hand-built models too, the same on the map (at the legendary size) and in the Codex, two steps above the epic nodes and treasures, alive through their parts, with the legendary aura (beam, curtain of light and embers) in their own colour. They have one look: a site harvested to its last share leaves the map, and the share left shows on its ground ring, as in Normal.
* **The Volcanic Dragonstone Core**: a volcano of craggy basalt in strata with molten veins, a jagged rim round a lava lake with dark crust drifting on it; the dragonstone heart, a glowing crystal in a cluster of dark ruby shards beating out of the lake over a white-hot well, an iron collar on it chained to stakes on the rim; shards wheeling round it; lava winding down the slopes in rock-banked channels into glowing pools; basalt columns and scree round the foot; a dragon's skull half buried on the slope (horns sweeping back, teeth, the jaw dropped, fire in its sockets) with its rib hoops breaking the ash behind it; smoke and embers pouring from the crater.
* **The King's Motherlode**: a mountain in strata laced with gold veins that glint, a royal portal of dressed stone cut into the rock face (voussoirs and a gilt keystone, pilasters with gilt capitals, a gilt-banded cornice, iron-banded doors swung open, lanterns swinging, timber props in the drift and a glow of gold deep inside); a timber headframe over a shaft beside it, its pulley wheel turning and a bucket of gold riding up and down; rails on sleepers out of the drift to an ore cart heaped with gold, heaps of gold with coins and gems spilled round them, quartz geodes and gold nuggets bursting from the slopes, the king's crimson banners, and a great crown turning over the door with coins circling it.
* **The Ancient Nethergarde Relic Hoard**: a dais of dark violet stone in three steps with silver lips and lit runes, four obsidian pylons with silver bands and caps, runes up their faces and a crystal floating over each; the reliquary floating and bobbing over it, lacquered void-wood bound in silver with a lock plate and a burning gem, its domed lid rocking open on the light inside; two void rings with silver beads turning on tilted axes round it; the old court's relics circling high above (a crown, a goblet, an orb, a sceptre, a tome, a great gem); coins, gems, gold bars and a toppled goblet spilled on the steps; wisps and motes.

**World bosses in HD.** With Graphics set to HD, all twelve world bosses are hand-built creatures, the same on the map and in the Codex, two steps above the epic creatures. They move like living things: a relaxed, uneven rest pose; a layered idle (breathing, limbs and tentacles each on their own tempo, a head glancing round in snaps, blinks, a tail flick, a claw flexing); a roar now and then, and on a loop while armies fight it, facing them, an attack that coils, hangs at the top, strikes through the target with a burst of spray, sand or venom and follows through. The Greatwyrm and the Fae Queen fly: each keeps to the air over its roost on a drifting figure of eight (never a circle), heading along it, banking into its turns and pitching as it climbs, its wings beating on an uneven stroke (a hard downstroke that heaves the body up, a slower recovery) with now and then a long glide on spread wings; when it roars or attacks it comes back to hover over its roost, still beating. A slumbering boss is the same model as a translucent phantom with a cyan rim in its column of light, as in Normal. No beam of light rises over a boss: embers and its own sparks (spray and mist at sea, blowing sand and dust in the desert, fireflies and mist in the Deepwood, smoke and embers off the Greatwyrm's lava, blowing snow on the peaks) instead of the ground aura. In the Codex its HD preview has **Idle / Roar / Attack / Slumbering** buttons; an impact there shakes the model, flashes, throws speed lines and closes a vignette for a moment.
* **Leviathan Kraken Lord**: a great wet mantle leaning back over its head, mottled, warty, barnacled and ridged, veins of light pulsing up its flanks and a sunken king's crown askew on its crest; amber eyes on bulges with bar pupils under heavy brows, lids that blink; a hooked beak; eight arms with suckers and glowing spots, the tips curling, the two hunting arms arching up with hooked clubs; a churning pool with foam, rocks and a galleon's broken mast with its torn sail. It rears its hunting arms high and slams them down on the water ahead; its roar flings every arm up and out like a fountain.
* **Dagon Tsunami Sovereign**: scaled coils with spined fins arching out of the water and a tail fluke breaking it; a long neck rising in an S with pale belly plates glowing down it and a fin along its back; a sea-serpent head with fangs, swept horns, a coral crown set with pearls, cyan eyes and fanned frills. Its neck sways while the head holds steady and glances round; it coils back and lunges, jaws snapping shut on the impact; it roars rearing high with its jaws gaping and frills flared.
* **Siren Broodmother**: a wet sea rock with shells, coral, a sailor's skull and ribs and swaying kelp; her scaled tail coiled round it with a finned fluke in the water; a gaunt, corpse-pale queen with ribs showing, a bodice of shell scales, a pearl necklace with a glowing stone, burning green eyes in dark sockets, a jaw that drops, long dark hair, a crown of bone, coral and pearls, long clawed arms and webbed fin-wings. She rakes down with both claws; she screams with her back arched and arms and wings flung wide.
* **Great Sandworm Ouroboros**: a ringed, armoured worm rising out of a sand crater (spines down its back, a paler belly), its lamprey maw a ring of four toothed petals round two rings of inward teeth turning against each other and a blazing throat, two great tusks beside it; old ribs and a toppled pillar in the sand. It draws back, hangs and slams its maw down into the sand ahead; it roars rearing tall, the petals flung wide.
* **Anubis Sunken Sphinx**: a lion of carved sandstone with glowing green cracks lying on a temple plinth half sunk in the sand (dressed blocks, a band of glowing hieroglyphs, broken columns); a broad gold-and-lapis collar, the striped nemes headdress, the gold death mask with emerald eyes lined in kohl, a cobra rearing from its brow, a plaited beard; great wings of turquoise, lapis and gold feathers; paws with gold claws, a tufted tail. It raises a paw high and swipes it down across; it roars rising on its forelegs with its wings flung up.
* **Dune Colossus Behemoth**: a colossal scorpion of glossy black chitin in ridged plates trimmed with old bronze, venom glowing in the seams and at the tail's joints; a cluster of burning red eyes, working mandibles, eight jointed legs that shift their footing, two great pincers with toothed fingers that clack; a tail arching over its back to a venom bulb and stinger. Its pincers lunge and snap while the stinger whips over its back; it roars rearing up with pincers spread open and tail arched high.
* **Elder Treant Heartwood**: a living ironwood on a mound of moss among its own roots: a trunk of twisting bark strands, a gnarled brow over ember eyes, a cracked mouth with fire in it and a beard of moss, blisters of glowing red sap, shelf fungus; two great branch arms with twigs in leaf and thorned twig fingers; a crown of boughs under swaying masses of leaves with dead snags rising out of it; glowing sap pools and mushrooms at its roots, fireflies round its crown. It hauls both arms up, hangs and smashes them down into the ground ahead, bowing after them; it roars rearing back with its arms flung wide.
* **Fenrir Shadow Wolf Packlord**: a colossal charcoal wolf in shaggy fur with a black ruff, spines of black bone bristling down its back, a paler grizzled chest, legs with paws and hooked claws, a long muzzle with fangs and a jaw that drops on a red-glowing throat, burning red eyes, turning ears and a bushy tail; shadow curling off it, on a glade floor with a fallen log and the bones of its last kill. It shifts its weight, lifts a forepaw, flicks its ears and sweeps its tail; it crouches, hangs and lunges, its jaws snapping shut, then shakes its catch; its roar is a howl, head thrown back.
* **Queen of the Fae Court**: hovering over a mossy knoll ringed with brambles, glowing mushrooms and a fairy ring of lit stones: a slender queen in a gown of layered violet petals (the lowest fluttering), a pale face with glowing eyes and long pointed ears, dark hair streaming back, a crown of thorns with twig antlers and a gem of light, long thorn-clawed arms, an orb of fae light in her right hand, two pairs of veined wings of light shimmering in their colours, motes circling her. She draws both hands back, hangs and flings a bolt of light at her foe; she roars arching back with arms and wings flung wide.
* **Ignis Molten Greatwyrm**: a crag of basalt laced with molten veins round a pool of lava, obsidian shards and smoking spires; the dragon in flight over it: a scaled crimson body with pale belly plates and veins of fire in its flanks, spines down its back, a long neck and a tail ending in a spade of horn, a horned head with burning eyes, fangs, a glowing throat and a jaw that drops, legs tucked up, great bat wings of arm, forearm and three long finger bones with leathery membranes between them. It rears back in the air, hangs with its throat swelling and lunges, breathing a long flickering cone of fire ahead; it roars rearing upright, wings flung high.
* **Frost Titan Yeti Chieftain**: a hunched white titan in shaggy fur on a snow drift with ice spikes thrust through it: a bare grey face with a heavy brow, burning cyan eyes, tusks and a beard of shag, ram's horns curling round its ears, ice crystals grown over its shoulders and back, huge arms with fur cuffs and grey fists rimed with ice. It breathes heavily, rolls its shoulders and flexes its fists; it raises both fists high, hangs and hammers them down on the ground ahead with a burst of ice; it beats its chest left and right, then roars with its arms flung wide.
* **Gorgon Stonecrusher**: a serpent queen coiled on a floor of cracked flagstones with a broken column and two soldiers turned to stone: a scaled tail with a pale belly coiled under her (its tip flicking), a woman's grey-green torso in a bronze cuirass and spiked pauldrons, a face with burning green eyes and a serpent's fangs, a bronze diadem and a crown of seven living snakes, each with glowing eyes and a tongue, writhing on its own; a stone maul banded in bronze with a glowing rune in her right hand, black claws on her left. She swings the maul up over her shoulder, hangs and crashes it down on the ground ahead, her whole body following it; she hisses rearing high on her coils with the snakes flared out.

**Legendary creatures in HD.** The two legendary creatures that are not world bosses are hand-built the same way, on the bosses' rig (one batch, so any number of them costs no more draw calls than one): a relaxed, uneven rest pose, a layered idle with seeded events, a "roar" and an attack that coil, hang, strike mid-swing and follow through, embers and their own sparks, no beam and no curtain. Neither slumbers, so each has its own buttons in the Codex.
* **Vargo Blackfang, the Bandit King** (*Idle / War cry / Attack*): a broad warlord in black leather and studded brigandine with gold buckles, a fur mantle under spiked iron pauldrons and a blood-red tattered cloak that trails in the wind; a bone mask with burning red eyes, black fangs and teeth under a crimson hood, a stolen crown worn askew on it and a second hung at his belt beside two empty scabbards; a curved saber in each fist (steel, a gold guard and pommel, the cutting edge lit crimson). He stands with his feet planted apart on a heap of plunder (earth poured over with coin, an iron-bound chest spilling gold and gems, another shut, a goblet on its side, loose gems, a rival's skull, a sword driven in) under his war banner (a tattered crimson flag with a painted skull, a horned skull on the pole). His warband stays round him: four hooded henchmen with red eyes over their scarves (an axe, a spear, a burning torch, a crossbow with a bolt laid in it) and a black war hound in a spiked collar; three crows circle overhead on drifting paths, beating and gliding. In his idle he breathes and shifts his weight from foot to foot, glances round, now and then flourishes a saber or rests the other on his shoulder; the henchmen shift and look about, the hound pants, sniffs the ground and wags. His war cry: a crouch, then both sabers flung up high, head back and eyes blazing, the warband raising its weapons and the hound howling, the crows wheeling wider. His attack: weight back with both sabers drawn up behind his head, a hang, then a step in as the blades cross down through an X in a burst of crimson sparks, the warband lunging and the hound leaping.
* **Archmage of the Voidrift** (the legendary mercenary, *Idle / March / Invoke / Cast*): the gilded mage come to life: a face of living gold with violet-burning eyes under a heavy brow and a long white beard that swings, a wide-brimmed hat with a gold band, a void gem and a ring of violet light, its tip drooping back; a cream underskirt under a gilded over-robe open at the front with gilt studs down its edges, a band of violet runes glowing round its hem over a gold trim, a violet stole, a gold sash with knotted tails, a capelet and gold shoulder plates, a violet cape that trails, and a rayed gold halo turning slowly behind his head. His right fist grips the crescent staff of ringed crystal (a void crystal floating in the crescent with gold rings turning round it); over his open left palm floats the void orb, its heart pulsing and motes circling it. In HD he hovers with no rune circle at his feet (the ladder keeps rings off the ground): five shards of void crystal circle him on their own tilted paths. In his idle he drifts and breathes, his hem and cape swaying, the orb rolling over his palm; now and then he lifts it to his eyes. Invoke: he draws in, then rises with the staff raised high and the orb lifted, the halo flaring and the shards racing wider. Cast: the orb drawn back to his shoulder and the staff swung behind him, a hang, then both thrust out together in a burst of void light. Mercenaries don't march in the game yet; when they do, every Archmage on the map shares the same draws.

**Mercenaries in HD.** The rest of Voidrift's pool is built the same way, on the same rig as the Archmage (so any number of them still shares his draws), each strictly on its rung of the ladder below him and in his family's look (ashen lavender skin, violet and indigo cloth; the gold stays his). Each has a relaxed, uneven rest pose, a layered idle with seeded events and the strike its Normal model plays, with a hang before the blow and a follow-through after it; no beam, no ring or disc at its feet.
* **Rift Acolyte** (common, *Idle / March / Staff slam*): plain and matte, nothing lit. A novice in a coarse grey wool robe with a darker hem, a plum sash knotted at his hip with its tails swinging, sandalled feet, a deep hood with a drooping point over a young ashen face (brow, eyes under lids, nose, mouth). His right fist grips a plain grained staff with an iron ferrule and a wooden claw holding a polished violet stone (unlit, a novice's). He leans on it, breathes, glances round; now and then he looks up at the stone or rubs his free hand. Staff slam: the staff lifted upright, a hang, then slammed down ahead of him in a puff of dust.
* **Riftguard Sentinel** (uncommon, *Idle / March / Strike*): one accent and a small prop. Dull iron plate (a great helm with a dark visor slit and pierced breaths, a riveted breastplate with a ridge, layered pauldrons, greaves and sabatons) over dark leather; the accent a violet wool tabard front and back that swings as he moves and a round shield of violet-painted boards with an iron rim, boss, bands and rivets on his left arm; the prop an unlit iron lantern at his belt that swings, with a scabbard and a pouch. His arming sword of dull steel is gripped through the gauntlet. He shifts his weight, glances, rolls his wrist, hitches the shield. Strike: the sword drawn back over his shoulder behind the raised shield, a hang, a step in and a cut down, grit flying.
* **Astral Seer** (rare, *Idle / March / Divine*): steel trim and one looping effect. She hovers a little off the ground in a deep indigo robe whose hem trails into five points weighted with steel, silver stars stitched over it, a mantle edged in steel and clasped with a star stone, a steel-trimmed collar, a deep hood with two silver locks falling from it, a pale blindfold under a steel circlet with a star stone (its ends trailing behind); bell sleeves with steel cuffs over open hands. Her tome floats before her with steel corner bosses, a faint star over its pages and a page the air riffles now and then; the one looping effect is her five star-orbs circling her on tilted paths. Divine: her hands gather over the tome, head bowed, a hang, then her arms fly up and out, her face lifts, the tome rises and the orbs flare in a burst of blue star motes.
* **Eclipse Reaver** (epic, *Idle / March / Reap*): bigger, glowing, orbited. A horned reaver a head taller than the Riftguard in glossy obsidian plate (a breastplate over a waisted belly of bone plates, tassets tipped with bone, pointed sabatons, bone spikes on knees and elbows) with seams of violet light, an eclipse gem pulsing on his chest, great pauldrons with curved bone spikes and violet flame licking off them, a horned skull faceplate with burning eyes and a crest of flame, a long tattered violet cloak that trails on springs. His right fist grips the scythe (a black grained haft through it with iron bands and a bone skull knob, a crescent blade with a glowing violet edge); violet flame burns in his open, clawed left hand with cinders circling it; three eclipse shards orbit him and violet embers rise round him (fewer than round the Archmage). He breathes heavily, shifts his weight, stares round; now and then he rests the blade on his shoulder. Reap: the scythe drawn back high over his shoulder and the flame hand back, a hang, a step in and the blade sweeping across before him, then the flame thrust after it in a violet burst.

**Every mercenary marches.** A band is one whole march, and every mercenary has a **March** button: two steps on a loop in place, the body dipping on each footfall and swaying over the supporting leg, the shoulders turning against the hips, the arms swinging against the legs (an arm holding a shield up or a weapon still swings less), capes, tabards and beards trailing. The floating ones (the Astral Seer, the Archmage) glide instead, leaning into the way they go with their hems and capes streaming back. In Normal every mercenary takes a few steps between its strikes. **Band ×N** (HD, next to the switch) shows the whole band in ranks of five: 20 for a common, 15 uncommon, 10 rare, 5 epic, the legendary alone. Each copy is seeded on its own, so no two move in step, and the whole band costs the same draws as one.

**The other four pools in HD.** Valoria's, Sylvandale's, Ironforge's and Drak'mar's bands are built on one shared kit of people on the rig (the same bones, idle, march and strikes for every band; each band its own clothes, armour, face and weapons), at their people's build: men of a soldier's height, elves tall and slim with long pointed ears and long hair, dwarves short and broad with big heads and beards in locks that swing, orcs big and stooped with tusks and ragged ears. Each band climbs the ladder exactly as Voidrift's does (common matte and unlit, uncommon one accent and a small prop, rare steel trim and one looping effect, epic bigger with glowing detail, circling bits and embers, legendary the civilization's Hero Monument statue come to life with gold, a cape, its embers and sparks and a war cry), and strikes with a coil, a hang, a blow mid-swing and a follow-through.
* **Valoria**: 🌾 **Village Levy** (*Spear thrust*: a farm lad in a quilted jerkin, wrapped puttees and a wide straw hat, an ash spear with a leaf head), 🛡️ **Dawn Banner Shieldbearer** (*Strike*: mail, a kettle helm, the blue tabard with the dawn sun on his breast, a blue kite shield with the sun painted on, an arming sword, the Banner's pennant fluttering on a pole at his back), 🏮 **Lantern Friar** (*Bless*: a brown habit with a rope belt, a deep hood over a tonsure, a steel holy sun on a chain, a steel-bound censer swinging from his fist with its warm light breathing inside, a little red prayer book with steel corners), ☀️ **Dawnbreaker Paladin** (*Sunder*: white plate trimmed in gold, a blue surcoat and cape, a closed helm with a glowing visor slit and gilt wings, a sun-gem pulsing on his breastplate, a greatsword held before him in both hands, the left fist under the right on the long grip, runes of sunlight down the blade, six sun motes circling him; his arms move together so neither hand lets go), 👑 **Ser Aldric** (*Rally the host / Sunburst strike*: crowned and plumed in gold plate with a sun in relief on his breastplate, a blue cape, a halo of sun-rays turning behind his helm, his sword and the sunburst shield with its glowing heart, eight motes of sunlight circling him).
* **Sylvandale**: 🌿 **Grove Initiate** (*Thorn darts*: a plain green habit and deep hood, a sapling staff still putting out leaves), 🍃 **Leafward Warden** (*Glaive thrust*: a teal cloak over leaf-green leather, a leaf-bladed glaive, a round shield of living bark, a firefly lantern swinging at his hip), 🌙 **Moonwell Oracle** (*Foresight*: white robes, a silver collar of leaves and a silver circlet with a moonstone, a silver scrying bowl of moonlit water held before her, moon-motes drifting round her), 🌀 **Thousand-Year Bladesinger** (*Leaf whirlwind*: low and coiled, twin curved moon-blades with edges of light, a leaf tattoo glowing on his chest, eyes lit from within, seven leaves of light circling him; his strike spins him round once), 🏹 **The White Archer** (*Call the glades / Starfall arrow*: in gold over white, a gilt breastplate with a white leaf and a glowing gem, gold greaves and bracers, gilt leaf tassets and a band of glowing runes round her hem; two wings of golden leaves spread behind her shoulders, veined with light and breathing open and shut, and a halo of golden leaves turning behind her antlered gold circlet; a great gilt bow with glowing runes strung with light, an arrow of light in her drawing hand, a quiver of shining arrows, a teal cape, leaves of light circling her. Her Starfall arrow: she turns three-quarters to the target, raises the bow upright at arm's length, draws the string to her cheek with the elbow high, holds as she sights, then looses, the drawing hand flying back).
* **Ironforge**: ⛏️ **Tunnel Delver** (*Pick strike*: a leather apron and cap, a miner's pick of dull iron), 🛡️ **Hearthguard** (*Hammer blow*: mail, a round helm with a nasal bar, a clan-orange sash, a tower shield banded in orange, a short war hammer, a war horn at his belt), ⚒️ **Runesmith** (*Rune strike*: a blue coat and leather apron with steel bosses, steel plates at the knee, a rune hammer whose runes pulse, sparks looping round its anvil head), 🔥 **Ironbreaker in Steam Plate** (*Furnace slam*: massive plate, a furnace glowing behind a grate in his chest, vents on his back breathing glow and smoke, a great maul banded in bronze with glowing seams, embers circling him), 🔨 **The Bronze Hold-King** (*Mountain's call / Mountain's wrath*: bronze plate and a rust-red cape, a gold crown with a molten gem, a white beard bound in gold rings, his hammer raised high as the statue holds it, its head molten, sparks showering off it and motes circling him).
* **Drak'mar**: 🪵 **Grunt Raider** (*Club*: hides, a fur over one shoulder, bare feet in wrappings, a knotted club), 🦴 **Bonewall Brute** (*Bash*: red war paint across his eyes and chest, a bone-headed mace, a shield of hide with long bones lashed across it and a red rim, a skull totem on a pole at his back), 🩸 **Blood Shaman** (*Blood frenzy*: a bone mask with a red stripe and a crest of black feathers, iron rings on a cord, a totem staff with a spirit drum, blood-red wisps circling him), 💢 **Ravager Berserker** (*Whirlwind*: huge, scars glowing like coals, bone spikes on his pauldrons and horns lashed to his skull, burning eyes, a cleaver with a blood-lit edge in each fist, embers circling him; his strike spins him round once), 🪓 **Warlord of Drak'mar** (*Warcry of Drak'mar / Cleave*: dark stone armour under great gold pauldrons, gold ribs and a gold skull boss with burning eyes, gold plates over his war skirt, gold greaves, a gold helm with great bone horns and a crown of gold spikes, a tattered blood-red cape, the war banner of Drak'mar raised on his back (a blood-red banner with a gold skull on a bone crossbar, a horned skull on top), a great axe with a crescent head and a glowing edge, embers storming round him).

**Xal'Vorath in HD** (the Codex, and its emergence and cast on the login screen's flyover; mythic, *Idle / Emerge / Walk / Attack / Cast / Taunt / Death*). The one mythic creature, hand-built to read above every legendary boss. A void titan of black basalt and obsidian held together by its own gravity: a ribcage of glossy obsidian ribs curving round from a basalt spine of vertebrae with dorsal spikes, and inside it **the black hole**, a black sphere in a tilted accretion disc (white-hot at its inner edge to crimson at its rim, bands of matter sheared as they fall in, the side turning toward you brighter) with a ring of light hugging the shadow from every side, the disc's far side bent round it and a prismatic fringe; ten obsidian plates float round the hole. A faceted helm with an overhanging brow and a mask tapering to a point round one burning eye, under a broken crown of spires floating a hair over the helm; pauldrons of three floating basalt slabs a side with obsidian blades sweeping up and out; arms of rock boulders held apart at shoulder, elbow and wrist by small gravity orbs, with obsidian gauntlet plates and long glossy talons; a vortex of rock and obsidian spiralling down into a funnel of churning dust that reaches the ground. Round it lie heaved slabs of broken earth, a standing column broken off half way, a fallen one with its capital and a lintel with a carved frieze; rubble lifts off the ground and drops back, and two rock islands with columns, rocks, a drum, a capital, a frieze block and a shard orbit it. The basalt is layered and pitted, with thin cracks whose crimson light throbs outward from the core; the obsidian is black glass with a crimson depth, shell-like fracture ripples, a thin-film sheen and crimson light through its thin edges; the temple stone is cold, stained and cracked. Every floating part lives: the crown's spires and the pauldron slabs trail the head and shoulders on springs, the plates and slabs loosen as it breathes and surges, and now and then a shudder shakes them all apart and they snap back. Its curtain of light and embers are crimson and faint, with **no beam**: light falls into this one. Impacts kick up rings of dust; nothing glows on the ground at its feet. **Emerge** (played once, then it idles where it came out) brings it into the world through a rift: a hairline of white-hot light tears down the air and opens to a slit of black void with a razor rim and crimson light bleeding from it; its talons pierce through from the other side, hook round the edges and wrench them apart in two heaves, the tear riding its arms as they spread, while its eye burns in the dark beyond and the core gathers; it coils, then breaks the rift apart in a blinding flash, splinters of the rim and rock flung out, and lunges through roaring with its arms flung wide, the ground it stood on dragged through after it.

**Legendary landmarks in HD.** With Graphics set to HD, the four provinces' legendary landmarks and the Nethergarde Core are hand-built models, the same on the map and in the Codex, each two steps above the epic landmarks and in its region's character, alive through their parts (structures don't breathe), with a legendary aura in their own colour: a beam of light, a curtain of rising light round them and embers climbing through both (in place of the shared rarity kit):
* **The Cathedral of the Dawn Banner**: a Gothic cathedral of white stone in dressed courses under blue slates on an octagonal terrace with a balustrade and steps up the hill: nave, transepts and apse with stained-glass lancets and rose windows, flying buttresses and pinnacles, the portal under a gilt arch, twin west towers with belfries and slate spires, the crossing tower with its bell swinging in the belfry and the golden sun turning on its spire; the First King raising the Dawn Banner (it streams), royal banners on the terrace, fountains playing, hedges, topiary, rose beds and lanterns.
* **The Heart-Oak**: a twisting, buttressed trunk in furrowed bark, roots arching over the moss, boughs that fork into branches under a crown of leaf masses swaying in the wind with blossom in it, the hollow heart a glowing doorway with green fire, silver lanterns swinging from the boughs, the wardens' standing stones with silver bands, moss and lit runes, mushrooms (some glowing) and ferns, wisps and fireflies.
* **The Anvil of the Gods**: a plateau in two craggy tiers of basalt with molten veins and a dressed forge floor; a real anvil (feet, waist, heel, horn, a steel face with a white-hot bar and a glowing blade, gold runes down its flanks); the god's war hammer (gold bands, rune-lit head, wrapped haft) circling and dipping over it; forge-fire running in stone channels off the floor and over the edge in falls, brass braziers burning and smoking, rune pillars with gold caps, two dwarf guardians by the steps, gold ingots and a steaming quench trough, sparks rising off the anvil.
* **The Bloodfire Throne**: a red mesa in two tiers with fire in its cracks and steps cut up the front; an iron dais ringed with spikes; the titan's skull with burning sockets, brow, cheekbones and teeth, great horns banded in iron and tusk armrests; the first Warchief's bones in a horned helm, cuirass and spiked pauldrons, the double-bitted war-axe raised with its blood-runes glowing; war drums thrumming, totems of stacked skulls with fire bowls burning and smoking, tattered war banners, a palisade of bone stakes and bones strewn about.
* **The Nethergarde Core**: an obsidian dais in three steps with silver inlay and lit runes, six faceted monoliths leaning out with violet runes and silver caps, the heart a beating crystal with a white-hot core in a cage of black spikes, three silver rings with glowing beads turning on their own tilted axes, obsidian rocks with crystal veins and motes wheeling round it. Risen over the Archon's Empyrean, the same Eye floats and bobs high over the citadel, its beam of light running down into the citadel's crown, its monoliths wheeling wide below it, points down, its curtain of light round the Eye.

Every map NPC stands inside a ground ring sized to its model, so it is always visible: **bosses** get a red hazard ring with a purple outer ring; everything else gets its rarity colour (buffer kingdoms and terrain landmarks are epic purple, legendary landmarks gold, terrain-spawned territories common slate).

### Living models

**The land itself moves.** The sea and Valoria's river are one living water: turquoise shallows and deep blue depths, ripples drifting across, **foam waves rolling onto every shore**, a **current of streaks flowing south** down the river and glints twinkling on the open water. With **HD water** the surface itself moves: a long swell rolls in from the open ocean (choppier in the northern fjord) and the sun glints across the waves in a glittering path, the sky shows in the water more the flatter you look across it, the shallows turn clear enough to see the seabed, the foam breaks into lace round every shore and islet, white water runs where the river is shallowest, ripples run downstream with its current, and its silt fans out into the sea at its mouth; the waves calm down as you zoom out, so the overview stays a clean map. Up to three **stone bridges** cross the river where it runs between dry banks clear of castles and landmarks: one arch of Valoria's pale stone springing from abutments on both banks, a ring of voussoirs round the opening, a timber deck between coped parapets and a lamp at each corner. **Every region grows its own trees**: layered pines in the Deepwood and the highlands (evergreen: they keep their needles through autumn, though winter snow dusts them), broad oaks and pale birches in Valoria with weeping willows by its water, silver-barked crystal-leaf trees in Sylvandale, charred dead trees and burnt stumps in the badlands, ribbed cacti in the desert with palms at its edges; boulders lie in ones, twos and threes. Each tree sways from its foot as one, and in HD the gusts that roll across the grass roll through the trees too. Ironforge's **forge vents** glow and smoke in layers, and Drak'mar's **lava fissures** are jagged cracks that follow the ground, lava pulsing between charred rocks with smoke rising. With HD graphics everything on the land shows what it is made of up close: grooved bark (ribs on the cacti), leaves in clumps darker inside each crown and every tree its own shade, cracked stone with moss settled on top (sand on desert stone), coursed masonry on walls, towers and bridges, wood grain on timber, and a dark crust of cooling lava drifting over glowing cracks; the board itself becomes a carved walnut table with a gilt inlay round its top. **Armies wade**: a march crossing the sea or a lagoon keeps its figures waist-deep at most (never down on the seabed), each with a ring of foam on the water round it that pulses with its stride, while the march's route line rides the surface. Figures over an islet stand on it.

Every model in the realm is alive; nothing stands frozen, on the map or in the Royal Codex. In a roving spawn **every creature gets about on its own** while its camp stands still: **wolves, chimeras, goblins, skeletons, werewolves, lizardfolk, trolls, ogres and magma golems walk** a small loop of their ground with weight: the body dips on each footfall and sways over the supporting leg, leans in as it sets off and rocks back as it stops, the shoulders turn against the hips and the arms swing close to the body a beat behind the legs, while the head rides steady; tails, ears and manes trail every turn. Each stops for 3–5 seconds (its own length) to breathe, shift its weight and glance round in quick snaps, and once a stop does something of its own: beasts look round, sniff, shake themselves or scratch; people and other two-legged folk look round, look down, stretch or shift their weight; **rats, spiders and scorpions scurry**, **slimes hop** (squashing as they land), the **basilisk slithers**, **bats, harpies and the drake fly** on beating wings (a quick power stroke down and a slower lift, the body heaving with each beat, now and then gliding) round loops that swing out and in, climb and dive and bank into the turns, the **chimera beats its wings** as it prowls, and **wraiths, the lich and the aberration hover**, drifting, swaying and swelling (the lich's robes stir and its orb throbs, the aberration's eyes blink open); **bandits pace** their camp (the raiders ride their camels round), and **treasure** gently bobs. Camps whose creatures stand as a whole prowl, float or breathe, and every few seconds one looks round, sniffs, shakes itself or shifts its weight; in a site only the living things breathe and look round the same way (the treant, the yeti, the hunting lodge's stag, the caravan's camels, cattle and fleece herds, each to its own rhythm), while **structures never breathe**: caves, tents, fences, towers and stones stand still as a whole and live through their parts (below); the shipwreck rocks on the swell, the drowned lighthouse sweeps its green beam, the Burning Disc turns over its temple, the Giants' Forge smokes and its embers whirl up, and bosses keep their own idles (the Kraken circles, the sandworm surfaces, the dragon hovers). Structures come alive through their parts: **chimneys and fires smoke** (hunter lodges, taverns, fishing villages, goblin camps, caravans, smelters, the dragon's nostrils), **flags, sails and signs flutter**, **trees, crops and vines sway** (wheat ripples across the field in a wave), **runes, motes and shards orbit** (wizard towers, portals, fairy rings, mana geodes, the relic hoard), the lighthouse sweeps its light, the pyramid's capstone turns and **every glowing crystal, rune and window pulses**. Each copy moves on its own seeded rhythm (its own phase, tempo and size of motion), so two wolves or two mills never move in lockstep.

Zoom in and the land around them is alive, like the townsfolk in a city: **bandits patrol** their camp (river pirates and corsairs in sea blue, cultists in violet robes), **wolf packs prowl** around their den, **goblins and lizardfolk** scurry about, **trolls and ogres** lumber, **skeletons and liches** shamble, and **bats, harpies and drakes wheel** overhead on flapping wings, each at its own height and speed and never on a perfect circle; **vultures circle** over beast skeletons, pyramids, dragon nests, the Titan skeleton, the Obsidian Pyramid and the Valley of the Sleeping Kings. At resource nodes **workers chop, reap and mine** while a foreman walks the site, **cows and sheep graze** at cattle and fleece nodes, **villagers bustle** around fishing villages, taverns, hunter lodges, caravans and wizard towers, **soldiers patrol** the buffer realms (more at each stage, sea realms' on their jetty) with townsfolk among the houses, and **pilgrims walk up to the great landmarks** to admire them, bow or gaze up before wandering back (at a province's legendary landmark, five of that civilization's own people). Every figure keeps its own pace. The figures appear once you zoom in past the overview. When an army attacks, the camp **drops everything and fights**: bandits, monsters, workers and villagers gather on the side facing the attackers and trade blows until the battle ends (livestock keeps grazing), then go back to their routine.

### Dynamic World Spawns

* **Daily Resource Spawns**: sixteen gathering nodes spawn each calendar day (UTC) — two in each mortal province and two in each of the four buffer zones (`DAILY_RESOURCE_NODE_COUNT`), none in the Voidrift (§13 Gathering) (seeded by the date, so everyone sees the same nodes), each picked by rarity weight (chance per node in brackets). Every resource type has its own model:

  | Tier | Resource nodes | Yield |
  |---|---|---|
  | Common (19.5% each) | Pine Timber Camp · Grain Field · Stone Quarry | 9k lumber · 10k food · 7k stone |
  | Uncommon (10.9%) | Iron Vein Mine · Cattle Herd | 10k iron · 15k food |
  | Rare (5.5%) | Gold Mine · Royal Vineyard | 300 gold + 5k iron · 20k food + 100 gold |
  | Epic (2.3%) | Mithril Chasm · Mana Crystal Geode · Golden Fleece Sheepfold | 16k iron + 16k stone + 2 mithril · 14k stone + 60 mana · 24k food + 500 gold |
  | Legendary (0.8%) | Volcanic Dragonstone Core · King's Motherlode | 40k stone + 900 gold + 3 ruby · 1,200 gold + 20k iron + 20k stone + 2 diamond |

* **Wave Resource Spawns**: every **2 harvests** (`SYNC_INTERVAL_MINUTES × 2`, every **30 minutes** by default; every other roving window, below) a **fresh wave of 8 nodes** appears on top of the day's sixteen, **one in each mortal province and buffer zone** (`WAVE_RESOURCE_NODE_COUNT`), none in the Voidrift. Same nodes, weights and yields as the daily ones, seeded by the window (an admin respawn draws a fresh wave too), so everyone sees the same wave. A wave node **expires when its window ends** (its panel reads *Wave Node · gone at the next respawn*): workers already at work there finish and carry their whole load home (§13 Gathering), a march still on the road turns back.

* **Dynamic Roving Spawns**: with **every harvest** — `SYNC_INTERVAL_MINUTES`, so every **15 minutes** by default and every 30 minutes with `SYNC_INTERVAL_MINUTES=30` (windows are aligned to the clock and seed the roll; each encounter expires when its window ends) **each of the four buffer zones** rolls its own encounters, placed inside that zone's strip between the provinces. Crews afloat — the **River Pirate Raft** and the **Corsair Plunderer Skiff** — rove only on the **Sunken Fjord's open water** (its deep middle, |x| ≤ 7 from z −33 to −62), floating there with no islet; the other three zones never roll them, so a boat never sits on dry land. A land mob or bandit pack whose spot falls on water still stands on an islet raised under it, and so does a resource node or treasure whose spot or work circle is mostly water (sea bosses and crews afloat never get one); under a node or treasure the islet is wide enough for the whole work circle, so gatherers stand on it rather than in the sea. The frontier holds **at least 4 and about 12 on average** (up to 20). Per buffer zone:

  | Slot | Roll (per buffer zone) |
  |---|---|
  | 1st | always there: a common |
  | 2nd | 80% chance; uncommon half the time, else common |
  | 3rd | 50% chance; uncommon for sure if the 2nd wasn't uncommon (or never came), 10% if it was |
  | Rare | 50% chance of one, rolled on its own |
  | Epic | 25% chance of one, rolled on its own (the legendary Relic Hoard can also land here, 1-in-4 when treasure is rolled) |

  On average a zone rolls 1.58 commons, 0.72 uncommons, 0.5 rares and 0.25 epics a window.

  Each slot then picks its category (50% mob / 35% bandit / 15% treasure) and a template of that tier. Expected appearances of each entry per window across the whole frontier in brackets:
  - **Monsters (20)**: common Green Slime Swarm, Forest Dire Wolf Pack, Cave Bat Cloud, Giant Sewer Rat Pack, Venomweb Spider Nest (63% each) · uncommon Goblin Scouting Party, Skeleton Skirmishers, Dune Scorpion Brood, Stormcrag Harpy Flock, Bogmire Lizardfolk Band (29%) · rare Mountain Troll Vanguard, Shadow Chimera, Moonfang Werewolf Den, Ogre Brute Camp, Wailing Wraith Coven (20%) · epic Emberwing Drake, Void Rift Aberration, Molten Magma Elemental, Stone-Gaze Basilisk, Ancient Lich Circle (10%).
  - **Bandits (12)**: common Highwayman Outlaws, Rogue Pickpockets, Poacher Gang (74%) · uncommon Blackwood Smugglers, Desert Sand Raiders (46%), River Pirate Raft (8.4%, afloat) · rare Shadow Syndicate Cadre, Ashen Cultist Raiders (32%), Corsair Plunderer Skiff (5.8%, afloat) · epic Dread Mercenary Legion, Warlord's Iron Horde, Blackpowder Saboteurs (12%).
  - **Treasure (9)**: common Weathered Wanderer Stash, Abandoned Supply Cart (47%) · uncommon Smuggler's Iron Lockbox, Dwarven Ore Cache (22%) · rare Gilded Royal Strongbox, Moonsilver Elven Reliquary (15%) · epic Sunken Corsair Treasure Chest, Dragon's Gold Hoard (6.4%) · legendary Ancient Nethergarde Relic Hoard (2.1%).
  - Every encounter has its **own 3D model** under a ground ring coloured by rarity. Mobs and bandits are flagged hostile.
* **🛡️ Pack gear (hidden)**: every mob and bandit pack rolls its gear when it spawns (seeded by the spawn, so everyone agrees): **🪵 Ragged** 60% (as its stats read), **🗡️ Armed** 30% (+25% HP and DEF, +10% ATK; +30% loot) or **🛡️ War-geared** 10% (+50% HP and DEF, +25% ATK; +75% loot). The map and the march form don't show it (*Gear: ?*); **a scout report does** (its stats and spoils already include it), and the battle log names it when the fight opens (`🛡️ They are war-geared: …`). Scouting a pack before attacking pays.
* **Encounter timer**: the rose 💀 encounter countdown in the top bar (next to the ☀️ / 🌙 day & season button, which is the harvest timer) shows when the roving encounters next respawn. The map swaps them in place for everyone at the end of each window (:00, :15, :30 and :45 by default) without reloading. Instant Harvest does **not** respawn them. Roving spawns that land on open water stand on a small sand islet, and trees under a spawn are cleared.
* **Spacing**: daily and wave nodes keep at least 14 units, and roving spawns at least 9 units, from every castle, persistent NPC and each other (a wave also keeps clear of its window's roving encounters). When no spot that far is free, the spot farthest from its nearest neighbour is used, so spawns never pile onto each other.

### World Disasters

Server: [disasterRules.ts](server/src/disasterRules.ts) (rules, schedule) · [disasters.ts](server/src/disasters.ts) (table, notices) · Client: [disasters.ts](src/utils/disasters.ts), [DisasterChip.tsx](src/components/ui/DisasterChip.tsx), [DisasterWeather.tsx](src/components/3d/DisasterWeather.tsx)

**1–3 disasters strike the realm each week**, never two on one day, each for **4 hours**. Every civilization **copes** with one of them (half the effect unless noted) and **suffers** from another (double unless noted), Nethergarde included.

**When.** Each realm day rolls for one, seeded by the date (a restart rolls the same):
* The chance starts at **35%**. Each day without a disaster adds **+15%** to the next day's chance (it carries over); a disaster day halves it (the next day rolls at 17.5%, then climbs again).
* **None by Saturday makes Saturday's certain**; after **3 in a week** none comes until the week turns (Sunday 00:00 UTC, as the other weekly resets).
* A disaster starts between **06:00 and 22:00 realm time** (`REALM_TIMEZONE`, §12; a 22:00 start ends at 02:00), on the quarter hour, **weighted toward the morning** (06:00–12:00 gets 60% of the rolls for its 6 hours, when people commit). Over a year this comes to about 2.5 a week.

**Which.** The kinds come from a **shuffled bag**: all five strike once, in a random order, before any strikes again, then the bag is shuffled anew. A bag not finished by the end of the week carries over to the next, so with at most 3 a week a round of five usually takes about two weeks. The same kind never strikes twice in a row, across two bags included. A disaster an admin calls (§11) doesn't count toward the bag.

**The herald warns an hour before** (*Dark clouds gather over the realm…*), and announces the disaster when it strikes and when it passes (§14). From the warning to the end, a **countdown chip** stands in the top bar (left of the day & season button): dashed while it is coming, solid while it lasts, with ▲ or ▼ on it when your civilization copes or suffers. Tap it for what the disaster does, who copes and who suffers, and how it looks.

| Disaster | For 4 hours | ▲ Copes | ▼ Suffers | How it looks |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 🌾 **Great Blight** | the harvest pool pays nothing (buildings, the workforce and the gem drop still pay) | Dwarves (mushroom caverns, stone granaries): half their pool share | Humans (grain-fed hosts): no pool share, and their buildings pay half | the green land turns brown-grey, crops and grass wilt, grey spores drift |
| 🌪️ **Tempest Roads** | every army that sets off loses **3–8%** of its marchers on the road (seeded per march, rounded down); a mercenary band loses the same share of its strength | Elves (sure-footed rangers): 1.5–4% | Dwarves (heavy armour on mud roads): 6–16% | wind streaks whip across the land, dust rolls along, the light dims |
| 🩸 **Blood Moon** | mobs, bandits and bosses fight with **+30% HP, ATK and DEF** | Orcs love it: their soldiers **+10% ATK** | the glades fear it: Elven soldiers **−10% ATK** | a huge red moon in the sky (when the camera looks up at the horizon), red motes rising, a red-tinted night and a rust tinge by day |
| ☠️ **Wasting Plague** | **1%** of every kingdom's civilians die at each harvest | Nethergarde: immune | Orcs (crowded war camps): 2% | a sickly green miasma creeps low over the land and the cities |
| 🥀 **Barren Spell** | births **×0.5** | Humans (the Dawn Banner's blessing): ×0.75 | Nethergarde: the Void Well returns only half its fallen | the colour drains from the land, ash-grey petals fall, and city streets empty (about a third of the grown-ups stay out, no children play) |

* **Battles feel the Blood Moon round by round.** Only rounds fought inside its 4 hours are changed: a battle already under way feels it from its first round inside the window and stops feeling it when the window ends. The battle log notes when the moon rises over the battle and when it sets. NPC realms (frontier gates, buffer realms, the Voidrift's NPC kingdoms) and rulers' castles are not touched; the Orc and Elf ATK changes apply in every battle round fought under it.
* **Tempest losses** come off the march as it sets off (a rally when it launches, each member army on its own). The lost marchers die (Nethergarde's soldiers go to the Void Well); the rations already paid for them are gone; the march keeps how many set off as its starting count. Your population ledger has a 🌪️ line for each.
* **Plague deaths** fall on civilians only, on top of natural deaths, and show in the harvest's population ledger line (☠️). The **Blight** shows in the harvest ledger line (🌾 pool ×0).
* What each disaster did (marchers lost on the roads, civilians to the plague, births lost, resources the blighted pool withheld, rounds and soldiers fallen under the red moon) is tallied by civilization for its closing notice. Every warning, start and end is also a **realm event** (`disaster_warning`, `disaster_start`, `disaster_end`) with the disaster, its times, the civilizations that coped and suffered, and (at the end) the tally.
* Admins can call a disaster now or in 30 minutes, or end the one in force (§11).

### Guide Book & Royal Codex

☰ menu → **📚 Royal Codex** opens one window with seven top tabs; the catalog ones are split into smaller tabs, sharing one rotatable 3D preview pane:

| Top tab | Holds |
|---|---|
| 📖 **Game Guide** | The Guide Book (below) |
| 🐺 **Creatures** | Monsters · Bosses · Bandits · Mercenaries · The Armory · Champions · Folk & Beasts · Void Gate |
| 🗺️ **World** | Landmarks · Frontier Gates · Free Realms · Wilds · Resources · Treasures |
| 🏰 **Structures** | Castles · Buildings · Improvements |
| 🎵 **Music & Sounds** | Soundtracks · Game sounds |
| 📜 **Chronicles** | The weekly stories (below) |
| 🤺 **Sparring Grounds** | Two units of your choice in a 6-round fight, and a spell gallery of every Battle Arts look (below) |

**📖 Game Guide** (the Guide Book) — the rules of the game, short and with the numbers, in searchable chapters grouped as *The Realm* (the realm at a glance, rarity & the living world, landmarks & their rotation, contribution XP, the Scriptorium (§17), omens (§11), day, night & seasons, world disasters, the Weekly Chronicle), *Your City* (buildings & districts, the castle, resources & the harvest, improvements, Royal Proclamations), *Civilizations* (one chapter per civilization: people, soldier, march and load numbers, and its Unique Trait with every buff and debuff and the legend behind it, §9) and *War* (armies & marches, battles, walls & plunder, scouting, gathering, treasure & spoils, rallies, bosses & overseers, Battle Arts, mercenaries, champions, free realms, frontier gates, milestone titles). Each chapter has a few 3D models to switch between: castles at each stage (world map or city town hall), a single building at a given level (huts, a manor, a ★★★★ keep, a red-star keep, in any civilization's style), improvements, a Festival on the plaza (Normal and HD), each world disaster's look over a farm plot (its weather, the Blood Moon's red moon, its light), resource nodes, bosses, and soldier squads (one civilization at ease, or two civilizations fighting).

**📜 Chronicles** — the realm's saga: every weekly story the Crown has published (§15 *Weekly Chronicle*), newest first, each a numbered chapter (*Chapter 3 · the week of 18 – 24 October*). The open chapter is drawn whole: a dark header with the chapter, its title and the crests of the peoples whose rulers it names; a strip of the week's happenings (up to 7, each its emblem in a ring with a short label: *First void seat*, *Solo kill*, *Blood Moon*…); then the story on parchment with its marks (the square insignia beside the opening paragraph, icons in the text, framed callouts, ornament dividers) and every named ruler as a chip with their people's crest (crown for Valoria, leaf for Sylvandale, hammer for Ironforge, shield for Drak'mar, void spiral for Nethergarde). Older chapters are a row each and unroll on a click. Anyone can read it, signed in or not. The 📖 banner of a new story opens the Codex straight on this tab.

**🤺 Sparring Grounds** — pick two units with the pickers (an attacker and a defender) and press **Spar**: the server fights them **6 rounds on the real battle engine** (`GET /sparring/fight`, the same `stepBattleRound` as every battle; §13 *Battle Arts*) and the tab plays it back round by round. The roster is every world boss as it fights (full HP, its own kit), every **mob and bandit pack** as it fights (its kit and stats, at its full size at its spawn's rarity; 20 packs strong against a boss) and six **drill bands**, one per rarity (Militia Captain common, Ember Adept uncommon, Flame Warden rare, Siege Marshal epic, Dawn Paladin legendary, Eclipse Shade mythic): 1,000 identical plain soldiers each (HP 10, ATK 2, DEF 20; 20,000 when they face a boss; against a pack, an even match: as many soldiers as make soldiers² × HP × blow after armour the same on both sides), each kit the one below it plus the slots its rarity adds, so the only difference between them is their Battle Arts. Both units' kits are listed; the arena shows plain stand-ins (a block of soldiers per band or pack, one great shape per boss, amber attacking, violet defending) that thin out as they fall and lunge as each round lands, with every round's numbers, casts and status ticks rising over them on the same floating text as the map's battles, and every cast, Finale, Instinct and status played on its look (§13 *Battle Arts*), the stand-ins flashing white and flinching, jolting or glowing as each lands; each round is listed under it with its casts and ticks, and the end says who stands stronger. **Spar again** fights a new bout (another roll). Under the arena, the **✨ Spell gallery** casts any one Art, Finale, Instinct or status (pick it, press **Cast**) between the stand-ins on its own, with its six stages listed and each lit as it plays (charge → core → trail → burst → reaction → aftermath) and its sound named.

**The catalog tabs** — an in-game encyclopedia anyone can browse, with a 3D preview of each entry:
* **Monsters** (the 20 roving mobs), **Bosses** (the 12 world bosses, each striking every few seconds), **Bandits**, **Treasures**, **Resources**: rarity, where they appear, spawn chance and yields. Bosses, monsters and bandits list their Battle Arts as one fact per Instinct, Art and Finale (§13 *Battle Arts*).
* **Teasers** are not in the game yet: every one carries the same 🔒 **Coming soon** mark, under its name in the list, beside its title and on a ribbon over its preview (*Coming soon · not in the realm yet*): **Vargo Blackfang, the Bandit King** (legendary bandit, Bandits tab: the King on his loot mound with four hooded henchmen and a war hound; a **Normal / ✨ HD** switch shows his hand-built model with *Idle / War cry / Attack* buttons, see *Legendary creatures in HD*) never spawns for now. His disaster, **Blackfang's Raid**, is a locked sixth option with the same ribbon on the Guide Book's World Disasters chapter: the same farm plot as the other disasters under a torchlit dusk, with ten of his hooded looters in blood red, three in the yard and seven running round outside the fence, with sacks on their backs or a bag in each fist. The chapter gives it a short section with no rules or numbers. It is not in the game yet either. **Xal'Vorath, the Devouring Singularity** (Bosses tab, the only **mythical** creature: a void titan of floating rock plates round a black core in a blazing accretion ring, with shattered land and temple ruins orbiting it over a glowing gravity well) is the Overseers' horror: also not in the game yet. A **Normal / ✨ HD** switch on its entry shows it in high graphics, with buttons for its seven actions (idle, emerging, walking, attacking, casting spells, taunt, death; see *Xal'Vorath in HD*). **The Void Gate** (Creatures → **Void Gate** tab, at its head, **mythical**; not in the Landmarks tab until it stands on the map) is a mythic gate coming to the Nethergarde Voidrift, shown ahead of its gameplay with no numbers yet: a tall arch of floating obsidian stones veined crimson, its crown broken open (two stones drift free, held by crackling arcs), round a swirling vortex that sinks into the dark on both faces, a prismatic edge along the arch's inner face, void shards circling it, chips torn off the rim drawn down the funnel and embers rising off the stone; no beam, ground disc or rune ring. Normal and **✨ HD** are the same model (layout, colours and motion from one seed): HD adds bevelled glassy obsidian with finer veins, a second current in the vortex, flakes peeling off the arch, more chips and embers and forked arcs. It replaces the look of the scattered *Unstable Void Gate* wild sight only once the gate is in the game. **The Warden's Oathbound** (Mercenaries tab, legendary, after the five pools) is the familiar of the gate's Warden, to be won by beating the gate's last level, the same for every civilization: two void koi curled round each other as a yin-yang (each a teardrop comma of the taijitu, its round head tucked into its half, its body sweeping round the rim) that floats at head height and turns slowly, one obsidian and one pale as moonlit pearl, each head carrying the yin-yang's dot in the other koi's colour in a lit rim, with broad pectoral and pelvic fins, a dorsal sail, a butterfly tail with lit tips, whiskers and two small lit horns, each trailing a streamer of light round the rim (crimson behind the dark one, gold behind the pale one), round a taijitu pearl turning the other way at the heart, void shards circling them all. Normal and **✨ HD** draw the same koi from one part list and one seven-bone chain; HD adds scaled skin, glassy fins and lit seams along the back and belly, as a rigged hero with *Idle / March / Oath / Twin strike* buttons (embers and sparks, no beam): at rest the ring drifts and turns at a changing pace, each body rippling with a slow swimming wave, and now and then the disc rolls half over to show its other face; on the **March** the koi leave their ring and swim slowly forward braided round each other like a twisted loaf, crossing over and under down their whole length without touching, the pearl leading; in **Twin strike** (its battle) they glide a wide figure of eight before the foe, half a loop apart, each in its own lobe, parting round the pearl at its heart (one over it, one under) so they swim out of it with the pearl in sight, in a soft bloom of light; each koi rolls smoothly half over as it swims through the heart, so its curling body bends the way each lobe turns; in the **Oath** the yin-yang rises and stands upright facing the foe like a sigil, the koi spin it twice round the pearl, and the pearl swells and flares at its heart before it all settles back. Every body follows the curve its head swam, so they flow rather than snap; its rules are not settled yet.
* **Landmarks**: each region's six epic landmarks (the one standing this week marked *🏛️ standing now*; a **Built / 🏗️ Rising / 🏚️ Crumbling** switch with a slider through the rotation day shows how each looks on its rotation day), each province's legendary landmark, plus the Nethergarde Core. With an Archon in the realm the Core's preview shows it risen, floating over the Empyrean.
* **Frontier Gates**: the four gates that seal the mortal provinces (Imperial High Gate, Tusked War Pass, Moonwood Arch, Anvilhold Deepgate).
* **Free Realms**: the NPC kingdoms — every buffer realm of each zone's pool of 3 (its garrison's Battle Arts listed stage by stage for each civilization that may hold it; a frontier gate lists its rare kit), with a **Common / Uncommon / Rare / Epic** switch to step through how it grows over its cycle and a **🏗️ Building day** toggle (with a slider through the day) to see a stage being built up, and the NPC void kingdom (its Warden) of each Voidrift slot, with its City Hall, garrison, walls and conquest loot, previewed as the Nethergarde castle it is drawn as.
* **Wilds**: the 16 wild sights that dot the map as scenery, about one per four kingdoms, each placed where the land suits it: sea monsters and siren rocks offshore, lighthouses and fishing villages on the coasts, treants, fairy rings and hunting lodges in the woods, yeti caves on the snowy peaks, sandworms, pyramids, caravans and beast skeletons in the deserts and steppes, goblin camps, wizard towers, crossroads inns and void portals. Each entry shows where it is found and its other names on the map. They hold no reward and can't be fought (the creatures among them are only marked hostile).
* **Mercenaries**: every civilization has its own mercenary pool, one band per rarity, common to legendary, slot for slot alike across the five (each band has a counterpart in every other pool). A band is one whole march, and the rarity sets how many walk in it: **20** common, **15** uncommon, **10** rare, **5** epic, the legendary **alone** (the *Band* fact). Each entry lists its power, the march slots it needs, its hire price, upkeep, healing and camp stock, and its Battle Arts, one fact per Instinct, Art and Finale (§13 *Mercenaries*). **Valoria**: 🌾 Village Levy, 🛡️ Dawn Banner Shieldbearer, 🏮 Lantern Friar, ☀️ Dawnbreaker Paladin, 👑 Ser Aldric, Champion of the Dawn Banner. **Sylvandale**: 🌿 Grove Initiate, 🍃 Leafward Warden, 🌙 Moonwell Oracle, 🌀 Thousand-Year Bladesinger, 🏹 The White Archer. **Ironforge**: ⛏️ Tunnel Delver, 🛡️ Hearthguard, ⚒️ Runesmith, 🔥 Ironbreaker in Steam Plate, 🔨 The Bronze Hold-King. **Drak'mar**: 🪵 Grunt Raider, 🦴 Bonewall Brute, 🩸 Blood Shaman, 💢 Ravager Berserker, 🪓 Warlord of Drak'mar. **Nethergarde**: 🕯️ Rift Acolyte, 🛡️ Riftguard Sentinel, 🔭 Astral Seer, 🌑 Eclipse Reaver, 🧙 Archmage of the Voidrift (in Normal hovering over a turning rune circle). Each civilization's legendary is its Hero Monument statue come to life.
