Each idles, strikes or casts, and takes a few marching steps every few seconds, with its tier's effects from the rarity ladder (none for common and uncommon, glints for rare, an aura and motes for epic, the full legendary kit for the legendary). All 25 have an HD version with their actions as buttons (*Mercenaries in HD*, *Every mercenary marches*, *The other four pools in HD*, *Legendary creatures in HD*) and a **Band ×N** button showing the whole band marching; the legendaries get embers and their own sparks instead of the beam, as every creature does.
* **The Armory**: one entry per set, five civilizations × uncommon, rare and epic (§13 *The Armory*). Its preview shows the civilization's soldiers wearing it, one walking, one fighting and one standing guard, with a soldier in standard gear behind them to compare, in Normal and ✨ HD. Each entry lists what it adds, its power, the cost of forging 100, its upkeep, the City Hall it is forged from, how it looks and where its steel counts less.
* **Folk & Beasts**: the realm's townsfolk and animals. One entry per civilization shows its folk as they live in a city: a villager and a soldier walking up and down, a farmer at work and a villager holding the light they carry after dark (Valoria's brass hand lantern, Sylvandale's moon-wisp, Ironforge's miner's lamp and iron lantern, Drak'mar's pitch torch, Nethergarde's void orb). One entry per civilization's **scout mount** (Courser, Moonstag, War Ram, Warg, Voidmare, §13 *Scouting*) shows one ridden up and down by a scout of its civilization and one standing, its rider dressed as the people of another. One entry per animal (sheep, cow, pig, chicken, deer, horse, rabbit, swan, goat, boar, pony, goose, wolf, lizard, crow, griffin, spirit stag, cat, owl) shows one grazing, pecking or perched beside one walking (the crow and the griffin circling overhead), with where it is found: the pens and yards of each civilization's cities (the griffins of the Archon's Griffin Roosts), and round the map (herds at cattle and fleece nodes, wolves at their lairs, crows and vultures wheeling over lairs and bones). **Battlefield remains** shows what a routed army leaves behind (§13 *Defeat*), in your civilization's colours, going round its three stages (burning, smouldering, cold) every 5 seconds. The same figures patrol the map's camps, guard its gates and realms, visit its landmarks and march to war. All have a **Normal / ✨ HD** switch (below).
* **Void Gate** (🔒 *Coming soon*, the whole tab): the gate and its 30-level climb, shown ahead of their gameplay with no numbers yet. It opens on **the Void Gate** itself, then lists one **family of creatures per stage of five levels**, each "the *boss*'s brood": its preview shows the family's sizes **side by side**, smallest to biggest, and a card for each level below it (name, level, rarity and what it looks like); pick a card to see that level's creature alone (with **Pack ×N** to see its whole pack), or *All sizes* to go back. A family is **one creature that grows up the rarity ladder**: its first four levels are packs of it at four sizes (common → uncommon → rare → epic: matte, then a violet accent and a small prop, then a glow that pulses, then a bigger body with lit detail and bits circling it and its embers), and its fifth is the family grown up into **one big boss** (legendary: a crown of crystal, a burning core or edge, sparks and embers; no beam, ground disc or rune ring). Every size is a **rigged creature, as the mercenaries are**: its own bones, a seeded idle (breath, glances, events), a walk and a strike, and the boss a roar; in **✨ HD** the action buttons play them (*Idle*, *Walk*, its strike, the boss's roar). Normal is the same design in plain parts, HD adds real stone, fur, chitin, cloth and metal and finer detail. Stage 1, **the Stone Giant's brood**: one rock titan, all shoulders and fists, its small head sunk between two boulders, short thick legs and forearms bigger than its upper arms; it lumbers heavily and smashes the ground with both fists: Pebblings (knee-high grey rock, dark pits for eyes) → Rubblekin (a violet crystal on the back, pebbles studding the fists) → Rockling Brutes (void light through the cracks of chest and eyes) → Boulderbacks (moss and lit crystals on the shoulders, stones circling) → **the Stone Giant** (a crown of crystal, a burning core in its chest; *Mountain's roar*). Stage 2, **the Hollow Wraith's brood**, floats: hunched in a tattered shroud of void-grey edged pale as smoke, its peaked hood a hollow of void, its shroud and ribbons of smoke rippling behind and dissolving into dark smoke below, its claws held out to grasp a soul: Void Wisps (all hood and tail, two dark eyes) → Shade Wisps (a violet sash and a cracked bell) → Wraithlings (bony hands, eyes and a heart-light pulsing, an old chain trailing) → Void Wraiths (runes lit round the hem, souls circling) → **the Hollow Wraith** (a crown of shards, long claws, a hollow chest burning behind its ribs, soul lanterns circling; *Wail*). Stage 3, **the Crystal Broodmother's brood**: a spider on eight jointed legs that walks four at a time and rears to strike with its fangs: Shardlings (one dull grey shard) → Shard Spiders (a violet cluster and a crystal egg) → Crystal Weavers (crystals and eyes lit and pulsing) → Prism Stalkers (long-legged, lit crystal at every knee, shards circling) → **the Crystal Broodmother** (her abdomen a burning geode, a crown of crystal spines; *Shriek*). Stage 4, **the Void Lion's pride**: a lion with a mane on its own spring, flicking ears and a five-bone tail, prowling in a diagonal gait and pouncing to rake with both forepaws: Void Cubs (big head, big paws, faint spots, no mane) → Young Void Lions (the first ruff, violet at its tips) → Maned Void Lions (a full dark mane, glowing eyes, a rift of light down the spine) → Crystal-Maned Lions (the mane set with lit crystal, motes circling) → **the Void Lion** (a mane of burning shards, a crown of them over its brow, the rift torn open along both flanks; *Roar*). Stage 5, **the Obsidian Knight's brood**: people of the gate's order on the mercenaries' bones, who stand in a guard stance, march like soldiers and strike with a shielded cut: Obsidian Squires (dull black plate, open helms, short swords) → Obsidian Men-at-Arms (violet heater shields, a pennant) → Obsidian Knights-Errant (steel trim, longswords, a closed helm whose visor glows) → Obsidian Champions (caped and horned, runes lit down the plate, shards circling) → **the Obsidian Knight** (a horned crown and long cape, a greatsword in both hands with a burning edge, brought down in a two-handed *Sunder*; *Oath of the Gate*). Levels 26–29, **the Gate Sentinels**, have no boss: one creature over four levels, **epic, epic, legendary, legendary**: floating suits of obsidian plate with no one inside, held together by void light: the helm floats over a glowing ring, the pauldrons over glowing orbs, a ring of light at the waist over a skirt of plates where legs would be; one crimson eye in each helm, carved runes glowing down the chest, a tattered crimson tabard, a halo of blades turning behind the helm, a halberd with a burning edge in the right gauntlet (a *Halberd chop*) and shards circling; from level 28 crowned in crimson shards, wings of floating blades spread behind them. Last comes **the Gate Warden** (level 30, **mythical**, on the dark violet mythic stage): a titan in black-crimson plate hovering over pooling crimson smoke, prismatic seams down its robe and a hem of crimson light, masked with one crimson slit and a prismatic third eye, horns sweeping up from the mask, a great key-staff with a crimson gem in its ring, a crown of shards turning over its head and, at its back, a gate of its own turning slowly: a ring of obsidian slabs round a disc of void, prismatic rims and arms of light spiralling in (*Turn the key*, *Command of the Gate*); in ✨ HD the mythic aura's crimson curtain and embers stand round it, no beam. The **Oathbound's two koi** each swim a wide ring above its head as its guard, the same koi as the Mercenaries tab's entry (their own model, Normal or ✨ HD): when the Warden falls they swear themselves to the ruler who beat it and become **the Warden's Oathbound**.
* **Castles**: all 5 stages for each civilization, as the world-map castle or the city town hall, each marked with its rarity (Keep common … Wonder legendary; the Wonder can preview prestige ★1–★4), with the civilization's Unique Trait (§9); both views (the world-map castle and the city town hall) have a **Normal / ✨ HD** switch showing their HD versions (§6 *HD castles*, *HD Wonders*, the Wonder's prestige in HD too); plus **The Empyrean** (mythical), the Archon of the Realm's castle, with a **Normal / ✨ HD** switch: HD shows the floating citadel in high graphics (veined marble, polished gold, lacquered crimson spires and fluttering banners over molten-veined rock, inside its mythic beam, curtain of light and rising embers).
* Every preview shows the entry's rarity effects as on the map.
* **Normal / ✨ HD**: every entry with an HD version has this switch at the top left of its preview, opening on your Graphics setting (☰ → ⚙️ Game settings), so you can flip between the two. Every monster, bandit band, wild sight, resource, treasure, landmark, frontier gate and free realm has one: the same model with rounder shapes, real materials (fur, hide, skin, cloth, leather, wood, bone, stone, leaves, worn metal, gems, glowing lava cracks), soft shading where parts meet the ground, a relaxed pose that is never mirrored, a real face (eyes under lids, a brow, nose, mouth and ears; skulls for the dead, snouted faces for beast-folk), closed fists that grip their weapons (swords with a guard and a pointed blade, daggers, spears with a leaf point, an iron-bound club, crossbows with a bolt laid in them), tails that curl and bend and ripple as the creature moves, real bones and skulls among the props (and the commons' camps in full: a slime wet and wobbling with bubbles rising in it, bats with scalloped wings of skin, rats with whiskers by a broken drain, spiders with jointed legs under a web of silk strands, pickpockets in true hoods with drawstring purses, poachers with a fox in a barred crate, pelts drying and a set trap; and the uncommons' accents in real materials: skeletons' rib cages and teal round shields, scorpions' plated backs with the stinger on a segmented tail, harpies' feathered wings and their nest on top of the crag, lizardfolk's long jaws and crested shaman, smugglers' hooded lantern and hooped barrels, raiders' camels and curved scimitars, the river pirates' lashed raft and chain across the water; and the rares' real metal and trim: the chimera's lion ruff, membrane wings and a serpent tail ending in a hissing head, werewolves' muzzles, claws and broken silver chains, ogres' riveted iron belly-plates and spiked clubs over a turning roast, wraiths' tattered shrouds, reaching sleeves and swinging grave-chains, cultists' gold-trimmed robes with candles held in pale hands, the corsairs' brass-fitted skiff and lantern, the syndicate's steel bracers and a dagger in each fist; and the epics, richest of all, with glowing detail and parts that trail and swing: the drake's dragon head breathing a tongue of flame, wings whose finger bones smoulder with fire and a barbed tail, its eggs glowing through cracked shells; the aberration's wet void flesh laced with glowing veins, lidded eyes and hanging tentacles; the basilisk's fanged head, scaled belly and splayed legs among adventurers turned wholly to stone; the lich's real skull with green fire in its sockets, gold crown and clawed staff, skulls on its shoulders and bony hands, one holding a soul-flame; the warlord's rune-lit barding, iron-bound tusks and glowing great axe, and his guards' shields and spears; the mercenaries' full plate with plumes and capes, tower shields and halberds; the saboteurs' brass goggles and bomb bandoliers; and the resource nodes and treasure caches the same way: layered pines and an axe bitten into the stump at the timber camp, wheat in stalks each with its ear, bending as one in the wind, by a scarecrow hung on its post, the quarry's cut benches and a block slung from its crane, the iron mine's rails, wheeled ore cart and brass lantern, cows with horns, hooves, udders and the lead cow's bell, the gold mine's iron-bracketed timbers, quartz round its seams and gold nuggets heaped in the cart, the vineyard's dry-stone walls, trellis wires, hanging bunches of grapes and iron-hooped barrels, the mithril crater's meteorite laced with glowing veins, the mana geode's split rock crust round a crystal heart, the golden fleece in curls of wool and beaten gold (the ram with gold horns); chests with domed lids, banded straps and handles, a padlock, studs or a jewel as their rarity climbs, coins and gems lying on open hoards, the supply cart's spoked wheels and battened crates, the sea chest's anchor, pearls, starfish and swaying weed, the dragon hoard's goblets, a sword driven into the gold and embers still glowing in the skull's sockets; and the wild sights: the kraken's tentacles as smooth limbs with suckers along them under a wet mantle, lidded eyes and a beak, the siren with a face, arms on the rock, long red hair and a scaled tail ending in a fin by a pearl in its clam, the lighthouse open round its lamp with a railed gallery and two beams of light sweeping round, the fishing hamlet's thatched plank huts, pier bollards, a rounded boat and nets drying, the treant's furrowed bark, knotted face and beard of moss, the fairy ring's spotted caps with pale gills, the hunting lodge's log ends, antlered skull over the door and a stag with branching antlers, the yeti with a blue face and claws under a rock arch hung with icicles, the sandworm's ringed throat and smooth coils, the pyramid in stepped courses with its doorway and a glyph-cut obelisk, the caravan's tents on poles and guy ropes with camels in saddle bags and tassels, the goblin camp's pole of skulls, the colossus's curved ribs and real skull, the wizard tower's stone courses, window arches and ivy, the timbered inn with shutters, mullions, a hanging sign and a lantern, and the void gate's lit runes round a swirl of light; and the epic landmarks, with glowing detail and life of their own: the wreck's strakes, stove-in hull, rigging and lit stern windows with gulls gliding over it, anemones waving glowing tentacles in the coral trench, the sunken spire's lit runes and turning capstone, the siren with her tail curled over the rock and a pearl in her hand, the drowned lighthouse's railed gallery and two beams of light sweeping round, the leviathan's jawbones lit with sailors' wards and hung with charms on a chain; the oasis palms' leafy fronds, reeds and lotus round the pool and a lantern by the tent, the obsidian pyramid's sealed portal and shards circling its apex, the titan's vertebrae and real skull with embers in its sockets under circling vultures, the canyon's rope bridge and gold vein, the sun temple's capitals, gilt frieze and a face on its turning disc, the sleeping kings' carved faces with lit eyes, striped headdresses and hands on their knees; Stonehenge's green-lit runes and ley lines, the fallen world tree's bark, broken branches and the sapling growing from it, the giant mushrooms' gills and spots, the bandit den's sharpened palisade with skulls and torches on its gateposts, the moonwell's marble stag and silver lantern cages with moths gliding round, the spirit grove's birches, upswept shrine roof, stone lanterns and wisps trailing the spirits; the caldera's lava running down its slopes into glowing pools, the iron delve's timbered tunnel, rails and laden carts, the glacial peak's rock arch hung with icicles round the ice cave, the basalt gorge's jointed columns over a crusted lava river, the giants' forge's brick-arched hearth, rune pillars and quench trough, and the sky bridge's stone dwarf guardians, merlons, icicles and eagles (the four legendary landmarks and the Nethergarde Core have hand-built ones instead, see *Legendary landmarks in HD* above); and the free realms and frontier gates, at every stage of a realm's cycle: houses with corner posts, lintels, windows and chimneys, tents with door flaps and guy ropes, walls with merlons, a beam and banner over the gateway, towers with corbels, arrow slits and iron brazier bowls, the epic great tower with string courses and arched windows; the corsairs' gun ports, ships with rails, spars and rigging, the tavern raft and rope-railed bridges, the merfolk's branching coral and trident-held pearl, the nomads' silk pavilions roped to stakes round a stone-ringed fire, the sultanate's brass arch, crescent and minaret balconies, the sand skiffs' booms and stays, the rangers' railed platforms and ladder, the dryads' living throne under its bough, the hermits' lashed palisade with a skull over the gate, the forgehold's iron-banded door between pillars carved with bearded dwarf faces, Thunderpeak's copper-coiled lightning rods cabled to its battery, the eyrie's saddled drake with horns, talons and a tail; Valoria's voussoirs and iron portcullis, Drak'mar's horned skull and lashed tusks, Sylvandale's silver lantern cages and filigree, and Ironforge's carved guardians, studded door and pilasters (a broken gate loses all of it with the rest)), and extra detail as its rarity climbs (a common stays matte and plain but well made; an uncommon gets its accent colour in a real material; a rare its metal fittings; an epic the most detail and its glow). It stands on a neutral dark stage. Xal'Vorath and the Empyrean switch to their hand-built showcases on their violet mythic stage, the legendary landmarks, the Nethergarde Core (risen, over the HD Empyrean, while an Archon reigns), the legendary resource nodes and the Relic Hoard to their hand-built heroes, the world bosses, the Bandit King and the Voidrift mercenaries to theirs with action buttons (the Empyrean keeps its *World map* / *City town hall* views: the same model, at the town hall's size in the city view). The **Folk & Beasts** have theirs too: people with legs that step and arms that swing (raised to work, striking with the weapon arm in a fight, both up in a cheer), closed fists gripping their tools and weapons (a hoe, a pick, a spear with a heraldic shield on the left arm, a longbow and quiver, a horned helm and axe, a cleaver and spiked hide shield, a staff with its orb in gold prongs), a face with eyes, brows, nose and mouth and each civilization's ears, tusks or beard, hats, hoods and hair (an elf's long hair and robe trailing as they walk); animals with four stepping legs and hooves or paws, real heads with eyes and ears (the cow's horns, udder and tufted tail, the sheep's fleece of curls, the pig's snout and curly tail, the horse's mane and streaming tail, the stag's antlers lit with the void), scout mounts in full tack (a fringed saddle cloth, girth, stirrups, a bridle on the horses, buckled saddlebags; the War Ram's great curled horns, the Voidmare's burning eyes) with their riders sitting astride, hands on the reins, birds with feathered wings that beat in flight and fold back along the body at rest (the griffin an eagle's head and wings with gilt primaries on a lion's body and tufted tail; Normal draws it as a gilded bird); the windmills' sails laced to lattice frames and the forge gear's bronze teeth and rivets. The Voidrift mercenaries have theirs on the bosses' rig (*Mercenaries in HD*, *Legendary creatures in HD*).
* **Buildings**: each district (Homes & Shops, Farms, Pastures, Barracks) step by step up the rarity ladder: Lv1 (tier 0, common), Lv4 (tier 1, uncommon), Lv12 (tier 2, rare), Lv20 (tier 3, epic), the grand Lv25, ★★★★ (epic aura), red ★ (legendary) and the Archon's Celestial Lv26 (mythical), in any civilization's style, with a **Normal / ✨ HD** switch showing the plot and its structures in their HD version (§5 *HD structures*, *Celestial structures in HD*).
* **Improvements**: every improvement at every tier with its cost, shown on a sample city in any civilization's style, with a **Normal / ✨ HD** switch showing its HD version (§8 *HD improvements*).
* **Soundtracks**: a radio that plays each civilization's three soundtracks (15 in all), composed for the civilization and played live by a synthesized orchestra (no audio files). Its deck shows the play state and the realm · track name (top left), the key and tempo and the instruments heard right now (top right), a live spectrum and waveform in the civilization's colours with the elapsed time in its corner, the track and measure number (bottom left) and a caption of what the music is doing (bottom right). Below it: ⏮ / ▶ Play · ⏸ Pause / ⏭, **Repeat track** or **Play on** (moves through the list), volume and hall echo, and a filter by realm (*All realms* or one civilization). Every track can be played by anyone for now; the game doesn't play them on its own yet. The music keeps playing on every Codex tab (a green dot on *Music & Sounds*) and stops when the Codex closes.
  * 👑 **Valoria** (Human): *The Dawn Banner* (realm theme: horns, trumpets and the high keep's bell), *Valoria at Harvest* (pastoral: lutes, flute and chapel bells), *March of Many Hands* (war march: snares, low strings and brass).
  * 🌸 **Sylvandale** (Elf): *Glades of Sylvandale* (realm theme: harp, flute and an old choir), *Echoes of the Thousand Years* (lament), *Moonbow Vigil* (battle: quick harps, darting flutes, a volley).
  * ⚒️ **Ironforge** (Dwarf): *Halls of Ironforge* (realm theme: deep choir, horns, the forge), *The Anvil Song* (work song on the hammers' beat), *Oath at the Pass* (battle: the shield wall holds).
  * ⚔️ **Drak'mar** (Orc): *Drums of Drak'mar* (realm theme: war drums, bone horns, a raw reed), *Blood Fury* (battle: the berserkers' frenzy), *Ashes of the Warcamp* (lament).
  * 🔮 **Nethergarde** (Arcane): *The Voidrift Hums* (realm theme: void glass and voices from beyond), *Rites of the Unbound* (ritual), *Under the Eye* (omen).
* **Game sounds**: every sound cue (§13 *Interactive Audio*), each on a little looping stage of its own: a smith tapping an anvil (click), soldiers cheering round their banner under golden sparks (victory fanfare), a broken standard over the fallen (defeat horn), a scroll unrolling on a proclamation board (herald's call), coins leaping from a chest (harvest chime), droplets rising from a well (water), two files marching (footstep), a footman flattening a slime (splat), and a battle mage casting at a straw foe on its post, on the same spell-effects kit as the map: a fireball (spell whoosh), a frost lance shattering on it (spell boom) and a champion's Dawnbreaker sunrise (Finale horn). Click a cue or **▶ Play** to hear it, even with game sounds switched off. Cues the game doesn't play yet are marked *not played in the game yet*.

---

### What's New (update notices)

When the realm opens after signing in (or looking around as a guest), a **What's New** window announces the game's newest features, one page each: a big headline, a short story, the key numbers (read from the game's own rules) and a link to the Guide Book chapter. Every update has a **headline design of its own** (gilded rays, inferno embers, forge sparks, arcane motes, a moonlit sky, savage claw marks, a thunderstorm, a sunrise or a heroic shockwave), each with its own entrance.

* **Versions.** Updates are grouped into **weekly versions**: the launch week (from Monday 12 October 2026; the realm opened on the 14th) is **v1.0**, and every Monday starts the next (v1.1 from 19 October, v1.2 from 26 October, v1.3 from 2 November, …). A notice belongs to the version of the week it shipped, by its date.
* **When it shows.** By itself the window shows only the **newest version's** notices from the **last 7 days** that the player hasn't dismissed; older versions stay under ☰ → What's New. Ticking **Don't show again** before closing hides every notice shown for good on that browser (`localStorage`); without it, the window comes back on the next visit. It waits until the herald's notices and any open battle report are closed.
* **☰ → What's New** reopens the notices of **any version**: pick one from the **Version** list (newest first, with its name, week and number of updates).

The notices are hard-coded in `src/components/ui/updateNotices.ts`; every major feature ships with one.

## 11. Signing In & Admin Controls

* **Login is a must**: nothing in the realm can be seen without signing in. A signed-out visitor sees only the login screen, and the server answers every request for realm data (kingdoms, repositories, developer names, the map, the Codex, the Hall of Renown…) with *Sign in with Git first*. The one exception is the login screen's flyover (below), which shows the world map as it stands without a single name: each castle only by its civilization and castle level, the open Voidrift slots only by where they stand. Only `GUEST_BROWSING=true` (§12, meant for a local machine) lets signed-out visitors in, through **Look around without signing in** on the login screen. Signing out returns to the login screen.
* **Login screen**: a drone flight over the real realm in HD, on a loop. The screen stays black (the sign-in card already up) while the realm loads, then light burns in from the black in pixel fire (a white-hot edge eating outward in jagged veins behind a ring of light, the pixels cooling through cyan to violet and crumbling away, sparks flying off) on the continent seen from high in the sky. The drone drops through the clouds and flies low round the four mortal provinces (Valoria, Sylvandale, Ironforge, Drak'mar) and in over the Voidrift. There a rift tears open in the air in front of the Archon's seat and the drone pushes in on it: Xal'Vorath's claws come through, wrench it open and it breaks out into the world (*Xal'Vorath in HD*, *Emerge*); then it casts its Void Self-Destruct (*Cast*): the core collapses, bursts, and the void swallows the screen. From the black the flight starts over. No castle, ruler or region is named. It is always drawn in HD, on every device, whatever your Graphics setting; the realm itself opens in your own setting. The **Sign in with Git** button stays on screen throughout (a failed sign-in, or a server without Git sign-in set up, is explained under it). Visitors who prefer reduced motion see the realm from a still drone instead: no flight, no cast.
* **Sign in with Git** (OAuth2) is enabled when `GIT_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` / `GIT_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET` are set. A kingdom belongs to the Git account that first held its login: if that login later passes to a different account, that account is refused at sign-in (*This Git login belonged to an earlier account*). Any signed-in ruler can browse every kingdom; only the signed-in owner can change civilization, swap building plots, or buy improvements. Once signed in, the ☰ menu button shows your initial in a **frame of your civilization**: gold laurels on royal blue (human), a silver leaf vine on teal (elf), a riveted iron ring with rune notches (dwarf), bone tusks on red leather (orc), a glowing void ring (Nethergarde); Special Overseers get the violet ring with a gold star; the Archon of the Realm a crimson ring with a flickering molten-gold rim and the Archon's Seal. Every ruler in the kingdom picker wears the same frame, with their castle level (`Lv12`) to the left of their name.
* **Administrators (`ADMIN_USERS`)**: Users listed in `ADMIN_USERS` receive access to the in-game **Admin Panel** (header ☰ menu → Admin controls):
  - 🌾 **Execute Instant Harvest** (Admin Panel, *Harvest & Encounters* card): Instantly executes an automated pool distribution cycle across all active kingdoms based on repository commits and PRs, without waiting for the 15-minute background timer. It does not touch the roving encounters.
  - ⚔️ **Respawn Encounters Now** (same *Harvest & Encounters* card): Rolls a fresh set of roving mobs, bandits and treasure for the current window (same tier rolls as usual); every player sees it within a few seconds. The regular respawn at the end of the window still happens.
  - ⛈️ **World Disasters** (Admin Panel, its own card under *Harvest & Encounters*): pick a disaster and **Strike now**, or **Warn, strike in 30 min** to see the herald's warning first; **End it now** ends the one in force (its closing notice follows). A called disaster lasts 4 hours, replaces one in force and any due meanwhile, and is announced to everyone like a scheduled one (`POST /api/admin/disasters`, `GET` lists the last week and the next two days). On a local machine, `npm run disaster -- <blight | tempest | blood_moon | plague | barren> [minutes] | end | list` does the same without signing in (the running server raises the notices within a minute).
  - 📜 **Reset Treasury & Ledger** (Admin Panel, in the *Treasury & Improvements* card with the improvement refund): Wipes all historical transaction ledger entries and allocation logs. Resets developer resource balances to baseline while preserving special overseer treasuries.
  - 🔄 **Re-check Commits & Repos** (in any developer's profile): re-reads that developer's emails from Git, links unassigned or mis-assigned commits to them and refreshes their kingdom immediately (see §2).
  - 🏗️ **Reset & Refund Improvements** (Admin Panel, *Treasury & Improvements* card: pick one kingdom or **All kingdoms** — or the red button in a kingdom's City Improvements window): strips every improvement tier the ruler owns and deposits **100% of the current price of every tier** (base resources and rare gems) back into their treasury, with ledger entries (`admin_refund_improvements`) the ruler sees in their treasury ledger: one line with the resources returned and one per gem. Walls drop to the fence and wall HP is capped at the fence's maximum. Every ruler who lost improvements gets a 🏗️ *Improvements refunded* herald notice (§14) listing what came down and what was returned. Special Overseers can't be reset (their improvements are fixed at maximum).
  - 🪶 **Realm Backup** (Admin Panel, under System Validations): **Download backup** saves the developers with their linked commit emails and per-repo matches (📧 Developer Emails), the Scriptorium project tree, which repo sits in which project and every deed report to a JSON file; **Restore from file** merges such a file back, e.g. after wiping the data, and reports how many of each it restored (§17, *Backup*). A file that isn't a Realm Backup (or an older Scriptorium backup), comes from a newer version, or has an unreadable developer, division or deed list is refused whole.
  - 📦 **Reset Historical Backfill** (the last card of the Admin Panel): Clears all repository backfill checkpoints in SQLite and re-indexes complete historical Git activity down to `BACKFILL_START_YEAR` (available via API `POST /api/admin/backfill/reset`).
* **👑 Royal Decrees** (👑 crown button in the top bar, for admins, Special Overseers and the Archon): announcements, royal gifts of resources (admins only), shields and punishments. See §15.
* **🌠 Omens** (feedback): the developers are the gods of this realm (their commits raise its cities), so a god doesn't pray: it sends **omens** down to the stewards. ☰ menu → **🌠 Send an Omen**, for every signed-in ruler: a kind (🐞 bug · 💡 idea · ⚖️ balance · 🙌 praise · 🌠 other), a title (up to 80 characters) and a message (up to 2,000), **5 a day** (UTC day). The window lists your own omens and which were heeded. **Only admins read them**: a **🌠 N new omens** banner at the top left (under the deeds banner), *New omens* in the ☰ menu and an **🌠 Omens** button on the Admin Panel's System Validations card open the list (Unread / All). **✨ Mark as read** sends the sender a herald notice (§14), by default *"Your omen was heeded"* with *"The stewards of the realm have read your omen: “{omen title}”."*; **Edit the notice** lets the admin change both words first. **Mark all read** sends every sender the default notice. Each one is written to the realm's event log (`omen_heeded`).
* **Admin warnings banner** (admins only, top-left; re-checked every 5 minutes) — opens the validation panel. It is also always reachable from the **System Validations** card at the top of the Admin Panel (the ☰ menu's *Admin controls* item and the card show the warning count; the panel re-checks on open and shows "all checks passed" when clean). It lists two Scriptorium checks (§17): **🪶 Repos with no project** (repos with commits that sit in no project; listed for the record with a button to the Scriptorium's Unsorted bucket, but not counted in the banner, since a new realm starts with every repo unsorted) and **🪶 Deeds waiting too long** (pending more than 3 days; counted, with a button to the deeds to seal). The card also has a **📜 Deeds to seal** button with the count. And it checks for **duplicate repositories**: the same repo name under several owners where at least one copy is an empty duplicate. A copy is deletable only when it is Level 1, has **0 commits**, carries **no deed reports** and has **finished historical backfill** (so unindexed history is never mistaken for an empty repo); the most recently updated copy of a name is always kept. Deleting (one at a time, **Delete Group** for one repo name, or **Delete All** for every group) removes the repo with its PRs, reviews, subscriptions and plot, and adds it to an ignore list so Git sync never re-adds it. **Restore** removes it from the ignore list; the next Git sync brings it back and backfills it again. On the **Ignored / Deleted Repos** tab, **Check for commits** reads each ignored repo's commits from Git (its default branch, or its newest branch when the default was never pushed), newest first, and compares them with the commits the realm already holds. A commit is counted once in the realm, so a repo copied under several owners has its history credited to one copy and the others show 0 commits: that is why such copies count as empty duplicates. Each row is tagged with what the check found: **Has new commits** (commits no repo in the realm holds yet and the copy it shares history with doesn't have in Git either, so they are its own and not just waiting for the next sync; with the latest one's date, and the row outlined green), **No new commits · same history as** *owner/repo* (it has commits, but all of them are already counted under that repo), **Still 0 commits**, **No longer in Git**, or **Check failed**. The panel checks a few repos at a time, so the rows fill in as Git answers, with a running count; a full check of a few dozen repos takes under a minute. The tags are saved and stay until the next check; the tab shows how many repos have new commits and when it last checked. The check changes nothing: a repo with new commits stays out of the realm until you Restore it. **Re-scan Realm** at the foot of the panel only re-reads these lists; it does not ask Git.
  - 📍 **Refresh Kingdom Positions** (same card): lays every castle out again by the spacing rules (*Where castles stand*, §10), e.g. after two castles ended up on top of each other. The Voidrift's NPC kingdoms find fresh plots in its band too. Only positions change: civilizations, cities, treasuries, bosses, landmarks and who holds each Voidrift slot stay; armies already on the road finish their march to the old spot.
  - 🗿 **Reset Landmarks** (Admin Panel, in the *Refresh & Rebalance World Map* card: region, *Skip to finished* and the button above its two buttons): rotates the epic landmarks now in every region or one picked region: the standing landmark starts crumbling and another of the pool starts rising for 24 h, or tick **Skip to finished** to have the new one stand at once. Kingdoms, bosses, castle positions and the legendary landmarks are not touched, and the weekly rotation still comes on Sunday (`POST /api/admin/reset-landmarks`).
  - 🚩 **Move Realms** (same row as Reset Landmarks, same region picker): the buffer realm of every region (or the picked one) is taken off the map at once (no crumbling day, so a region never shows two realms), and another of the region's 3 stands at a fresh spot, full, at the stage its cycle is on (`POST /api/admin/move-realms`).
  - 🗺️ **Execute Reset & Refresh**: Reallocates all developers across the 5 provinces using the allocation formula, clears saved plot arrangements to reset district selection, re-rolls the buffer realms (they join the current week of their cycle) and epic landmarks (they start finished), and regenerates the 3D world map placements.

---

## 12. Configuration

| Env var | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `GIT_SYNC_INTERVAL_HOURS` | 1 | Background Git refresh interval in hours (1 to 596, e.g. 4) |
| `SYNC_INTERVAL_MINUTES` | 15 | Resource harvest interval; roving encounters respawn with every harvest (15 min by default) and resource waves every 2× this (30 min) |
| `LIVE_SYNC_LOOKBACK_DAYS` | 3 | Rolling window for live head sync in days (backward-compatible alias: `GIT_LOOKBACK_DAYS`) |
| `GIT_MAX_COMMITS_PER_REPO` | 5000 | Commit safety cap per repo per sync (0 = unlimited) |
| `BACKFILL_ENABLED` | true | Enable or disable background historical repository backfill |
| `BACKFILL_START_YEAR` | 2020 | Starting year for historical walk (starts Jan 1st 00:00:00 UTC) |
| `BACKFILL_DELAY_MS` | 500 | Gentle pause between repos in ms (keeps CPU & Git load near zero) |
| `BACKFILL_FORCE_RESET` | false | When true, resets all repository backfill checkpoints on startup |
| `GIT_MAX_BUILDINGS` | 100 | Max buildings per kingdom |
| `SPECIAL_USERS` | — | Comma-separated logins of managers/trackers locked to Nethergarde Voidrift as max-level overseers |
| `ARCHON_USERS` | — | Comma-separated logins of the Archon of the Realm (department head): everything an overseer has, plus untouchable, castle Lv30 and 50 Lv30 buildings at the Voidrift's heart. Don't also list them in `SPECIAL_USERS` |
| `ADMIN_USERS` | — | Comma-separated logins of administrators with access to the Admin Panel and World Reset |
| `GIT_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` / `_SECRET` | — | Enables Sign in with Git |
| `ADMIN_WITHOUT_SIGNIN` | false | `true`, with `ADMIN_USERS` set and Git sign-in not configured, makes every request an admin. For a local machine only; a server missing its OAuth settings otherwise has no admin at all |
| `APP_URL` | http://localhost:5173 | Public URL used for Git OAuth2 redirect (`APP_URL/api/auth/callback`) |
| `BASE_LIFESPAN_HOURS` | 24 | Natural lifespan of a 1.0× civ (humans); sets death rates (§13) |
| `NETHERGARDE_PLAYER_SLOTS` | 3 | Voidrift slots open to mortal developers; empty ones are held by NPC void kingdoms (§13) |
| `GUEST_BROWSING` | false | `true` lets signed-out visitors browse the realm (*Look around without signing in*); anything else and every API route but sign-in needs a session. Turn it on only on a local machine, e.g. to check visuals |
| `GAME_LAUNCH_DATE` | — | The day the game started (`YYYY-MM-DD`) for the 🔭 Watchtower's before / after comparison (§18). Empty: the realm's first harvest, remembered once read |
| `FISCAL_YEAR_START_MONTH` | 10 | Month (1–12) the fiscal year starts; the Hall of Renown's Year tab covers this fiscal year so far (§16) |
| `REALM_TIMEZONE` | Asia/Manila | The realm's time zone (an IANA name): world disasters start between 06:00 and 22:00 on this clock (§10 *World Disasters*). Weekly resets stay at Sunday 00:00 UTC |

---

## 13. Population, Armies & War

Server: [demographics.ts](server/src/demographics.ts), [warConfig.ts](server/src/warConfig.ts) (every number below), [combat.ts](server/src/combat.ts), [warEngine.ts](server/src/warEngine.ts), [diplomacy.ts](server/src/diplomacy.ts)

### Population & Soldier Cap

* **Capacity** = City Hall level × 100 × civ multiplier (Human 2.0, Orc 1.4, Dwarf 0.8, Nethergarde 0.6, Elf 0.35). A new kingdom starts at 10% of it.
* **Saturation** S = population ÷ capacity. Every harvest:
  * **births** = max(1, ⌊population × birth factor × (1 − S)⌋) × (1 + 2% per 🌷 Royal Gardens tier) × the season (±10% for its buffed / debuffed civ, §7) while there is room and food in the granary (Human 12%, Orc 15%, Dwarf 4%, Elf 1.5%). The free room (1 − S) makes it an S-curve: a kingdom gutted by war breeds at almost its full rate, so **orcs rebuild fastest** (50 survivors → ~84 in 1 h, ~700 in 6 h, near capacity within a day; elves 50 → ~74 in 6 h), while elves gain about one citizen per harvest.
  * **deaths** = ⌊population × S ÷ lifespan in harvests⌋, civilians only. Lifespan = `BASE_LIFESPAN_HOURS` (24 h) × civ factor (Human 1.0, Orc 0.6, Dwarf 3.0, Elf 10.0): an empty kingdom barely dies, a crowded one loses ~1/lifespan per harvest. Kingdoms level out at roughly 89–100% of capacity.
* **Nethergarde** never breeds or dies of age. It grows by a **void ritual** (Kingdom → Population & Army: 20 food + 10 iron + 10 stone per construct, up to capacity). Its soldiers killed defending the citadel fall into the **Void Well**; 10% of the well (at least 1) returns to the garrison each harvest. Special Overseers respawn to full population every harvest.
* Population is split into **civilians** (work: each 100 add the civ's yield to the harvest — Human 150 food + 50 gold + 50 lumber, Orc 100 food + 100 iron, Dwarf 150 stone + 100 iron + 40 gold, Elf **80 lumber + 3 mana + 1 of that harvest's scarce gem** (a full City Hall 10 elf kingdom ≈ 10 mana + 3 gems per harvest, ~1.5–2× an average developer's share of the drops), Nethergarde 50 of each base resource), the **home garrison** and **soldiers away on a march**. Soldiers away still count in the population but can't be re-mobilised and don't work; the dead leave the population for good. **Civilians away gathering** (below) also still count, but don't work, don't die of age and can't be armed until they are home; the Kingdom panel shows them as 👷 gathering.
* **Soldier Cap (`maxSoldiers`)**: Only part of a kingdom's population can bear arms at once — `⌊population × CIV_SOLDIER_SHARE[civ]⌋` (Nethergarde 75%, Orc 70%, Human 50%, Dwarf 50%, Elf 40%) — ensuring every realm retains a civilian workforce.
* **Arming** a civilian costs 2 iron + 5 food; sending soldiers home is free. The home garrison eats **1 food per soldier per harvest**. If the granary can't pay, 10% of the garrison deserts (🌷 Royal Gardens cut that by 4% of it per tier), and a garrison with an empty granary defends with half its armor.
* Set the garrison in **👥 Kingdom → Population & Army** (header). The **Census** tab lists every harvest, mobilisation, summon, battle, healing (🌙 elves' wounded back from the Moonwell) and civilization swap. `npm run sim:population` prints the growth table:

```
From genesis (City Hall 10)
civ       max  start     1h     2h     4h     6h     1d     3d     7d
human    2000    200    296    430    808   1236   1837   1837   1837
orc      1400    140    227    354    707   1025   1251   1251   1251
dwarf     800     80     90    102    128    161    605    717    717
elf       350     35     39     43     51     59    131    323    350
arcane    600     60     60     60     60     60     60     60     60

After a war: 50 survivors (City Hall 10)
civ       max  start     1h     2h     4h     6h     1d     3d     7d
human    2000     50     75    113    255    531   1837   1837   1837
orc      1400     50     84    138    350    701   1251   1251   1251
dwarf     800     50     54     62     78     99    515    717    717
elf       350     50     54     58     66     74    146    338    350
arcane    600     50     50     50     50     50     50     50     50

(arcane doesn't breed: it grows only by summoning and its Void Well)
```

### Royal Proclamations (Paid Settlers & Festival)

Server: [warConfig.ts](server/src/warConfig.ts) (`SETTLERS`, `FESTIVAL`, `CALL_TO_ARMS_MS`), `callSettlers` / `holdFestival` in [db.ts](server/src/db.ts) · Client: [RoyalProclamations.tsx](src/components/ui/RoyalProclamations.tsx)

Proclamations are one-off royal orders with a cooldown, paid mostly in food (the resource kingdoms pile up most). The owner gives them in **🏗️ Improvements → 📜 Royal Proclamations**. They are for **mortal kingdoms only**: Nethergarde grows by its void ritual and Special Overseers' people return in full every harvest, so neither can use them.

* **🏘️ Paid Settlers** refill a kingdom gutted by war. Only while the population is **below 50% of capacity**, and never past that line: one call brings as many settlers as the room to the 50% line and the treasury allow, at **60 food + 1 gold each** (dearer per head than a Nethergarde construct). **Cooldown: 1 day.** The Census lists them as *Settlers*.
* **🎉 Festival**: for **24 hours** the kingdom's births are **×2** and its civilian workforce brings in **+10%** of its yield. It costs **10,000 food + 100 gold per City Hall level** (100k food + 1,000 gold at City Hall 10) and can be declared **once a week** (7 days from the start of the last one). It **can't be declared while the ruler is ⚔️ Called to Arms**; an attack on the city during a Festival doesn't end it. The herald tells the ruler's own civilization (§14).
  * **By royal decree**: admins, Special Overseers and the Archon can give a kingdom a Festival for free, apart from its own weekly one (§15 *Proclamations*).
  * **In the city**: bunting and paper lanterns are strung between four poles over the plaza (round it and across the fountain), ten townsfolk dance in a ring round the fountain while others cheer, and fireworks burst over the castle. **On the world map** fireworks burst over the castle and its label shows 🎉. Every panel showing the kingdom shows **🎉 Festival — time left**.
  * **Normal and HD**: in Normal the props are plain shapes and the fireworks round sparks bursting in spheres. In HD the props go through the HD model kit (materials, bevelled shapes, stone feet and iron collars on the poles, wooden caps and red tassels on the lanterns), the dancers are the HD townsfolk, and the fireworks get more sparks, each a streak along its flight, a short rocket trail, ring and willow shells beside the round ones, and a crackle as they die.
* **⚔️ Call to Arms**: a ruler who sends an attack (a march, or leading or joining a rally) on **another ruler's city or a frontier gate** is Called to Arms for **1 hour**, refreshed by every such attack. Only the attacker is; the defender never is. Mobs, bandits, bosses, NPC kingdoms, Voidrift slots, scouting and gathering don't count. While it lasts no Festival can be declared. It shows as a red ⚔️ pill in the top bar (hover or tap it for the time left) and as **⚔️ Call to Arms — time left** in the kingdom's panels.

### Marches, Slots & Capacity

* **Civilizations as Factions**: Any developer kingdom (mortal or Nethergarde) can march on any kingdom of **another civilization** (or a mortal can march on an open Voidrift NPC spire), and on roving **mobs and bandits**, **world bosses** and **NPC kingdoms** (below). You can **never** attack a kingdom of your own civilization, and **Resource Caravans** can only be sent to kingdoms of your **own civilization**. Special Overseers never march, but mortals **can attack them** (a loot area, below).
* **March kinds**: ⚔️ **attack**, 🚩 **rally** (lead or join), 👁️ **scout**, ⛏️ **gather** and 🛡️ **reinforce**. Every kind uses one slot until it is home (a rally lobby and stationed reinforcements included).
* **March Slots**: A kingdom can have **`⌈City Hall level ÷ 5⌉` simultaneous marches** out at once (1 slot at Lv 1–5, 2 at Lv 6–10, 3 at Lv 11–15, 4 at Lv 16–20, 5 at Lv 21–25). Each march occupies its slot from launch (`outbound`) through combat (`acting`) and the walk home (`returning`) until it reaches home (`done`). You can send multiple marches at the same target up to your slot limit.
* **March Capacity**: Each march can carry at most **`⌊maxPopulation × 0.20 × CIV_MARCH_MULT[civ] × banners⌋`** soldiers (Elf 1.5×, Orc 1.25×, Nethergarde 1.25×, Human 1.0×, Dwarf 1.0×; 🚩 Kingdom Banners add +2% per tier, §8).
* **March Speed**: travel time is the distance at the civ's march speed (×1.05 for the season's buffed civ, ×0.95 for its debuffed one, §7 *Day, night & seasons*), clamped to 30 s … 4 min 59 s, then divided by **1 + 2% per 🛣️ Roads tier** (up to 10% faster, also on the way home).
* **Round-Trip Rations & Recall**: Launching a march pays **round-trip rations up front** (`2 × supplyTicks × 2 food × soldierCount × rationMult`; **elves pay 25%**; 🏮 Lantern Streets cut it by 2% per tier). An owner can **Recall** an `outbound` march (turning back immediately from its current position on the road; elapsed travel time becomes the return duration) or retreat an `acting` march from battle at the end of the current round. **Rations are never refunded on recall** — food is already packed and eaten on the road.
* **Return Trip & Plunder Delivery**: Surviving soldiers and any plundered resources (`carry`) walk back along the road in the `returning` phase and are credited back to the home garrison and treasury only when the army reaches home (`returnTime`). The moment an army is home, the page picks it up on its own: soldier counts, treasury, the March Queue and free march slots update within a couple of seconds, no reload needed.
* **March Queue Panel**: Available on the left of the world map and in the **Marches** tab of the City Directory (`MarchQueue.tsx`), showing used slots (`2 / 3`), march capacity, live progress bars, ETAs, battle rounds (with the last round's losses on both sides), carried loot, and one-click camera focus or **Recall**.

### Launching marches: the Muster

Server: `launchMuster` in [warEngine.ts](server/src/warEngine.ts) (`POST /api/marches/muster`), the pure rules in [musterRules.ts](server/src/musterRules.ts) (client copy `src/utils/muster.ts`); window: [MusterPanel.tsx](src/components/ui/MusterPanel.tsx) in the march form

Every ⚔️ **attack** and every 🚩 **rally you lead** is launched from the **Muster**, one screen in the march form that sends several marches on one target at once, so none of them reaches the fight late. Scouts, gatherers and reinforcements still go one march at a time, and **joining** someone's rally is still one army (*Open rallies* → *Join*): a joiner brings one march.

* **The header** names the target and your **march slots used / total**, plus how many marches you have ticked.
* **Mercenary bands**: each of your bands with a tick box, its name and rarity, its **strength** (a wounded band shows e.g. *80% wounded* and marches at that strength) and its **power**. A band that can't march (away, defending your walls, healing, dead) is greyed out with the reason; bands don't rally, so a rally muster shows them greyed too.
* **Soldiers**: how many are **at home** and how many **a full march carries** (your march capacity), and four **split chips: 10%, 25%, 50%, 100%**. Each soldier box carries that share of a full march, and there are as many boxes as **your free march slots and your soldiers at home allow** (`min(free slots, ⌊soldiers at home ÷ box size⌋)`). If soldiers are left over and a slot is still free, **one smaller box carries the rest**: with 250 to a box and 4 free slots, 800 at home makes 250 / 250 / 250 / 50, while 1,100 at home makes 250 / 250 / 250 / 250. Picking a split rebuilds the boxes; each keeps its tick, loadout and champion.
* **Each soldier box** has a tick box, its soldier count, an **Armory loadout** picker (*Standard gear* or one of your civilization's sets, each with how many hang at home), a **Lead with champion** switch (one box at a time; greyed out while the champion is away or wounded) and its **power**.
* **Ticks are capped at your free march slots**: once they are all spoken for, the other tick boxes grey out.
* **Power** is counted in your own soldiers: a box is its **soldiers × its set's power** (standard ×1, uncommon ×1.08, rare ×1.16, epic ×1.25) **× its champion's worth** (about +25% × level ÷ 30, so +0.8% at level 1 and +25% at level 30); a band is **the full march it was hired for × its rarity's power × its strength**. The numbers come from the server's march preview.
* **Order of battle: automatic, strongest first.** The ticked marches are listed strongest first with their **total power**; there is no manual sorting. That is the order they take their places in the battle, so the defenders strike your strongest stack first (your own soldiers still stand before any band, §13 *Mercenaries*).
* **One Launch button sends them all, all or none.** The server checks the whole muster first: the target, the free slots, the soldiers at home, enough sets at home for every loadout, the champion, every band (yours, home, not healing, a target bands may strike) and the cost of it all together (every soldier march's round-trip rations plus every band's battle pay). If anything is wrong nothing sets off, and the window lists **every** problem at once; it shows the same checks inline before you press Launch.
* **They arrive in the same moment and all fight from round 1.** Every march of a muster sets off together and moves at **the slowest one's pace**, so they reach the target in the same instant: the first (the strongest) opens the battle and the others take their places before round 1 is fought (the battle log: *⚔️ Ser Aldric's 300× Royal Footman arrive alongside and fight from round 1!*; a band *arrives alongside and fights*). Every march of one ruler to one target takes the same road time today (your civilization's pace and your 🛣️ Roads; a band marches at its hirer's), so the shared arrival costs nothing; it is there so the rule holds should paces ever differ. A march only ever arrives later than it would alone, never earlier, and none waits at home in a hidden state.
* **Each is still its own march**: one slot each, its own Recall from the March Queue, its own survivors and plunder.
* **On the map they walk as one host**: marches that leave one castle together for one target march side by side in a grid across the road (four marches, two by two), so the bigger the attack, the bigger the army you see. The host carries one label on the way out (*⚔️ 4 marches*). Each block keeps its place if another turns back. Scouts never form a host.
* **Leading a rally** with the Muster raises the rally with every ticked soldier box as its own army in the lobby (one slot each, rations paid now); they set off with the rally and fight from round 1 like any lobby army. Recalling the **first** (strongest) army calls the rally off, as before; recalling another only walks that one home.

**🔨 Forging in the Muster.** If a box's loadout is short of sets (none forged yet, fewer than the ticked boxes in that set need, or some rusted away at the last harvest, §13 *The Armory*), the box shows the **shortfall** and a **Forge** button. It opens a small forge under the box: the **quantity** (the shortfall by default), the **cost** and **how many your treasury can forge**, and a confirm. It is the Armory's own forge with its rules (cost per set, the epic set's gem, upkeep from then on). After forging the Muster reads your armory again and can launch.
* **Can't afford them all**: the forge says how many the treasury can pay for and offers to forge that many; then pick a smaller split, another loadout, or untick a box.
* **A set your City Hall can't forge yet** (sets forged before a change are kept): the sets at home can still be worn, but there is no Forge button (*send fewer or pick another loadout*); with none at home the set is greyed out in the picker.

### 🌙 Moonwell Healers (elves): wounded, not dead

Elves are the fewest people in the realm (a City Hall 25 elf fields about 175 soldiers against a human's 833) and the slowest to be born, so every death costs them more than anyone. Their trait, **Moonwell Healers** (`MOONWELL` in `server/src/warConfig.ts`, its rules in `server/src/moonwell.ts`), softens it:
* **Who and when**: of the soldiers an **elf army loses in a battle**, **50% are carried home wounded** instead of dying (rounded down, counted over the whole battle: an army that has lost 71 so far has 35 wounded and 36 dead). It holds in every battle an elf army fights, attacking (castles, gates, realms, bosses, packs), defending its own castle, or reinforcing an ally. Only elves have it; every other civilization's fallen die as before. Losses **on the road** (🌪️ Tempest Roads) and **caught scouts** are still deaths, and so are gatherers (they are civilians).
* **Population**: the wounded **keep their place in the population** while they heal. They don't work, eat, age or fight, and they count against the soldier cap like soldiers away, so no recruit can take their place: elves can't arm replacements and also get the wounded back.
* **Healing**: at **every harvest** a few wounded rejoin the home garrison, the longest-wounded first: **2% of the kingdom's soldier cap at full population, rounded up, at least 1** (`woundedHealPerHarvest`: City Hall 3 → 1 a harvest, 5 → 2, 10 → 3, 15 → 5, 20 → 6, 25 → 7). So a march wiped out (half of it wounded) is back in about **3½–5 hours** and a whole soldier cap's worth in **4½–6½ hours** at any City Hall, while births would take 13–33 hours to replace the same dead at City Hall 10–25. The rate goes by capacity, not by today's population, so a war-thinned kingdom heals as fast. A ruler leaving the elves (ascending into the Voidrift, or a peaceful civilization swap) gets every wounded soldier back at once first.
* **⏩ Hasten**: the ruler can pay to bring **every wounded soldier home now**: **20 food + 2 gold per soldier, plus 1 🟢 Sylvan Emerald per 25 soldiers** (rounded up). That is about a third of what raising a new soldier from nothing costs (a Paid Settler, then arming them).
* **Reports**: a battle report shows an elf army's losses as dead and wounded together: the side card's *Fallen* `−71` with `🌙 35 wounded` under it, the Armies list `−71 (35 wounded)`, the War Room's report list the same, and the battle log closes with `🌙 35 of Ser Aldric's fallen are carried home wounded to the Moonwell (a few rejoin the garrison at every harvest)`. Arts that bring fallen back (*Second Wind*) still do; the wounded are settled from what is left lost.
* **Kingdom window**: 👥 Kingdom → Population & Army shows the elf kingdom's **🌙 Wounded** line (how many heal, how many come back at the next harvest and when, and roughly when the last are back) and, for its ruler, the **Hasten** button with its cost; the population bar shows them in indigo. The Census lists each round's dead and wounded, and each harvest line says how many were healed (`🌙 +6 healed at the Moonwell`).
* **Balance**: `npm run sim:balance` counts **only the dead** as an army's losses everywhere (PvP loss gaps, bosses, packs). With the wounded left out, elves defending against orcs won too easily, so **Hold the Glade** came down from +1300% ATK / +650% HP to **+1270% / +635%** (orcs win about half again). The sim also checks that an elf rally's **effective losses** (its dead plus its wounded weighted by the hasten's cost, about a third of a soldier each) against every world boss stay **no lower than those of the civilization that loses least** against the same boss: at City Hall 20 an elf rally of 4 now loses 23–35% dead and 31–47% effective per try, against dwarves' 24–27%.

### Live Multi-Stack Battles, Walls & Burning

* **Live Battles (10 s rounds)**: At most one active battle takes place at a target at a time (`battles` table), ticking one round every **10 seconds** (up to 6 rounds; a rally's armies fight up to **8**, each counted from its own arrival). If another army arrives while a battle is already in progress (`status = 'fighting'`), it **joins the live battle** on the next round (`joinedRound = round + 1`) so players can swarm a hard target together. Armies arriving **in the same moment** (a Muster, below, or a rally setting off) all fight from round 1.
* **Seeing a battle**: crossed **⚔️ swords** float high above every live battle (a castle siege, a mob or boss fight, gatherers fighting off raiders). They strike **four times per 10-second round**, the heaviest blow landing on the round tick, throwing golden sparks and dust, and they grow with camera distance so a skirmish is easy to spot across the continent. Clicking a target that is being fought over shows **⚔️ In Combat / Under Siege (Round X/6)** at the top of its panel: join as a reinforcing wave or wait for the outcome.
* **Battle numbers**: after every round (the first is fought the moment the army arrives, then one every 10 seconds), its numbers rise over both sides of the battle on the round's tick and fade within ~4 seconds: the **soldiers lost** in big red (`−320`) over the side that lost them, and the **damage it dealt** in smaller gold text underneath (`⚔️ 1,240`); the last round's numbers rise as the battle ends. A side that lost nobody shows only its damage, and an unguarded castle none at all. **Battle Arts** rise on the same text a beat later (§13 *Battle Arts*): a unit's casts, Finale and Instincts by name over its own side (`❄️ Frost Lance`, `🪓 Cleave −12`, a Finale larger and gold), and status ticks as their icon and losses over the side they hurt (`🔥 −40`; its damage when no one falls, as on a boss: `🔥 2,400`), up to 4 a side per round. Once per round however many armies of a rally fight it; the two sides' numbers never overlap on screen, from any angle. They show at near and mid zoom and fade out as you zoom out to the overview. The battle report's **Round by round** table lists the same numbers per side (lost, dealt, then the soldiers still standing), with each round's Battle Arts under it (casts and Instincts in the caster's colour, ticks in orange, a Finale on its own gold line; the battle log has them too), and while your army fights, its March Queue row carries the last round's losses next to the round counter (`−22 you · −73 foe`).
* **The outcome**: the moment a battle ends, a **🏆 Victory!** (attackers cheering over the fallen garrison) or **🛡️ Repelled** banner (fallen attackers, survivors falling back; **🛡️ Driven off** for gatherers routed from their node) stands on the battlefield for **6 seconds**; then the survivors set off home. Gatherers reaching their node set to work (**⛏️ Gathering**) — they only fight when raided.
* **Defeat**: when an army is repelled (or gatherers are driven off their node), its **remains** lie on the battlefield for **45 seconds**, in its civilization's colours: its standard snapped and leaning with the torn banner still hanging from the crossbar, a second banner trampled into the mud, a spear with a torn pennon, shields, helmets, skulls and the army's own broken weapons (Valoria's swords and kite shields, Sylvandale's bows and leaf shields, Ironforge's axes, Drak'mar's cleavers and horned helms, Nethergarde's cracked crystal staves), round a fire of broken shafts. The more soldiers fell, the more lies strewn round the standard (5 to 11 pieces). The remains burn down as they fade: flames, bright embers and a column of smoke for the first third, red embers and grey smoke next, then cold with a last wisp of smoke (a staff's crystal goes dark). With HD graphics (§10) they are drawn through the HD model kit: real wood, cloth, iron and bone, a trim band and tassels on the banner, iron rims and painted bands on the shields, real skulls and bones, arrows stuck all over the field and a shattered cart wheel. In the March Queue the survivors' row turns red: **💀 Defeated** with "Repelled at … Returning survivors (−N soldiers)". If the army was yours (leader or rally member), the Chronicle shows a red alert for 30 s (click ✕ to dismiss) with a falling horn cue: `⚔️ Defeat: Your march on [Target] was repelled (Losses: -X soldiers)`.
* **Focus Fire**: Every attacking stack strikes the defender each round; the defender focuses its counter-attack on the **earliest-arrived attacking stack first**, spilling leftover damage onto the next stack only when the front stack is wiped out. Marches of one Muster arrive together and stand **strongest first** (§13 *Launching marches: the Muster*).
* **The ring on the map**: every army fighting one target stands at its own place in a **ring round it**: the first army stays at the end of its road and the others walk round to theirs in the moments after they arrive, spaced evenly at 2, 4 and 8 armies (past 8, a second rank further out). An army keeps its place when another joins or falls, and at the end walks back round to its road before heading home. A boss or a mob or bandit pack **turns to face the army it is striking** (the same one Focus Fire picks) and turns to the next when that one falls. The crossed swords stand over the target with the **round number** between them, one per battle. The ring is only how the battle looks: order and damage are as above.
* **Defender groups**: a castle defends with its home garrison **plus every reinforcement stationed there** and its ruler's mercenary bands at home that were paid their battle pay (§13 *Mercenaries*), each as its own group with its own civilization's stats, traits and buffs, all behind the host's walls. Each attacking stack splits its blow across the groups by headcount; every group strikes back. Reinforcements' losses come out of their own ruler's population (an elf's are half wounded, §13 *Moonwell Healers*); a band's are its strength.
* **Combat Formula**:
  * damage = soldiers × ATK × (1 − armor) × a ±15% roll, where armor = DEF / (DEF + 20); defenders multiply their DEF by their **City Wall multiplier** (`WALL_DEF_MULT`) and take **10% less damage per wall tier** (`WALL_DAMAGE_TAKEN_MULT`: tier 0 full damage … tier 5 half). The DEF multiplier alone barely helps low-armor soldiers; the damage cut makes every tier count: a City Hall 25 castle needs ~2.9 waves of City Hall 15 orc marches to breach at tier 0 and ~5 at tier 5 (`npm run sim:balance`).
  * Kills = damage ÷ HP (leftover damage carries to the next round).
  * Civ traits — Human *Swarm Tactics* +25% ATK when outnumbering 2:1 (scaled), Orc *Frenzy Charge* +25% ATK in round 1, Dwarf *Shield Wall* takes 40% less damage, Elf *Arcane Precision* ignores 30% of armor, Nethergarde *Ethereal Shield* halves the first volley.
* **City Walls (5 Tiers), Wall HP & Burning Protection**:
  * Every castle has **Wall HP** = `1,000 × City Hall level × WALL_TIER_HP_MULT[wallTier]` (1.0× at Tier 0 up to 2.0× at Tier 5; Voidrift NPC spires have Tier 3 walls = 1.70× DEF, 24,000 HP).
  * Walls take **1 HP damage per defender killed** each round, plus **6 HP damage per surviving attacker** when the garrison is breached — so a City Hall 1–5 castle burns after 1–2 waves of a City Hall 10 march and is then left alone, while a City Hall 25 castle with tier-5 walls takes ~20 such waves.
  * When a castle's Wall HP drops to **≤ 25% of Max HP**, the city **catches fire** and enters **ceasefire protection**. The fire is **epic-class** on the visual ladder: on the world map its castle burns, with tongues of fire round and on it, a column of smoke, embers streaming up and an orange curtain of fire-light round it (a Wonder or the Empyrean keeps its own higher kit under the flames); in the city view fire runs all along the walls (either side of the gates), about a third of the houses (at least 3, at most 10, the same ones every visit) burn in their own curtain of fire-light, and the town hall smokes. The same fire burns on Voidrift NPC kingdoms, buffer realms and frontier gates whose walls burn; a burning gate is also **broken open** (see *Frontier gates*). Then:
    * If walls drop ≤ 25% mid-battle, the current round finishes and all surviving attackers automatically march home.
    * A burning kingdom **cannot be attacked** (outbound marches already on the road turn back empty-handed on arrival) and **cannot launch offensive marches**, re-align, or swap civilizations.
    * Burning walls slowly self-repair from **0% to 26% over 8 hours** (~4 hours from 25% to 26%, lifting burning status), and once above 25% repair at **full Max HP per 4 hours**.
* **Plunder by Troop Load & Vault Protection**:
  * When a developer's garrison is breached (0 defenders left), each resource in the defender's treasury has a **protected vault floor** of **`1,500 × City Hall level`** that can never be stolen.
  * Surviving attackers take what their survivors can carry — no fixed share: **load** = survivors × `LOAD_PER_SOLDIER` (20) × `CIV_LOAD_MULT[civ]` (Dwarf 1.5× = 30/soldier, Orc 1.2× = 24, Nethergarde 1.0× = 20, Elf 0.8× = 16, Human 0.7× = 14).
  * Load is filled in priority order **`gold → iron → stone → lumber → food`** (scarce gems are never plundered in PvP); each surviving army fills its own load from what is left, earliest arrival first.
  * **After a breach**: armies that arrive while the garrison is empty (and the walls are still above 25%) meet no defenders and plunder **what is left**, each by its own load. Once the walls burn, later arrivals turn back empty-handed.
* **Buffs & debuffs** (`CIV_MODIFIERS` in [warConfig.ts](server/src/warConfig.ts)) add to a civ's HP / ATK / DEF and stack. An entry can be limited to fights **vs** certain civilizations and/or to **when** attacking or defending, so each matchup is tuned on its own and never leaks into other fights. Tuned with `npm run sim:balance` to the matchup chart below:

  | Civ | Modifier | Effect | Applies |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 🔮 Nethergarde | ▲ Universal Harmony | +15% HP, ATK, DEF | always |
  | 🏹 Elf | ▲ Void-Piercing Volleys | +250% ATK, +100% HP | vs Nethergarde |
  | 🏹 Elf | ▲ Volleys From the Treeline | +200% ATK, +100% HP | attacking humans |
  | 🏹 Elf | ▲ Cull the Vanguard | +500% ATK, +250% HP | attacking orcs |
  | 🏹 Elf | ▲ Hold the Glade | +1270% ATK, +635% HP | defending against orcs |
  | 🏹 Elf | ▲ Armor-Piercing Runes | +400% ATK, +200% HP | attacking dwarves |
  | 🏹 Elf | ▲ Glade Ambush | +900% ATK, +450% HP | defending against dwarves |
  | 👑 Human | ▲ Massed Levies | +25% ATK | vs Nethergarde |
  | 👑 Human | ▲ Grain-Fed Hosts | +230% ATK | attacking orcs |
  | ⚒️ Dwarf | ▼ Void-Dulled Steel | −20% ATK | vs Nethergarde |
  | ⚒️ Dwarf | ▲ Anvil Against the Horde | +100% ATK, +100% DEF | vs orcs |
  | ⚒️ Dwarf | ▼ Slow Advance | −50% ATK | attacking humans |
  | ⚒️ Dwarf | ▲ Stone Ramparts | +30% ATK, +15% HP | defending against humans |
  | ⚒️ Dwarf | ▲ Break the Charge | +120% ATK, +60% HP | defending against orcs |
  | 🛡️ Orc | ▼ Frenzy Breaks on Void Shields | −70% ATK | vs Nethergarde |

  The elf numbers look big because elves are so few: at City Hall 10 an elf can field 140 soldiers against 800 human footmen or 700 orcs in two marches, and defends with 70 at home, so each elf has to be worth five to ten of them. New buffs or debuffs are one entry in `warConfig.ts` plus its copy in `src/utils/civDemographics.ts` (the balance sim fails if the two differ); the Kingdom panel lists them as ▲ / ▼ chips under the civilization's Unique Trait (§9), the legend that explains them.

### NPC void kingdoms (open Voidrift slots)

Every slot no developer holds is an **NPC void kingdom** (a named Warden at City Hall 15, Tier 3 Fortress Walls with 1.70× DEF and −30% damage taken; 23% of its 900 people — **207 Void Weavers** — stand in the garrison, `VOID_NPC.GARRISON_SHARE`) drawn as an arcane castle in the Voidrift. Losses stick between assaults: the Void Well only re-manifests 2% of the garrison per harvest once nobody has attacked for 6 hours. It is sized so **three assaults of 800 Footmen** break it; the target is 2–6 assaults for a City Hall 10 developer filling every march slot. Assaults needed, live battles, every slot filled (`npm run sim:war`):

| City Hall | Human | Orc | Dwarf | Elf |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | 102.7 | 71.7 | 74.9 | 25.3 |
| 5 | 38.4 | 26.4 | 27.1 | 10.6 |
| 8 | 4.0 | 4.0 | 6.0 | 3.0 |
| 10 | 3.0 | 3.0 | 4.8 | 2.0 |
| 12 | 2.0 | 2.0 | 3.0 | 2.0 |
| 15 | 1.0 | 2.0 | 3.0 | 1.0 |
| 20 | 1.0 | 1.0 | 2.0 | 1.0 |

Low City Halls barely dent it (one march slot); from City Hall 8 the second slot makes it a real target. Elves are the fastest (at City Hall 10 one march of theirs needs ~3, a human one ~9); dwarves, who fight best behind walls, are the slowest. Orcs, who would otherwise be the fastest everywhere, break on the Void Shields. `npm run sim:balance` checks City Hall 10 and prints ⚖️ BALANCED. Several players can wear the same garrison down together.

**No rallies:** an open slot is claimed by one ruler alone — it can be attacked (several players may still wear it down with separate marches) but not rallied on.

**Taking a slot:** whoever deals the breaching blow to the garrison claims the slot, the Warden's treasure and **ascends to Nethergarde** — the kingdom becomes arcane, its people converted at the same fill ratio of the new capacity, and its former mortal civilization gains an **open place** (§9).

**Usurping a developer:** a mortal who breaks a Nethergarde developer's garrison takes their slot and ascends; the defender is cast down into the attacker's former civilization with 75% of its fill ratio.

**Moving on the map:** whenever anyone's civilization changes (ascending, being cast down, a peaceful swap or re-alignment), every open page reloads kingdoms, castle positions and Voidrift slots together within a few seconds, so castles **teleport** to their new province with no page reload: a pillar of light and a shockwave burst in the old civilization's colour where the castle stood, then in the new one's where it lands. If it was your own civilization, the map glides to your castle in its new province.

**The conquering army teleports too:** the new Nethergarde ruler's armies that won the battle don't walk back to the old castle — after their victory banner they vanish in a void flash and are home at once with their survivors and spoils (rally joiners of other rulers walk home as usual). Any other march of a ruler whose castle moved (the ascender's or the cast-down defender's) walks back to the castle's new place.

### Peaceful civilization swap & Open Places

Two **mortal** rulers (human, elf, dwarf, orc — never Nethergarde, never a Special Overseer) can trade civilizations: click the other castle → **Swap civs** → *Propose swap*. The panel previews both sides' population after the swap. The other ruler answers from the same panel or the **Diplomacy** list in the War Room (the ⚔️ badge counts open offers); the proposer can withdraw, and offers lapse after 24 h. On acceptance both civilizations change at once with **no casualties**: each side keeps its fill ratio (e.g. an orc kingdom at 11% becomes an elf kingdom at 11% of elf capacity), castles move to their new provinces, buildings and levels stay. Neither side may have any march out (`outbound`, `acting`, or `returning`), neither city may be burning, and the two civilizations may not be in **War Frenzy** this week.

### Resource Caravans (Sending Resources)

Server: `transferKingdomResources` in [db.ts](server/src/db.ts), `POST /api/kingdoms/:id/send-resources` in [routes.ts](server/src/routes.ts)

* Rulers can transfer stockpiled basic resources (Gold, Iron, Stone, Lumber, Food) directly to any developer kingdom of their **own civilization** (click a friendly castle → **Send Resources**).
* Deductions and credits take effect immediately; double-entry ledger entries are recorded for both the sender and recipient treasuries.
* **Restrictions**: Caravans can **never** be sent to other civilizations (faction economic assistance is strictly intra-civilization) and cannot be dispatched to your own realm. **Special Overseers and the Archon send none**: their treasury refills every harvest, so a caravan from it would be resources from nothing.


### War Frenzy

Stops rulers from defecting to the civilization they are fighting.
* Two civilizations are in **War Frenzy** once any battle this week pits a member of one against a member of the other — either side attacking: sieges of a ruler's castle (Nethergarde developers included), rallies on them, and attacks on gathering marches.
* Fights that **don't count**: NPC kingdoms (they only borrow a civilization's soldiers), world bosses, mobs, bandits, Voidrift NPC kingdoms and Special Overseers.
* While it lasts nobody of either civilization may **swap into the other's open place**, and no **peaceful swap** between the two is allowed — in both directions (the attacked civ can't run to the attacker either). Swaps with civilizations you aren't fighting, caravans and rallies within your civ are unaffected.
* It **resets every Sunday 00:00 UTC** (a war that starts on Friday clears on Sunday; a fight on Sunday starts a fresh week). The War Room lists this week's pairs.
* **Seeing it on a kingdom**: when you click a kingdom of another civilization and your two civilizations are in War Frenzy this week, its panel shows a red **⚔️ War Frenzy · time left** badge (hover for what it blocks and when it ends).

### Civilizations at war

| Civ | Soldier | HP | ATK | DEF | Trait | March speed | Rations | Load / soldier | Soldier cap | March cap mult | Archetype |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 👑 Human | Royal Footman | 12 | 5 | 3 | Swarm Tactics: up to +25% ATK when outnumbering | 1.0× | 100% | 14 | 50% | 1.0× | **The Swarm** — huge numbers, cheap manpower, colossal civilian output; fragile soldiers and a big food bill |
| 🛡️ Orc | Blood Berserker | 28 | 16 | 6 | Frenzy Charge: +25% ATK in round 1 | 1.2× | 100% | 24 | 70% | 1.25× | **The Horde** — highest mortal soldier cap (70%) and fastest to rebuild after a lost war (15% births); thin armor |
| ⚒️ Dwarf | Ironbreaker Sentinel | 42 | 8 | 24 | Shield Wall: −40% damage taken | 0.8× | 100% | **30** | 50% | 1.0× | **The Mountain Clan** — toughest soldiers, heaviest looters (30 load), stone/iron economy; slow to march and rebuild |
| 🏹 Elf | Sylvan Ranger | 24 | 18 | 8 | Arcane Precision: ignores 30% armor | 1.4× | **25%** | 16 | 40% | 1.5× | **The Ancients** — few but deadly, cheap surgical strikes, near-immortal (240 h lifespan); every loss takes days to replace |
| 🔮 Nethergarde | Void Weaver | 50 | 14 | 14 | Ethereal Shield: −50% damage taken in round 1 | 1.1× | 100% | 20 | 75% | 1.25× | **The Timeless Ascendants** — +15% all stats, never die of age, summon with resources, the fallen return through the Void Well |

Stats above are base values; buffs and debuffs (Battle, above) apply on top. `npm run sim:balance` prints the live sheet, effective stats per matchup and the balance verdict.

### Matchups (design)

How the civilizations fare against each other: `npm run sim:balance` fights every pair at equal City Hall 10 in live battles — the attacker fills every march slot (capacity, capped by the soldier cap), the defender holds half its soldier cap behind tier-0 walls — and fails unless each cell lands on this chart — **win** (the attacker wins ≥ 60% of those wars), **loss** (wins ≤ 40% and loses clearly more of its army) or **even** (a close, bloody fight).

| Attacker ↓ / Defender → | 👑 Humans | 🛡️ Orcs | ⚒️ Dwarves | 🏹 Elves | 🔮 Nethergarde |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **👑 Humans** | — | out-produce them in food; swarm if the army is huge | struggle against the Shield Wall without siege tools | heavy losses, but can overwhelm through numbers | can overwhelm with 1,000+ footmen |
| **🛡️ Orcs** | overwhelm human levies with the Frenzy Charge | — | **blunted** — the charge breaks on the Shield Wall | **win the long game** — relentless waves wear elves down by attrition | heavy casualties against Void Shields |
| **⚒️ Dwarves** | hold the walls; slow to advance | **hard counter** — dwarven armor crushes the orc burst | — | struggle marching into elf magic; excel when elves attack them | tough slugfest |
