| **🏹 Elves** | snipe human levies with ease | wipe early orc waves, but suffer if the waves never stop | **hard counter** — magic pierces dwarven armor | — | **top contender** — magic pierces the void, cheap rations for surgical strikes |
| **🔮 Nethergarde** | superior soldiers, shrug off attrition | absorb the frenzy burst, respawn the fallen | void energy bypasses physical armor | stalemate of magic | — |

### Scouting & fog of war

Server: [scouting.ts](server/src/scouting.ts), `planMarch` / `fogKingdomsFor` in [warEngine.ts](server/src/warEngine.ts)

* **Fog of war**: kingdoms of **other civilizations** keep their population, garrison, soldiers away, treasury (and gems) and wall HP secret. Until you scout them these show as **?**: no hint, not even whether the garrison is empty. Only what can be seen from outside the walls is public: City Hall level, wall tier, and walls *intact / damaged / burning*. Your **own civilization** and **Special Overseers** are always exact. Not signed in = every mortal and Voidrift NPC kingdom is fogged. Their treasury and census ledgers stay private too. Their **marches** can be seen walking the map, but how many march is shown only as **?**, and the figures on the road are all the hint you get; the exact number is known to the march's own civilization and to the ruler it is marching on.
* Voidrift NPC kingdoms and the buffer-zone NPC kingdoms are fogged the same way for every mortal (garrison **?** on the map until scouted).
* **👁️ Scout march**: 1–5 **civilians**, not soldiers: they come from your free civilians (population − home garrison − soldiers away − civilians already away gathering or scouting), so the garrison stays home. While away they don't work and don't die of age, like gatherers (the 👥 Kingdom panel counts them under *gathering or scouting*). They ride **3× faster** (10 s … 1 min 40 s, then faster still with Roads and +3% per 🏮 Lantern Streets tier), round-trip rations like any march, **one march slot** until they are home. Scouts never fight and may leave a burning castle. Click a rival castle → **Scout**, or any map NPC → **Scout**. You can't scout your own civilization or a Special Overseer (nothing is hidden).
* **On the map** ([WarParties.tsx](src/components/3d/world/WarParties.tsx)): one or two riders per party (none ride home once caught). Each sits **your own civilization's mount**: a 🐎 **Courser** (Humans, a bay riding horse), a 🦌 **Moonstag** (Elves, a pale stag ridden bareback on its cloth), a 🐏 **War Ram** (Dwarves, a shaggy ram with great curled horns), a 🐺 **Warg** (Orcs, a hulking grey wolf on a bone saddle) or a 🐴 **Voidmare** (Nethergarde, a night-black mare with a violet mane and burning eyes), each with a saddle cloth in your colours and saddlebags (uncommon on the ladder, alive with the walk of its animal). Scouting a **kingdom** (a ruler's city, an NPC kingdom or frontier gate, a Voidrift citadel) the rider is **disguised as its people**: a civilian of the target's civilization, to blend in on its roads; scouting anything else they wear their own civilization's clothes. Only scouts ride: armies, rallies, gatherers and reinforcements march on foot.
* **Spies & Counterspies** ([warConfig.ts](server/src/warConfig.ts) `SCOUT_ODDS`, `npm run sim:scouting`): scouting a **ruler's city** or an **NPC kingdom** (Voidrift citadel, buffer realm, frontier gate) can go wrong. Mobs, bandits, resource nodes, treasure, bosses and landmarks are always scouted safely. One roll per party when it reaches the target:

  | Outcome | Base chance | What happens |
  |---|---|---|
  | ✅ True report | the rest | as below |
  | 💀 Caught | 12% | the scouts die and no report comes back: a red row in War Room → Scout reports (*N scouts caught · no report*), your last true report on that target stays. You get *Your scouts never returned*; a ruler you scouted gets *Caught 3 scouts from Ser Aldric*, riding in dressed as their own folk (§14). The dead were civilians: your population falls, your garrison doesn't |
  | 🎭 Deceived | 10% | a **false report**: garrison, population and treasury (and gems) each off by 30–60%, up or down at random, kept within what the target can hold; everything else is true, so it looks like any other report (and its estimates are built on the false numbers) |

  * **Their side** raises the danger: each tier of the target ruler's 🦉 **Spymaster's Tower** (§8) adds **+3% caught** and **+2% deceived**. NPC kingdoms have no tower.
  * **Your side** lowers it: **+2% success** per scout beyond the first (more eyes), **+2%** per 🏮 Lantern Streets tier, **+3%** per tier of your own Spymaster's Tower, and **+5%** for elves (forest stealth). The bonus takes the danger down and keeps its caught : deceived split.
  * **Watched Borders**: a **city in a province sealed by its frontier gate** adds the gate's watch to the catch chance: **+15%**, rising to **+30%** as the gate fills up with garrison and troops on duty. While the gate burns, the border is unwatched (no gate bonus, only the odds above). Caught there, the notices name the gate's watch.
  * Success never drops below **50%** or rises above **95%**.
  * **Doubtful intel**: when your Spymaster's Tower tier is at least the target's (always true against NPC kingdoms), a false report has a 50% chance to carry **⚠️ This intel feels doubtful** (amber border in the War Room and on the report), so you know to scout again. A true report never carries it.
  * The **march form** shows the three chances for the number of scouts you pick (and how much of the catch chance is the gate's watch).
  * A ruler whose Spymaster's Tower fed false intel hears of it (*False intel for Ser Aldric*, §14); without a tower nobody is told. Caught and deceived parties are realm events.
* **Reports** are written when the scouts reach the target and **delivered when they get home**; the latest one per target is kept for 7 days (War Room → **Scout reports**). They hold exact numbers as of the moment the scouts looked, and show their age ("seen 12m ago · 3 harvests ago"):
  * kingdom: City Hall, garrison, population, soldiers away, unit stats against your civ, trait, buffs/debuffs, treasury (rounded to 100), gems, wall tier & HP, last battle;
  * Voidrift NPC kingdom: garrison / max, wall tier & HP, conquest loot;
  * mob / bandit: units left / full pack, unit stats, trait, spoils;
  * resource node: its yield, what's left and who is gathering it · treasure: chest size and exact contents · landmark: **lore** (on a rotation day, also whether it is rising or crumbling and how long that has left);
  * boss: shared HP, stats, skills, rotation and lore · NPC kingdom: City Hall, donor civilization, garrison / max, population, unit stats, treasury, wall tier & HP.
* **Estimates**: a kingdom's report never goes stale silently. Every number shows as **`~scouted–estimate`**: the value the scouts saw and what it has likely become since (`~1,240` while nothing can have changed, `~1,240–1,610` later). The estimate uses only the report and the game's public rules, never the kingdom's live numbers:
  * population: the civilization's births and deaths for every harvest since (Royal Gardens can't be seen);
  * garrison: soldiers who were away may be home again (after the first minute);
  * treasury and gems: what the kingdom's last harvest paid when the scouts looked, for every harvest since (a gem range assumes every drop was that gem);
  * wall HP: repaired at the normal rate since;
  * Voidrift NPC kingdoms regrow their garrison per harvest; buffer-zone NPC kingdoms regrow by births and refill their treasury like they really do.
  
  Marches, battles, spending and mobilizing since then aren't in the estimate. Scout again for fresh numbers. Panels (castle, kingdom info, top bar, march form, ledger, map labels) show these ranges; without a report they show **?**.

### World encounters: mobs & bandits

Server: `WORLD_NPC_UNITS` in [warConfig.ts](server/src/warConfig.ts), [worldNpcs.ts](server/src/worldNpcs.ts)

* Every roving **mob** and **bandit** spawn is a pack of `count × tier` units (tier multiplier: common ×0.7, uncommon ×1.6, rare ×2.5, epic ×4; commons are small, so most City Hall 1 armies win their first fights; elves, with so few soldiers, from about City Hall 3) with its own HP / ATK / DEF, sometimes a trait (e.g. Dire Wolf *Pounce* +25% first strike, Ancient Lich *Soul Drain* ignores 40% armor) and a **Battle Arts kit** sized by its rarity (§13 *Battle Arts → Mobs & bandits*). They fight in the open: no walls, no civ buffs on either side.
* **Fog**: the map shows only a band of the pack (`~40–50 Dire Wolfs`, *wounded* once someone has fought it) and a treasure's chest size (*small / large / hoard*) until you scout it. A pack's **HP left is public**: its ground ring is an HP bar (the lit arc runs clockwise from the back and covers the share left, the lost share is a dim track), and the hover card and panel show `❤️ 64% HP`. Anyone can spot a pack someone else has worn down and finish it off; the army that lands the last blow takes the spoils.
* **Attack**: a normal live battle (10 s rounds, armies that arrive later join, the pack focuses the earliest army). Rough guide from the sim: a common pack falls to 20–60% of a City Hall 3 march; uncommon needs most of a City Hall 3 march or ~40% at City Hall 8; rare packs want City Hall 8+; epic packs need a full City Hall 15 march or several marches at once.
* **Spoils**, carried home by the survivors: mobs drop a fixed reward by tier (common 1,600 food · 1,200 lumber · 600 iron · 50 gold … epic 12,000 · 10,000 · 7,200 · 400) split by surviving troops; bandits carry a gold-heavy stash (common 2,400 gold · 900 iron · 600 stone · 900 food … epic 18,000 · 9,000 · 6,000 · 6,000) that each surviving army takes **by its PvE load** (3× the normal load, `PVE_LOAD_MULT`), earliest first. Spoils are fixed by tier, so world PvE is the grind for low kingdoms: a common win is worth several of their harvests, while high kingdoms earn far more just from the harvest.
* **Shared world**: losses stick for everyone, wounds included: damage that killed no whole unit stays on the pack (a march that only wounds one drake leaves that drake hurt for the next challenger), and the pack shows *wounded*. The army that wipes the pack out takes it; the pack disappears from the map for everyone until the next roving window, and **every other march heading there turns back at once** from where it is (rations not refunded). A march that arrives at a pack that is already gone also turns back.
* **Bosses and NPC kingdoms**: Attack · Rally · Scout (below). **Landmarks**: scout only (lore). Resource nodes and treasure: see Gathering below.
* **Target panel & foot icons**: clicking a map NPC opens its panel with the actions above (resource node: Gather · Scout; chest: Claim · Scout; plus *Attack workers* when another civ's gatherers there are exposed) and its intel. Each NPC wears a badge on the front of its ground ring: ⚔️ mob, 🗡️ bandit (red), 🔥 boss, 🚩 NPC kingdom (orange), ⛏️ / 🌾 / 🪵 / 🪨 / 🪙 / 💎 resource by its main yield (green), 💰 treasure (gold), 👁️ landmark (blue); the risen Nethergarde Core over the Archon's citadel wears the **Archon's Seal** (crimson starburst on a gold rim).

### Gathering & treasure

Server: gathering in [warEngine.ts](server/src/warEngine.ts), node state in [worldNpcs.ts](server/src/worldNpcs.ts), numbers in [warConfig.ts](server/src/warConfig.ts)

* **⛏️ Gather march** (target panel of a resource node → *Gather*, of a chest → *Claim*): one march slot until home, round-trip rations like any march, allowed while your castle burns.
* **👷 Civilians work safe nodes**: a gather march to a node in your **own safe territory** (below) sends **civilians** instead of soldiers: no arming, the garrison stays home, same march capacity, load, speed and rations. The march form reads *👷 Civilians (safe territory)* and counts the free civilians (population − home garrison − soldiers away − civilians already out). The catch: while they are away they don't work, so their share of your civ's per-100 workforce yield is lost until they're home. They can never be attacked (even if your civilization changes while they work). Anywhere else, the gather march sends soldiers as before.
* **First come, first served**: the first march to arrive claims the node and works it **alone**. Every other gatherer heading there turns back at once (rations not refunded), and nobody can be sent to a node someone is working.
* **Load & time**: a gather march carries workers × 20 × `CIV_LOAD_MULT` × 3 (`PVE_LOAD_MULT`: Dwarf 90, Orc 72, Nethergarde 60, Elf 48, Human 42 per worker). It takes min(load, what's left) and works at **16 units/s** × its civ bonus. 🛒 Market Square adds +2% per tier to both the load per worker and the work speed. Bonuses: Elf ×2 on lumber, Dwarf ×1.5 on stone / iron / gold, Human ×1.5 on food, Orc ×1.25 on everything, Nethergarde ×1. At City Hall 10 a human march empties a common 9,000-lumber node in about 9 min; an elf march carries only 5,040 but loads it in under 3 min.
* **What comes home**: the haul is split across the node's resources by its yield mix. **Gems ride along without weight**: each gatherer gets its share in proportion, and whoever **empties the node takes the gems left**. The rest stays in the node for the next player; an emptied node is gone for everyone (a daily one until the next day's nodes, a wave one for good). The haul is credited when the march gets home. **Recall** any time: the workers walk home with what they've gathered so far.
* **Treasure**: claiming takes **30 s**, and the chest goes **whole** to the claimer, whatever its load. Contents by tier: common 4,000 (1,200 gold · 1,000 iron · 800 stone · 600 lumber · 400 food), uncommon 10,000, rare 20,000, epic 32,000 + 1 gem, legendary 60,000 + 2 gems. A claimed chest is gone for everyone.
* **Safe territory**: your own province and the two buffer zones bordering it.

  | Civ | Safe to gather in |
  |---|---|
  | 👑 Human | Valorian Heartlands, Whispering Deepwood, Scorchwind Expanse |
  | 🏹 Elf | Sylvandale Glades, Whispering Deepwood, Sunken Fjord |
  | ⚒️ Dwarf | Ironforge Highlands, Sunken Fjord, Dragonspine Peaks |
  | 🛡️ Orc | Badlands of Drak'mar, Scorchwind Expanse, Dragonspine Peaks |
  | 🔮 Nethergarde | nowhere — no node spawns in the Voidrift, so its gatherers are always exposed |

  Anywhere else your workers are **exposed**: any other civilization can click the node → **Attack workers**. Other civs may still march through your safe territory; only your gatherers there are protected.
* **Attacks on gatherers**: a live battle in the open (no walls; civ buffs and debuffs apply as in any fight between civilizations). **Gathering pauses** while it lasts. If the attackers win, they carry off what the workers had gathered so far, by load, earliest army first (a claimed chest goes whole to the first army; gems to the first army). The node keeps the rest and is free again. If the workers hold, they go back to work where they stopped. These fights count toward **War Frenzy**, and the gatherer gets the battle report.
* **Day rollover & expiring waves**: workers still on a node at 00:00 UTC, or on a wave node when its roving window ends, finish their work and carry their whole load home; the old node just takes no new marches (a march still on its way turns back), and its workers can no longer be attacked there.
* **On the map**: workers stand around the node, bent over their work (swinging when attacked), under a "⛏️ Gathering" label, with a green progress ring around the node. The node's ground ring shows **what's left** like an HP bar (the lit arc covers the share left, clockwise from the back; public, no hover needed), and the hover card reads `⛏️ 63% left`. The node panel shows what's left (a band unless you scouted it or your own workers are there) and whose workers are there, **🛡️ safe** or **⚠️ exposed**. The March Queue shows ⛏️ rows with the haul so far and time left (*Attacked* while fighting).
* `npm run sim:balance` checks that one City Hall 10 march working common nodes back to back hauls 50–250% of an average kingdom's harvest income per hour (today: Human 117%, Orc 122%, Dwarf 114%, Elf 98%), and that no civ out-gathers another by more than 2×.

### Rallies & reinforcements

Server: rallies and reinforcements in [warEngine.ts](server/src/warEngine.ts), defender groups in [combat.ts](server/src/combat.ts)

**🚩 Rallies**
* **Raise one** from any target you could attack (rival kingdoms, Nethergarde developers, Special Overseers, world bosses, NPC kingdoms, mobs, bandits) — except the NPC void kingdoms on open Voidrift slots, which one ruler claims alone: march form → **🚩 Rally**, pick a wait of **2, 5 or 10 min** and your armies in the **Muster** (§13 *Launching marches: the Muster*: one or more soldier boxes, each its own army with its loadout and maybe the champion; bands don't rally). They wait in the **lobby at your castle** (one march slot each, round-trip rations paid now).
* **Join**: only rulers of the **leader's civilization** see it (March Queue → *Open rallies*, War Room). *Join* sends your army to the leader's castle. It must get there before the rally sets off, and you can't join if it can't. Joiners pay rations for the trip to the lobby plus the round trip to the target. The rally takes **3 joiners** (leader City Hall 1–15), **4** (16–24) or **5** (25) besides the leader; first come, first served.
* **Set off**: when the wait is over, everyone in the lobby marches together from the leader's castle at the **slowest member's speed**; joiners still on the road are sent home. Each member's army fights as its own stack, and **every army gets +5% ATK per joiner** (up to +25% with 5 joiners), plus **+1% per tier of the leader's 🚩 Kingdom Banners** — cooperation pays. Each member's survivors carry their own plunder **back to their own castle**, and every member gets the battle report.
* **Call off**: the leader can cancel before it sets off (or recall their lobby army, which does the same); the lobby walks home, joiners on the road turn back. Rations are never refunded.
* `npm run sim:balance` keeps a rally better than the same armies arriving separately: 5 orc armies of 350 against 2,500 footmen lose ~1,170 trickling in one per round, ~390 arriving together, and ~380 as a rally (+20%).

**🛡️ Reinforcements**
* Click a castle of **your own civilization** → **Reinforce**. Your soldiers walk there and stay **stationed** on its walls, using one march slot, until you **recall** them or the host **sends them home** (Kingdom → Population & Army → *Defence*).
* They fight in **every defence** of that castle as their own group (your civ's stats and buffs, the host's walls), and they keep eating **1 food per soldier per harvest from your own granary**. If your granary is empty they defend with half armor.
* **Room on the walls**: a castle holds allies only up to its **max population minus all its own soldiers** (home and away), so its soldiers and reinforcements together never exceed its max population (City Hall 10 human: 2,000 — e.g. 1,000 own soldiers + 1,000 allies, or 0 + 2,000). Each sender is still limited by their own march slots and march capacity. The march form shows the free room.
  * Troops on the way reserve their room when they set off: a march bigger than the free room is refused, so allies sending at the same time can't overfill it.
  * When troops arrive, only what fits takes station; the rest walk straight home as their own column (holding a march slot until they're home). This happens when the host armed more soldiers while they marched.
  * If the castle goes over (the host arms more soldiers, or its max population falls), the **newest** reinforcements walk home first, only as many as needed. Never mid-battle: they leave once the battle is over.
* A burning castle can't send or receive new reinforcements; troops already stationed stay. Special Overseers can't be reinforced.
* **Your civilization's frontier gate** can be reinforced too (map → the gate → **Reinforce**): the same rules with the gate's own cap and a gate-duty upkeep (§13 *Frontier gates*). You recall them from the March Queue; there is no host to send them home.
* **Seeing them**: rulers of the host's civilization see how many troops are stationed there: the map panel of an allied castle or of your civilization's frontier gate shows **Reinforcements: total — yours, allies'**, each sender with their count, and how many more are on the way (the gate also shows its reinforce cap); the castle's *Defence* section shows the same total. Other civilizations never see this count in their panels, and scout reports don't include it (§13 *Scouting & fog of war*); an attacker learns of them in the battle log.
* They can't leave while the castle is fighting off an attack. If the host's civilization (or yours) changes, they go home.
* Survivors stay stationed after a battle. Reinforcements arriving during a battle join it on the next round.

### World bosses, NPC kingdoms & Special Overseers

Server: [warEngine.ts](server/src/warEngine.ts), shared state in [worldNpcs.ts](server/src/worldNpcs.ts), numbers in [warConfig.ts](server/src/warConfig.ts) (`BOSS_UNITS`, `BOSS_HOARD`, `BOSS_BOUNTY`, `NPC_KINGDOM_*`, `OVERSEER_*`)

**🔥 World bosses** — one per buffer zone
* **Attack or Rally** it (a kingdom may also swarm it with all its march slots). A boss is **one giant unit** fighting in the open: 105,000–135,000 HP, ATK 750–1,000, DEF 10–14.
* **Shared HP**: every battle wears it down **for the whole realm**, and it **never heals**. Its HP bar is public (target panel, march form, battle report).
* **Colossus Hide**: armies land only part of their blows on a boss, by their ruler's City Hall: **(City Hall ÷ 25)²**, so City Hall 10 lands 16%, 15 lands 36%, 20 lands 64% and 25 hits at full strength. Each army in a battle uses its own ruler's level, and the battle log names the share (`🪨 Colossus Hide: Ser Aldric's troops (City Hall 15) land 36% of their blows`). The march form shows yours.
* **Frenzy Breaks on Colossi** (orcs): an orc army lands **50%** of what Colossus Hide lets through. The war-drums that drive a horde mean nothing to a colossus: it neither fears nor bleeds the way a foe should, and the frenzy breaks on its hide (`🪓 Frenzy Breaks on Colossi: …'s troops land 50% of their blows on the colossus`). Without it a City Hall 25 orc's soldiers alone would kill some bosses in one battle.
* **Heartseeker Volleys** (elves) and **Giant-Felling Runes** (dwarves): an elf army lands **200%** and a dwarf army **180%** of what Colossus Hide lets through. Their armies are the smallest in the realm (at City Hall 17 an elf ruler fields 238 soldiers, a dwarf 680, a human 1,700), so without it they needed two or three times the battles of the others, alone and in a rally. With it a rally of four City Hall 17 rulers kills an average boss in about 4 battles whatever their civilization (elves 3.2, humans and dwarves 4.4, orcs 3.8, Nethergarde 4.2, each in its epic set), and a lone elf or dwarf needs about as many battles as a lone dwarf did before (elves ~16, dwarves ~9). The battle log and the march form say so (`🏹 Heartseeker Volleys: Ser Aldric's troops land 200% of their blows on the colossus`). An elven archer knows where a colossus's hide wears thin; the oldest runes on dwarven steel were cut in the giant wars.
* **🗿 Titan's Endurance**: no single battle can take more than **60%** of a boss's max HP, whoever fights it (one ruler, a rally, every title, set and band there is). Blows past that bounce off for the rest of the battle, and the battle log and the floating numbers say so (`🗿 The Kraken's Titan's Endurance wakes`). So a boss at full HP never falls in one battle; one already under 60% can. Its HP still carries over between battles as before.
* **🌋 Colossus's Wrath**: when every army fighting a boss belongs to **one ruler** (their mercenary bands count as theirs) and they take **30%** of its max HP in one battle, the boss rages for the rest of that battle: **ATK ×2**, multiplied with its own 💢 Enrage if that wakes too. A rally of several rulers never wakes it. A lone City Hall 25 ruler with every combat title maxed in their epic set wakes it against every boss and loses a few points more of their army for it (humans −24% → −31%, elves −46% → −54%, `npm run sim:balance`).
* **Mercenary bands** land **30%** of what Colossus Hide lets through (the Sellsword's Oath, §13 *Mercenaries*). Bosses are raid content for high kingdoms: a mid-level ruler sending every march slot barely scratches one.
* **Battle Arts** (§13 *Battle Arts*): every boss carries **a colossus's kit**, one Instinct more than a legendary unit's — **3 Instincts**, 2 Arts and **an ult (Finale) of its own** — so an army that brings no Arts of its own (today: every ruler's own soldiers; a mercenary band brings its own, and a champion brings its own at a hero's share) fights it at a disadvantage. **Every boss has 🪓 Cleave** among its Instincts: each round it kills **3% of every army** on top of its blow, so its blows reach the whole host, not only the army in front (about 17% of each army over a 6-round battle; a mercenary band loses that strength too, and comes home needing a heal). Every ult hits in round 5 of each battle, or in round 1 as a last stand once the boss is under 25% HP, and each is worth about the same (`npm run sim:arts`: 1.4–1.6 rounds of blow).

| Boss | Instincts | Arts | Ult (Finale) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Kraken | Cleave, Thick Hide | Frost Lance, Glacial Prison | 🐙 **Abyssal Grasp**: tentacles drag at every army (125% of its blow), then Frozen |
| Leviathan | Cleave, Stoneskin | Ward, Frost Lance | 🌊 **Maelstrom**: a whirlpool over every army (160%), then Chill −25% |
| Siren | Enrage, Bloodlust | Ward, Thunderclap | 🎵 **Siren's Requiem**: a deathly song over every army (145%), then Paralyzed |
| Sandworm | Cleave, Thick Hide | Armor Break, Rending Blow | 🪱 **Devouring Maw**: the ground swallows the enemy front (200%, that army only) |
| Sphinx | Enrage, Stoneskin | Ward, Thunderclap | 🌪️ **Sandstorm**: a wall of sand over every army (160%), then Sunder −30% |
| Scorpion | Cleave, Enrage | Venom Volley, Armor Break | 🦂 **Stinger Storm**: a rain of stingers on every army (145%), then Poisoned |
| Treant | Stoneskin, Bloodlust | Ward, Second Wind | 🌳 **Grasping Roots**: thorned roots through every army (165%), then Bleeding |
| Wolf | Enrage, Bloodlust | Rending Blow, War Cry | 🐺 **Wild Hunt**: spectral wolves run down every army (160%), then Bleeding |
| Fae | Cleave, Thick Hide | Ward, Venom Volley | 🧚 **Glamour of the Court**: enchanted petals over every army (140%), then Chill −30% |
| Dragon | Cleave, Enrage | Fireball, War Cry | 🐉 **Dragonfire**: a cone of fire over every army (160%), then Burning |
| Yeti | Enrage, Thick Hide | Frost Lance, Shield Wall | 🏔️ **Avalanche**: the mountainside comes down on every army (170%), then Chill −20% |
| Gorgon | Cleave, Stoneskin | Ward, Glacial Prison | 👁️ **Petrifying Gaze**: every army turns half to stone (120%), then Frozen |

* 🪓 Cleave kills 3% of each attacking army every round on top of its blow; 💢 Enrage is +50% ATK below 50% HP; 🛡️ Ward is −80% damage taken in the first round of each battle. A boss's heal (the Treant's Second Wind) brings back HP lost in that battle only, never what earlier battles took. The panel, the march form, scout reports and the Codex list each kit; every battle's report shows its casts, ticks and ult.
* **Killed**: the zone **rests for 24 h**, then the **next boss of its pool** wakes there at full HP (or sooner, at the weekly rotation). Every other army heading there turns back. Meanwhile the next boss **rises as a spectral phantom** where the old one fell (dark translucent body with a bright cyan rim, drifting slowly, in a column of light with violet smoke curling up) under a **👻 <name> rises in H:MM:SS** badge, and can't be attacked. Its panel names it (*👻 Rising where Gorgon Stonecrusher fell*), counts down to the second and shows the last fall: who led the killing army, the hoard carried home and each ruler's bounty share with the damage they dealt.
* **Alive and scary**: each boss is a rigged model with glowing eyes that blink. It breathes, sways, waves its tentacles or beats its wings, looks slowly round and now and then rears up in a roar, over a dark pulsing aura with embers rising. While armies fight it, it **turns on them and strikes** on a loop: tentacle slams, snapping jaws, paw swipes, stinger strikes, fists and mauls smashing down, and the Greatwyrm breathes fire. The Codex preview shows each boss striking every few seconds. With HD graphics every boss is a hand-built creature with a full idle, roar and attack, and the Greatwyrm and the Fae Queen fly over their roosts (§10 *World bosses in HD*).
* **On the map**: the boss's red ground ring is its HP bar: the red arc runs clockwise from the back of the ring and covers the share of HP left, and the lost share is a dim track (all dim while the next one rises). Hovering a living boss shows its real shared HP (`❤️ 82,400 / 120,000 HP`, with the bar and percentage).
* **Rotation panel**: the boss panel lists the zone's pool of 3. The current boss reads ⚔️ CURRENT BOSS, or 💀 FALLEN once felled. The next boss in the pool is highlighted as ⏭ NEXT BOSS (👻 RISING after a kill) with the time until it wakes.
* **🔥 Vengeance**: every time a zone's boss is felled this week, the boss that wakes there next is **+10% stronger in HP, ATK and DEF**, stacking (third kill this week → +30%). It **resets to 0 at Sunday's rotation**, so a zone can't be farmed every 24 h at the same strength. The boss panel shows the stacks (and what the rising boss wakes with), as do the map hover card and the rotation card. `npm run sim:balance` (rally of 4 City Hall 20 rulers): human 3 → 5 tries from +0% to +50%, dwarf 5 → 9, elf 3.5 → 6, orc ~2.
* **Rotation**: the pool of 3 moves on one boss every **Sunday 00:00 UTC**, whatever state the current one is in, and one more **every time its boss is felled** (the next boss wakes when the 24 h are up). If Sunday comes first, the boss after that one wakes at the rotation.
* **Loot**: the armies in the killing battle carry its **hoard** home (36,000 gold, 60,000 iron / stone / lumber, 90,000 food) by load — survivors × load, first-arrived army first, gold → iron → stone → lumber → food; what nobody can carry is left behind — plus its **6 gems** (one kind per boss) split by survivors. **Everyone who damaged it this life** shares the **bounty** (12,000 gold, 24,000 iron / stone / lumber, 36,000 food) in proportion to the damage their armies dealt, paid straight into their treasury when it falls. The target panel lists who wounded it most.
* `npm run sim:balance` (with Colossus Hide): a lone City Hall 20 march takes under 25% of a boss's HP; a rally of 4 City Hall 20 rulers kills any boss in 1–6 attempts (today, with full kits and rallies' 8 rounds: orcs 2–3, humans 3–4, elves 2–3, dwarves 2–4, losing about 20–50% of their soldiers a try, counting only the dead: an elf rally loses 23–35% dead, with as many wounded, §13 *Moonwell Healers*), and in no more tries than the same 4 marches attacking it apart (a try is 4 lone battles); a City Hall 15 ruler with a veteran's titles (+10%) sending every march slot takes at most 30% per battle (elf 2%, dwarf 4%, human 7%, Nethergarde 10%, orc 11%). It also prints that solo table at City Hall 15 and 25 with no titles, veteran and maxed (+37%) buffs: at City Hall 25 a lone ruler takes 12–18% (elf) to 45–62% (orc) per battle.
* NPC kingdoms (`npm run sim:balance`): a City Hall 10 one (a common realm at its top) takes 1–2 rallies of 4 City Hall 15 rulers, a City Hall 25 one (an epic realm at its top, with its epic Battle Arts kit) 1–9 (elves fastest, Nethergarde 4, dwarves 7, humans 8, orcs 9; before kits 2–10), and its kit never takes a rally more tries than its garrison without one; a single march loses about half its soldiers against one; after a failed rally a City Hall 15 kingdom regrows its garrison in ~12 h (orc, human donors), ~22 h (dwarf) or ~33 h (elf).

**🚩 NPC kingdoms** — the 4 buffer realms and the 4 frontier gates
* **✨ Battle Arts** (§13 *Battle Arts*): the home garrison fights with its civilization's kit, sized by the realm's rarity, which is its cycle stage (a frontier gate is rare): common 1 Instinct, uncommon + 1 Art, rare + a second Instinct, epic + a second Art. 👑 Human: Stoneskin → Shield Wall → Bloodlust → War Cry · 🛡️ Orc: Bloodlust → War Cry → Enrage → Armor Break · 🏹 Elf: Keen Eye → Frost Lance → Light-Footed → Second Wind · ⚒️ Dwarf: Stoneskin → Armor Break → Thick Hide → Shield Wall (Nethergarde garrisons, where they hold one: Undying → Fireball → Bloodlust → Thunderclap). Like a pack's, the kit is paid for out of the soldiers' HP and ATK (`kitTrimFor`, the soldiers' DEF behind their walls), so it changes how the garrison fights, never how hard it is to beat. Troops on gate duty fight with their own stats, without the gate's kit. Scout reports, the battle log and the Codex list the kit.
* **Simulated kingdoms** (buffer realms): the **City Hall of their 28-day cycle** (*Buffer realms* in §10: common 6–10, uncommon 11–15, rare 16–20, epic 21–25, a level a day, shielded on each stage's building day), the demographics and soldiers of a **bordering mortal civilization** picked at random, spawned at full population with a full garrison (that civ's soldier share) and a full treasury. **Each new level rebuilds it to full** (population, garrison, treasury, walls). The civilization stays for the realm's whole cycle; the next realm rolls its own. The frontier gates are fixed instead (below).
* **Attack or Rally** them like any castle: walls by level (City Hall 10 → tier 2, 12–19 → tier 3, 20–24 → tier 4, 25 → tier 5), wall HP, burning and the 8 h protection like any castle. Anyone may attack them (they count as another civilization for everyone), and they are always **fogged**: level, civilization, wall tier and burning are visible; garrison, population and treasury are unknown (**?**) until scouted, then show as `~scouted–estimate`.
* **Real losses**: a beaten garrison stays beaten until the realm's next level-up (a frontier gate: until it regrows). Every harvest they **regrow with their civilization's birth formula** (S-curve, no deaths of age); the newborn refill the garrison up to the civ's soldier share. Elf-borrowing kingdoms recover slowly, human ones fast.
* **Plunder by load** (§13 *Plunder*) from a treasury that holds per City Hall level 5,000 gold, 7,000 iron and stone, 8,000 lumber and 10,000 food, **× its tier** (common ×1, uncommon ×1.5, rare ×2.5, epic ×4: a City Hall 25 realm holds 500k gold, 700k iron and stone, 800k lumber and 1M food), refills 10% of that every harvest, and keeps the protected vault (1,500 × level per resource). **Never destroyed.** No War Frenzy.
* **👑 Conquest hoard**: beating a realm's garrison also wins its **conquest hoard**, paid **straight into the conquerors' treasuries** (no load needed), **split by the damage each ruler's armies dealt in that battle** (a ruler's bands and champion count for them); gems go by share too, what rounding leaves to the top dealer. It pays **once a day per realm** (24 h after it was won); a rally that breaks the garrison again that day still plunders the treasury. The march form shows it to everyone (`👑 Conquest hoard (epic): 320k resources + 5 gems · waiting…`, or when it is back), and the battle report lists each ruler's share. By tier (gems: one boss gem kind per realm):

  | Tier (City Hall) | Gold | Iron · Stone · Lumber | Food | Gems |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Common (6–10) | 3,000 | 4,000 each | 5,000 | — |
  | Uncommon (11–15) | 9,000 | 12,000 each | 15,000 | — |
  | Rare (16–20) | 22,000 | 30,000 each | 38,000 | 2 |
  | Epic (21–25) | 50,000 | 60,000 each | 90,000 | 5 (more than a world boss's hoard) |

  Frontier gates hold no hoard, and their treasury stays at the City Hall 20 rate.

**🚧 Frontier gates** — the keys to the mortal provinces
* **Fixed**: City Hall **20**, walls tier 3 with a fixed **12,000 HP** (DEF ×1.70), the treasury of a City Hall 20 NPC kingdom. Each belongs to its province's civilization (Valoria → human, Drak'mar → orc, Sylvandale → elf, Ironforge → dwarf) and is held by that civilization's soldiers.
* **Base garrison**, sized by combat power so a lone City Hall 15 ruler of any civilization, sending its 3 marches with no buffs, beats a bare gate in one assault (`npm run sim:gates`): **430** Royal Footmen (human), **490** Blood Berserkers (orc), **95** Ironbreaker Sentinels (dwarf), **45** Sylvan Rangers (elf). It regrows **50% of itself every harvest**, never above the base.
* **🚧 The seal**: while a gate is **not burning**, no other civilization can **attack or rally on a city in its province** (the march form says why). Burning it (walls below 25%) **opens the province** for the burn (the gate lies broken and burning on the map, §10), about **8 h** until the walls repair to 26%; armies already on the road when it repairs still arrive. Only cities are sealed: resource nodes, treasure, mobs and bosses in the province stay open to everyone. Nethergarde has no gate and no seal. A herald notice tells the realm when a gate falls (§14).
* **Its own civilization** can never attack or rally on it (the server refuses; the target panel greys Attack and Rally out) but can **scout** and **🛡️ reinforce** it. Burning a bare gate takes follow-up waves: a City Hall 15 human or orc army burns it in about 2 assaults, a dwarf one in 3, an elf one (few soldiers, so little wall damage) in about 9.
* **Gate duty**: troops of the gate's civilization stationed there fight beside its garrison in every attack, with their own civ's stats, titles and buffs, behind its walls. The gate holds its garrison plus troops on duty up to its **reinforce cap**: **4,000** (human) and **2,800** (orc), their max population at City Hall 20, and **800** (dwarf) and **380** (elf), trimmed because their soldiers are so strong that a fuller gate would hold off any rally. A full gate is beyond a lone ruler (except a City Hall 15 human swarm against a full elf gate: humans counter elves), but a rally of 5 City Hall 20 rulers of any civilization breaks any full gate (`npm run sim:gates`). Each soldier on duty costs its ruler **1 food, 1 lumber, 1 stone and 1 iron per harvest** (keeping the gate and its wall in shape) on top of the normal 1 food upkeep. A ruler who can't pay for them all keeps as many as they can pay for; the newest of the rest walk home (never mid-battle) and a notice says so. A burning gate takes no new reinforcements; troops already there stay. Troops whose ruler changes civilization leave.

**👑 The Archon of the Realm** — untouchable
* Never attacked, rallied on or scouted: the march form refuses (*The Archon of the Realm is untouchable*), and the castle panel shows **Untouchable** instead of a siege button. Respawns every harvest like an overseer, at City Hall 30.

**👑 Special Overseers** — a loot area
* They never march (no attack, rally, reinforce, scout or gather) and can't be reinforced, but **mortals can attack and rally on them** (Nethergarde can't — same civilization). Never fogged.
* **Respawn every harvest**: City Hall 25, full population, a garrison of **20% of their maximum soldiers** (`OVERSEER_GARRISON_SHARE`) and their treasury back to the preset (50k gold, 200k iron / stone / lumber, 500k food). Walls tier 5, which burn and repair like anyone's.
* **Plunder by load** from that real treasury. Beating one never swaps civilizations and causes no War Frenzy.
* **Overseer's Resolve**: every defeat this week adds a stack: **+20% HP, ATK and DEF** per stack, uncapped, reset **Sunday 00:00 UTC**. The castle panel shows the stacks and battle reports show what the garrison fought with. `npm run sim:balance`: a full City Hall 25 rally (leader + 5, 8 rounds) stops winning after about 3 (dwarves), 5 (orcs), 8 (humans) to 16 (elves) defeats a week.

### Battle Arts

Server: [battleArts.ts](server/src/battleArts.ts) (kits, Arts, statuses, cast order), run by `stepBattleRound` in [combat.ts](server/src/combat.ts); the Sparring Grounds in [sparring.ts](server/src/sparring.ts). Client copy of the rules the books quote: [battleArts.ts](src/utils/battleArts.ts).

Anything that fights as **a named unit** carries Battle Arts on top of its stats: today the **world bosses**, each with a full legendary kit, so armies without Arts of their own face them at a disadvantage, **every mob and bandit pack**, with a kit sized by its spawn's rarity that reshapes it without making it any harder to beat (*Mobs & bandits* below), and the garrison of every **free realm and frontier gate**, with its civilization's kit by the realm's stage (*NPC kingdoms* above). **Mercenary bands** carry a kit of their rarity themed to their civilization (*Mercenaries* below). **Champions** carry a legendary kit rolled for them, at a hero's share of their army's blow (*Champions* below). The mythical horror will carry them too; it is not in the game yet. Ordinary soldiers carry none.

* **🧠 Instincts** are always on. Stat Instincts add up among themselves and multiply on top of the unit's stats like titles (Bloodlust +15% ATK, Stoneskin +40% DEF, Thick Hide +30% HP; the wild's own under their names: 🐺 Pack Hunter, 🗡️ Cutthroat and 🎯 Keen Eye +15% ATK, 🦇 Swarm and 💀 Undying +30% HP, 🪶 Light-Footed +40% DEF); others act each round (Cleave's cut, Enrage below half strength).
* **✨ Arts** are cast. Each has a first round (`castRound`, the unit's own round counted from its arrival), a cooldown (cast in round r → ready in round r + cooldown + 1; some are once a battle), a target (the enemy front: the home garrison or the earliest-arrived army · all enemies, split by headcount · itself · its allies), its effects (a strike of a share of its own blow, a heal, a status) and maybe a condition (Second Wind needs 10% of the unit lost). **Arts have no rarity**: every one is tuned to one power budget; a rarer unit is stronger because it carries more of them.
* **🎺 The Finale** is a unit's ultimate: once per battle, in its own slot beside the round's Art, on its round (usually 5) **or** earlier as a **last stand** when its unit drops under 25% of its strength — never both. A Finale that breaks the last of an army gets its own line in the report and the log (*🎺 Ser Aldric's Finale — Dawnbreaker — broke the last of the Riftguard.*).

| Rarity | Instincts | Arts | Finale |
|---|---|---|---|
| Common | 1 | — | — |
| Uncommon | 1 | 1 | — |
| Rare | 2 | 1 | — |
| Epic | 2 | 2 | — |
| Legendary | 2 | 2 | 1 |
| Mythic | 3 | 3 | 1 |
| World boss (a colossus) | 3 (🪓 Cleave on every boss) | 2 | 1 |

**What is cast each round** (deterministic): before the blows, every unit with a kit casts (the defending garrison first, then the attacking armies in arrival order): its Finale if due, then **at most one Art**. Of the Arts past their first round, off cooldown and whose condition holds, one reaching its first round this round (never cast yet) wins; otherwise the one waiting longest; a tie goes by a roll seeded from the battle, the unit and the round, so the report and a replay always agree. A failed condition skips the Art **without** a cooldown. A stack joining late counts rounds from its own arrival. A wiped-out unit casts nothing more, but its Burns and Poisons tick out. Then the blows land (shaped by every status), the Instincts cut, and damage over time ticks.

**Statuses**: the same status from the same caster **refreshes**; from different casters it **stacks up to 3** (a fourth replaces the one closest to running out).

| Status | Effect |
|---|---|
| 🔥 Burn | Damage at the end of each round for 3 rounds |
| ☠️ Poison | Weaker, 5 rounds, and the same caster stacks it 3 deep |
| 🩸 Bleed | 3 rounds, each tick × (1 + the share its target has lost) |
| ⚡ Paralyze | −50% damage dealt the round it lands; on a boss −25%, and never two rounds in a row |
| ❄️ Chill / Freeze | −ATK for 2 rounds / no counter-strike for a round (a boss is never frozen, only Chilled) |
| 🛡️ Ward | Less damage taken from blows and strikes (own side) |
| 💢 Rally cry | +ATK for the caster's side |
| 💔 Sunder | −DEF |

**The Arts** (each worth the budget, ±15%): 🛡️ **Ward** (bosses; round 1, once: −80% damage taken that round) · 🔥 **Fireball** (a strike of 25% of its blow, then Burn) · ❄️ **Frost Lance** (a strike, then Chill −15%) · ☠️ **Venom Volley** (Poison on every enemy army, a stack deeper each cast) · 🩸 **Rending Blow** (a strike, then Bleed) · ⚡ **Thunderclap** (from round 2: a strike over every enemy army, then Paralyze) · ❄️ **Glacial Prison** (from round 2, every 5 rounds: Freeze the enemy front) · 🛡️ **Shield Wall** (Ward −30% for 2 rounds) · 💢 **War Cry** (Rally cry +30% ATK for 2 rounds, every army of its side) · 💔 **Armor Break** (Sunder −35% DEF for 3 rounds) · 💚 **Second Wind** (once, from round 2, once 10% is lost: 60% of its fallen rise again). The wild's own: 🗡️ **Ambush** (round 1, once: a strike of 72% of its blow on the enemy front) · 🦂 **Venom Sting** and ☠️ **Poisoned Blade** (a strike of 15%, then Poison) · 💚 **Regenerate** (from round 2, every 3 rounds, once 10% is lost: 20% of its fallen stand again) · 👻 **Dread Wail** (Paralyze every enemy army) · 🐉 **Fire Breath** (a strike of 20% over every enemy army, then Burn) · 🌀 **Void Lash** (a strike of 10%, then Sunder −25% DEF) · 👁️ **Stone Gaze** (from round 2, every 5 rounds: Freeze the enemy front, turned to stone) · 💀 **Raise the Dead** (from round 2, every 4 rounds, once 15% is lost: 40% of its fallen rise) · 💣 **Blackpowder Keg** (from round 2: a strike of 40% over every enemy army, then Burn). **Finales**: 🌅 **Dawnbreaker** (round 5: a sweep of 220% of its blow over every enemy army) · ☄️ **Cataclysm** (round 5: 180% over every enemy army, then Burn) · each world boss's own ult (*World bosses* above: Abyssal Grasp, Maelstrom, Siren's Requiem, Devouring Maw, Sandstorm, Stinger Storm, Grasping Roots, Wild Hunt, Glamour of the Court, Dragonfire, Avalanche, Petrifying Gaze).

**How a cast looks** (client: [SpellFx.tsx](src/components/3d/SpellFx.tsx), the looks in [spellFx.ts](src/utils/spellFx.ts)): every Art, Finale and Instinct that shows has a look on one shared spell-effects kit, played over its battle on the world map (and in the Sparring Grounds) as its name rises. A look picks one option for each of six stages:

| Stage | Options (Fireball's first) |
|---|---|
| Charge | its colour pulled out of the air into the caster's hand · motes spiralling in · light rising off the ground · none |
| Core | a hot ball swelling in the hand · a spike of ice · a glowing edge · a flare over the caster · none |
| Trail | tongues of flame behind the bolt · glittering frost · venom flung behind three bolts · a thin streak · none (it lands at once) |
| Burst | a flash and sparks over the enemy · shards · a green splash · a cut of light across their line · lightning from above · a dome of shields · a ring of light · a sunrise sweeping rays · burning stones from the sky · a whirlpool · a wall of sand · spectral wolves · a cone of fire |
| Reaction | the enemy flashes white and flinches · the enemy is jolted · the caster's side glows, lifted |
| Aftermath | the status it leaves, played on its army while it holds (Fireball: burning) |

| Art / Finale | Look |
|---|---|
| 🔥 Fireball | a fireball with a flame trail, a flash, then burning |
| ❄️ Frost Lance | a spike of ice trailing frost, shattering, then chilled |
| ☠️ Venom Volley | three venom bolts arcing over, a green splash on each army, then poisoned |
| 🩸 Rending Blow | a glowing edge, a cut of light across the line, then bleeding |
| ⚡ Thunderclap | motes spiral in, two forks of lightning, then paralyzed |
| ❄️ Glacial Prison | rising frost, a spike of ice shattering, then frozen |
| 🛡️ Ward / Shield Wall | light rising, a dome of shields closing round the caster, then warded |
| 💢 War Cry | a flare, a ring of light bursting out, then rallied |
| 💔 Armor Break | a violet edge, shards bursting off the enemy, then sundered |
| 💚 Second Wind | green light rising, a ring of light, the side lifted |
| 🗡️ Ambush | a grey edge, a cut of light across the line at once |
| 🦂 Venom Sting · ☠️ Poisoned Blade | a sting (a green edge) flung with venom, a splash (a cut), then poisoned |
| 💚 Regenerate · 💀 Raise the Dead | green light rising, a ring of light, the pack lifted |
| 👻 Dread Wail | motes spiral in, a pale ring bursts out over the enemy, then paralyzed |
| 🐉 Fire Breath | heat gathers, then a short, narrow jet of fire over the enemy, then burning |
| 🌀 Void Lash | violet motes spiral in, a cut across the line, then sundered |
| 👁️ Stone Gaze | grey light gathers, a wall of dust over the enemy, then frozen |
| 💣 Blackpowder Keg | a lit keg with a flame trail, burning blasts raining down, then burning |
| 🌅 Dawnbreaker | a sun rises over the caster and sweeps its rays down over every enemy army |
| ☄️ Cataclysm | burning stones rain from the sky, each with its flash, then burning |
| 🌊 Maelstrom | a whirlpool of water spins round the enemy, then chilled |
| 🌪️ Sandstorm | a wall of dark sand swirls over the enemy, then sundered |
| 🐺 Wild Hunt | spectral wolves run in from behind the boss and through the enemy, then bleeding |
| 🐉 Dragonfire | two long sweeps of fire over every enemy army, the ground left burning with flames and embers climbing off it, then burning |
| 🐙 Abyssal Grasp | violet motes spiral in, tentacles burst up through the enemy, sway and sink back, then frozen |
| 🎵 Siren's Requiem | a flare, rings of song roll out over the enemy with notes drifting through them, then paralyzed |
| 🪱 Devouring Maw | the ground gapes under the enemy front in a cloud of grit, a ring of teeth closes round it |
| 🦂 Stinger Storm | a green shard grows, stingers rain straight down, each splashing venom, then poisoned |
| 🌳 Grasping Roots | green light rising, roots race along the ground and thorns spear up through the enemy, then bleeding |
| 🧚 Glamour of the Court | a pink flare, a whirl of glowing petals wraps the enemy and drifts down, then chilled |
| 🏔️ Avalanche | snow and ice pour down from beyond the enemy, across it, into a white cloud, then chilled |
| 👁️ Petrifying Gaze | green light gathers, twin beams from the boss's eyes, the enemy crusts over in grey stone, then frozen |
| 🪓 Cleave (Instinct) | a cut of light across the attackers' line, every round |
| 💢 Enrage (Instinct) | a red flare and ring off the boss as it wakes |

Each status has its look while it holds (until the next round shows whether it still does): 🔥 **burning**, flames licking up through the ranks and embers rising · ☠️ **poisoned**, green bubbles in a sickly haze · 🩸 **bleeding**, red drops falling · ⚡ **paralyzed**, sparks crackling between the soldiers · ❄️ **chilled**, frost drifting down in a pale mist · ❄️ **frozen**, ice crystals standing round them · 🛡️ **warded**, a golden shimmer of shields · 💢 **rallied**, red-gold sparks streaming up · 💔 **sundered**, violet flakes of armour falling away. A Finale is bigger, with a war horn under its whoosh. **A boss casting its Finale, or whose Enrage wakes, rears up in a battle cry** (its roar, in Normal and HD); a boss's casts breathe embers and sparks, never a pillar of light. A cast sounds (a whoosh as it is cast, a boom as it lands; *Interactive Audio* below) only in battles near the camera. Every look is drawn with one batch of GPU particles: one draw call for every cast on screen, none when nothing is up.

**Mobs & bandits** (server: `kit` in `WORLD_NPC_UNITS`, `npcKitFor` and `npcKitWorth` in [warConfig.ts](server/src/warConfig.ts)): every roving pack fights with a kit sized by its spawn's rarity, themed to the creature, never a boss's (a pack is many units, so a boss's Paralyze and Freeze rules don't apply). The kit **doesn't make a pack harder to beat, only different**: what it is worth comes off the pack's own stats. Its offence (ATK Instincts, Enrage, Arts that strike or wound) comes off its ATK, its defence (HP and DEF Instincts, Arts that ward, heal or hold the enemy back) off its HP; an Art counts ×1.3 (a first-round opener such as Ambush ×1.9, since a pack seldom lives past its second round; one that guards ×1.4; a second Art half that, since a unit casts one Art a round), Enrage ×1.15. So a Dire Wolf fights at ATK 6.1 + Pack Hunter instead of a flat 7, and a Werewolf at a little less ATK with Rending Blow and Enrage. `npm run sim:balance` checks it pack by pack (below). Scout reports and the Codex list each pack's kit; its casts and ticks rise over its battles and show in the report like a boss's.

| Rarity | Mobs | Bandits |
|---|---|---|
| Common (1 Instinct) | Slime: Thick Hide · Dire Wolf: Pack Hunter · Cave Bat: Swarm · Sewer Rat: Swarm · Venomweb Spider: Bloodlust | Highwayman: Cutthroat · Pickpocket: Light-Footed · Poacher: Keen Eye |
| Uncommon (1 + 1 Art) | Goblin: Cutthroat · Ambush · Skeleton: Undying · Shield Wall · Dune Scorpion: Stoneskin · Venom Sting · Stormcrag Harpy: Bloodlust · Thunderclap · Lizardfolk: Thick Hide · Rending Blow | Smuggler: Cutthroat · Poisoned Blade · Sand Raider: Bloodlust · Ambush · River Pirate: Bloodlust · Ambush |
| Rare (2 + 1 Art) | Mountain Troll: Thick Hide, Enrage · Regenerate · Shadow Chimera: Bloodlust, Thick Hide · Fireball · Moonfang Werewolf: Pack Hunter, Enrage · Rending Blow · Ogre Brute: Thick Hide, Bloodlust · Armor Break · Wailing Wraith: Undying, Bloodlust · Dread Wail | Corsair: Cutthroat, Thick Hide · War Cry · Syndicate Blade: Cutthroat, Keen Eye · Poisoned Blade · Ashen Cultist: Bloodlust, Stoneskin · Fireball |
| Epic (2 + 2 Arts) | Emberwing Drake: Thick Hide, Enrage · Fire Breath, Rending Blow · Void Aberration: Stoneskin, Bloodlust · Void Lash, Thunderclap · Magma Elemental: Stoneskin, Thick Hide · Fireball, Armor Break · Stone-Gaze Basilisk: Stoneskin, Thick Hide · Stone Gaze, Venom Sting · Ancient Lich: Undying, Bloodlust · Frost Lance, Raise the Dead | Dread Mercenary: Stoneskin, Bloodlust · Shield Wall, Rending Blow · Iron Horde Warrior: Stoneskin, Enrage · War Cry, Shield Wall · Blackpowder Saboteur: Keen Eye, Thick Hide · Blackpowder Keg, Ambush |

**A pack strikes back on the map** (client: [npcStrikes.ts](src/components/3d/world/npcStrikes.ts), moved in the NPC batch's vertex shader in [InstancedParts.tsx](src/components/3d/city/InstancedParts.tsx)): while a pack fights a live battle, every round its creatures **strike at the armies**: each coils back and crouches, lunges at them with the blow landing mid-swing, follows through and settles. When the pack casts (an Art, or Enrage waking), they **rear back** first, rise and hold it a trembling beat, then hurl themselves forward as the spell flies. Each creature takes its own beat, reach and lean from its place and the round, so no two strike alike; the camp around them stands still. It plays in Normal and HD alike, on the shared batch's own draws (no draw call per pack), and the moving creatures cast no shadow.

**Rally battles last up to 8 rounds** (every other battle 6), room for a Finale to land; `npm run sim:balance` keeps a rally better than the same armies arriving separately (*Balance targets* below).

**`npm run sim:arts`** fights a 6-round duel in the open on the live engine: a drill band of 1,000 plain soldiers with the kit against the same band without, the same rolls with and without, and measures the kit's worth (extra damage dealt + damage not taken + HP healed, in rounds of the band's own opening blow). It must end ⚖️ BALANCED: **every Art within ±15% of the power budget (1.25 rounds of blow over 6 rounds)**; the six drill kits (the Sparring Grounds' bands) climb a **smooth curve**, common < uncommon < rare < epic < legendary < mythic, no step more than 3× another (today 0.95 → 2.46 → 3.36 → 4.44 → 6.81 → 8.03); **Mythic never beats Legendary by more than the ladder gap** (the Legendary − Epic step: today +1.22 vs +2.37); every kit fits its rarity's sizes (drill bands, bosses, every mob and bandit, each carrying a full kit of its spawn's rarity and none a boss's, and every NPC kingdom garrison at every stage); a battle fought twice from the same seed casts and ticks the same; the client's copy of the rules matches the server; and every Art, Finale, showing Instinct and status has its look on the spell-effects kit, named as the server names it and leaving the status it lays.

**World bosses carry full kits**: their old skills moved onto the engine first with unchanged strength (`npm run sim:balance` gave byte-identical output), then each got a full legendary kit (2 Instincts, 2 Arts, an ult of its own), so an army without Arts is at a disadvantage: a lone City Hall 20 march now takes about half the HP it did (human 8% → 4%), and a rally of 4 loses more (humans ~36% → ~40% a try) and needs more tries (humans 2.3 → 3.0, dwarves 4.0 → 5.1 on average), inside the 1–6 target (*World bosses* above). Then every boss got a **third Instinct**: Cleave for the five that had none (the Siren, the Sphinx, the Treant, the Shadow Wolf and the Yeti, whose blows had reached only the front army, so a rally lost as little as 4–9% a try), and another for the seven that had it (Enrage, Bloodlust, Stoneskin or Thick Hide). Kills take about as many tries as before (a rally of 4 City Hall 20 rulers: 2–4), and every boss now costs a rally about 25–50% of its soldiers a try (an elf rally up to ~70% against the Shadow Wolf and the Scorpion).

### Mercenaries

Server: [mercenaryRules.ts](server/src/mercenaryRules.ts) (the pure rules: rarities, power, kits, prices, camps, healing), [mercenaries.ts](server/src/mercenaries.ts) (camps, hiring, upkeep, wounds, healing, desertion, death); bands march and fight through [warEngine.ts](server/src/warEngine.ts). Client copy of the numbers the books quote: [mercenaries.ts](src/utils/mercenaries.ts). Camp: [MercenaryCampModal.tsx](src/components/ui/MercenaryCampModal.tsx).

**A band is one whole march.** A mercenary band takes **one march slot** on its own: none of your soldiers march with it, no rations are paid (its upkeep feeds it), it costs **no population** and doesn't count against the soldier cap. Its power is **one full march of its hirer × its rarity's power**, where a full march is the march you field from every slot: your soldier cap (at full population) shared over your march slots, never more than your march capacity, at the moment you hire it (a City Hall 25 human: 500; their march capacity is 1,000, but 5 slots of soldiers are 500 each). **Bands take the march slots your soldiers can't fill**: at full population no civilization's soldier cap fills all its slots (at City Hall 25: Nethergarde about 3 of 5 full marches, orcs 2.8, humans and dwarves 2.5, elves 1.3), so a band adds its power on top of the army you have. It fights as that many of your civilization's soldiers (their traits and civilization buffs, never your milestone titles or the Dragon Statue / Mana Spire / Plaza Fountain) with its own **Battle Arts** of its rarity, themed to its civilization; the soldier-equivalents it fields are set so that the band **with its Arts** lands on its power (`npm run sim:balance`). The rarer the band, the fewer models walk in it and the stronger each one is.

| Rarity | Models | Power (× a full march) | Battle Arts | Hired with | Hire price | Upkeep (every harvest) | Heals from 0% in |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Common | 20 | 0.9× | 1 Instinct | any slots | 1,000 gold · 20,000 food | 3 gold · 150 food | 1 h |
| Uncommon | 15 | 1.0× | 1 Instinct · 1 Art | any slots | 3,000 gold · 40,000 food · 10,000 iron | 6 gold · 300 food | 2 h |
| Rare | 10 | 1.1× | 2 Instincts · 1 Art | any slots | 8,000 gold · 80,000 food · 30,000 iron | 15 gold · 600 food · 80 iron | 4 h |
| Epic | 5 | 1.25× | 2 Instincts · 2 Arts | **4+ march slots** (City Hall 16+) | 20,000 gold · 150,000 food · 80,000 iron | 40 gold · 1,500 food · 200 iron | 8 h |
| Legendary | 1 | 1.4× | 2 Instincts · 2 Arts · a Finale | **5 march slots** (City Hall 21+) | 50,000 gold · 300,000 food · 200,000 iron | 100 gold · 3,000 food · 500 iron | 12 h |

**Each civilization's band kit** (a band carries the first slots by its rarity: Instinct 1, then Art 1, Instinct 2, Art 2, the Finale): Valoria 🪨 Stoneskin, 🩸 Bloodlust · 🛡️ Shield Wall, 💚 Second Wind · 🌅 Dawnbreaker; Sylvandale 🎯 Keen Eye, 🪶 Light-Footed · ❄️ Frost Lance, ☠️ Venom Volley · 🌳 Grasping Roots; Ironforge 🪨 Stoneskin, 🛡️ Thick Hide · 💔 Armor Break, 💣 Blackpowder Keg · 🏔️ Avalanche; Drak'mar 🩸 Bloodlust, 💢 Enrage · 🩸 Rending Blow, 🗡️ Ambush · 🐺 Wild Hunt; Nethergarde 💀 Undying, 🎯 Keen Eye · 🔥 Fireball, ⚡ Thunderclap · ☄️ Cataclysm. Each band's Arts are only its own: none lifts the armies beside it (a War Cry would), so a band adds its power and no more.

* **⛺ The camps.** Your own civilization's legendary landmark is your **Mercenary Camp**: Valoria's Cathedral of the Dawn Banner, Sylvandale's Heart-Oak, Ironforge's Anvil of the Gods, Drak'mar's Bloodfire Throne, Nethergarde's Core. Hovering it says *Mercenary Camp - Click to Hire*; clicking it opens the camp, with the landmark's story at its top. Another civilization's landmark (and every landmark for a visitor who isn't signed in) opens as before, to scout and read its lore: you only hire from your own people. Each camp keeps its own stock, shared by every ruler of its civilization, **first come first served**: a new recruit of each rarity every **15 min** (common, up to 10 waiting), **30 min** (uncommon, 7), **1 h** (rare, 4), **4 h** (epic, 2) and **once a day** (legendary, 1); a camp nobody has hired from yet starts full. A band arrives as your civilization's mercenary of that rarity, at home and whole. A rarity you can't hire shows greyed out with the reason (*Needs 5 march slots*, *You already hold a band*, *None waiting*). The camp also lists **your bands**, their strength, and their heal and dismiss buttons; so does your kingdom window's **🪙 Mercenaries** tab (👥 Kingdom → Mercenaries, yours only), wherever you are on the map.
* **Holding bands.** At most **1 band with 1–3 march slots, 2 with 4–5**, whether at home, marching, wounded or healing; one that dies, deserts or is dismissed frees its place. If your slots drop (or you change civilization) you **keep the bands you have**; you just can't hire above your gate or past your cap again.
* **Civilization changes.** A band carries over **one to one**: once it is home it becomes your new civilization's mercenary of the same rarity, with its wounds, fighting as the new civilization's soldiers in the number that makes the same share of a full march at your City Hall.
* **What a band does.** Bands **attack** (rulers, open Voidrift slots, mobs, bandits, free realms and frontier gates, exposed gatherers) and **defend their hirer's castle**; they don't scout, gather, rally or reinforce anyone else. On a **world boss** a band keeps **the Sellsword's Oath**: it lands only **30% of its blows** (after Colossus Hide; its Arts still weaken the beast), and the battle log says so (`🪙 The Sellsword's Oath: Ser Aldric's Ironbreaker in Steam Plate lands 12% of its blows on the colossus (Colossus Hide 41% × the Oath 30%)`). Every free company swears it at its camp — *our steel is for hire, our graves are not* — and before a colossus fights from the flanks, hamstringing and harrying with a road home kept open. Bosses are raid content: bands help a ruler's soldiers there, but one ruler with their bands never kills a boss in one battle. Tick it in the march form's **Muster** (§13 *Launching marches: the Muster*), alone or beside your soldier marches and your other band. In a battle the defenders strike **a ruler's own soldiers before their sellswords** (by arrival among each), so a tough band can't shield a whole army from the blows. A band plunders by its standing soldier-equivalents like any army.
* **🛡️ On your walls.** When your castle is attacked, every band **at home** (whole or wounded; not healing, not away on a march) takes the walls beside your garrison, oldest band first, if your treasury pays its **battle pay** right then (below). It defends as its own group, like a reinforcement: its soldier-equivalents behind your walls (Walls DEF), with its Battle Arts and never your titles, landmarks or Armory sets; your champion leads only the garrison. Its losses are strength, and wiped out it dies. A band the treasury can't pay **stays inside** and the battle log says so (`🪙 🌙 Moonwell Oracle won't take the walls unpaid (battle pay 15 gold, 600 food, 80 iron): the band stays inside`). While it holds the walls it can't march, heal or be dismissed; when the battle ends it is back inside. A band that deserts over unpaid upkeep leaves the walls mid-battle. On the walls a band is worth its rarity's power in soldiers, as in the field (`npm run sim:balance`). A band that comes home during a battle stays out of it.
* **🪙 Battle pay.** Every fight costs a band **one harvest's upkeep on the spot**, on top of its upkeep: when it **marches out** to attack, and when it **takes your walls** in a defence (common 3 gold · 150 food, … legendary 100 gold · 3,000 food · 500 iron). A treasury that can't pay keeps it out of that fight: it won't march (the march form says so), and it stays inside while your walls are attacked. The camp and your bands list it as *per fight*.
* **🩹 Strength, wounds and death.** A band's losses are **strength**: 0–100% of its full count. It fights at the strength it has; battle numbers over it on the map, the report (its card and its line in the armies list) and the March Queue show its losses as **−18%**, never as soldiers. When bands fight beside soldiers, the report's side card counts the **soldiers only** and lists each band under them by strength (`Eclipse Reaver 99% −2%`), the War Room's report list reads `70 −2% −2%`, and reports and herald notices name the hirer, not the band. A battle's log tells each ruler's titles, set and boss shares once, not once per march or per wave; a line that would repeat in one round (two alike bands casting the same Art, two alike boxes arriving together) is told once and counted (`🔥 Eclipse Reaver casts Fireball on Gorgon Stonecrusher (×2).`), and armies that have fought their rounds fall back in one line per ruler and per band (`⏳ Ser Aldric's 2 armies have fought 6 rounds and fall back.`). At **0%** it is **dead**, gone for good (a herald notice tells its owner). The 🌪️ Tempest Roads take the same share of a band's strength on the road as of an army's soldiers.
* **Healing.** Healing always costs resources and time: **the share of strength lost × 50% of the hire price**, and **the share lost × the rarity's full-heal time** (above). A healing band is home and can't march; stopping the heal keeps what it healed so far and refunds nothing. A wounded band not healing may march at its current strength, and the march form warns you.
* **🪙 Upkeep and desertion.** Every harvest each band costs its upkeep (home, marching, on your walls, wounded or healing), oldest band first. A band whose upkeep your treasury can't pay is **warned once** (a herald notice); unpaid again at the next harvest, it **deserts** (from the battlefield, if it is marching). **Dismiss** a band at your camp to send it away: no refund, its place is free. A band away on a march or on your walls can be dismissed once it is home.
* **On the map.** In high graphics a band marches as **its own models**, as many as its strength keeps standing (20, 15, 10, 5 or the legendary alone at full strength; a band at 75% shows 8 of its 10). Every model of every band is drawn by one batch, carried along its road on its root bone, walking, fighting at the walls and falling back, so more models cost vertices and a pose each, never a draw. In Normal graphics a band marches as **low-poly copies of its own mercenaries** (their Normal models from the Royal Codex, kept to their biggest parts), one figure per standing model (up to 12), in wider ranks than soldiers, at no extra draw cost. Its badge reads its name and strength (*Lantern Friar 75%*).
* **Notices and the weekly story.** A legendary hire is announced to the whole realm; a band's death, an upkeep warning and a desertion go to its owner (§14). Mercenary deaths and legendary hires are happenings of the weekly story (§15).

### The Armory

Server: [armoryRules.ts](server/src/armoryRules.ts) (the pure rules: sets, power, costs, upkeep, the target shares), [armory.ts](server/src/armory.ts) (forging, equipping, upkeep and rust); sets fight through [combat.ts](server/src/combat.ts) and [warEngine.ts](server/src/warEngine.ts). Client copy of the numbers: [armory.ts](src/utils/armory.ts). Panel: [ArmoryPanel.tsx](src/components/ui/ArmoryPanel.tsx).

**Quality instead of quantity.** Every civilization forges three sets of war gear of its own, **uncommon, rare and epic**. A march in a set fights like **1.08×, 1.16× or 1.25×** the same march in standard gear, and a set costs what that power would cost in extra soldiers: a Paid Settler armed is 335 food's worth (60 food + 1 gold + 2 iron + 5 food, with gold worth 250 food, iron 10 and lumber 2.5 by how scarce the harvest pool makes them, §7) and eats 1 food a harvest, so an epic set (+25%) costs about a quarter of that to forge and keep. Sets never let a kingdom field more soldiers; they let its soldiers hit harder once its march slots are full.

| Set | Power | Forged from | Forging 100 sets | Upkeep per 100 sets, every harvest |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Uncommon | 1.08× | City Hall 6 (2 march slots) | 150 iron · 300 lumber · 2 gold | 8 food |
| Rare | 1.16× | City Hall 11 (3 march slots) | 300 iron · 400 lumber · 5 gold | 12 food · 0.4 iron |
| Epic | 1.25× | City Hall 16 (4 march slots) | 400 iron · 300 lumber · 6 gold + a gem of the civilization (Valoria 0.07 💎 Flawless Diamond, Sylvandale 0.07 🟢 Sylvan Emerald, Ironforge 0.21 🪙 Dwarven Mithril, Drak'mar 0.07 🔴 Blood Ruby, Nethergarde 0.18 ⚡ Aether Shard) | 15 food · 1 iron |

Totals are rounded up (1,000 epic sets for humans cost 4,000 iron, 3,000 lumber, 60 gold and 1 diamond).

**Every civilization's sets.** A set's gains multiply its soldiers' HP, ATK and DEF on top of the civilization's buffs and debuffs and the ruler's milestone titles; the rare and epic sets of most civilizations also sharpen the civilization's own edge:

| | Uncommon | Rare | Epic |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Valoria** | 🔱 Pikes & Brigandines: +5% HP, +5% ATK, +20% DEF | 🛡️ Knightly Plate: +10% HP, +6% ATK, +45% DEF | ☀️ Sunforged Arms: +17% HP, +20% ATK, +34% DEF, +17% ATK while outnumbering the foe (Swarm Tactics) |
| **Sylvandale** | 🏹 Living-wood Bows: +4% HP, +10% ATK | 🌙 Moonsilver Mail: +12% HP, +4% ATK, +30% DEF, pierces 5% more armour | ✨ Starshot Quivers: +30% HP, +12% ATK, pierces 8% more armour (Arcane Precision) |
| **Ironforge** | 🪓 Riveted Axes: +4% HP, +10% ATK | ⚒️ Runeforged Plate: +8% HP, +4% ATK, +20% DEF, takes 7% less damage | 🔨 Anvil-of-Ages Hammers: +10% HP, +24% ATK, takes 10% less damage (Shield Wall) |
| **Drak'mar** | 🦴 Bone Cleavers: +4% HP, +10% ATK | 🛡️ Blackiron Hides: +12% HP, +4% ATK, +30% DEF, +5% ATK in round 1 | 🔥 Bloodfire War-gear: +24% HP, +19% ATK, +5% ATK in round 1 (Frenzy Charge) |
| **Nethergarde** | 🗡️ Void-touched Blades: +4% HP, +10% ATK | 🔮 Riftglass Wards: +10% HP, +4% ATK, +20% DEF, takes 20% less damage in round 1 | 🌑 Eclipse Panoply: +19% HP, +19% ATK, +19% DEF |

* **Forging.** Your kingdom window's **🗡️ Armory** tab (👥 Kingdom → Armory, yours only) shows your civilization's three sets with how many hang at home and how many are away on marches, what they add, this harvest's upkeep and a **Forge** button with the cost of the number you pick (a full march is one click). A set your City Hall is too low for shows why (*Needs City Hall 16*); sets already forged are kept if your City Hall ever drops. Forging counts as a game action (§7 play streak).
* **Loadout.** Every soldier box of the **Muster** (an ⚔️ attack or a 🚩 rally you lead, §13 *Launching marches: the Muster*), and the form for joining a rally, has a **Loadout** picker: **Standard gear** (the stock pile every soldier is armed from) or one of your sets. Every soldier of the march wears one, so you need as many sets at home as soldiers you send; the Muster shows the shortfall and **forges them right there** (quantity, cost, what you can afford, a confirm). Mercenary bands, scouts, gatherers and reinforcements march in their own gear.
* **Losses.** Soldiers who fall on a march lose their sets on the enemy's field; the survivors bring theirs home and hang them back in the armory when they reach home. A Voidrift civilization swap that rescales a marching army never brings home more sets than it took.
* **The garrison.** When your castle is attacked, your home garrison defends in the sets still at home, the best first: as many soldiers as there are sets, the rest in standard gear (the gains blend by the share wearing each). Its fallen are carried home, sets and all. Gatherers in the field and reinforcements stationed elsewhere defend in standard gear.
* **Where steel counts less.** Forged sets are made for wars between kingdoms. Against the realm's raid content a set counts only part of its gains: **🐉 Forged for Armies**, 30% against a world boss (after Colossus Hide and any civilization's own boss share), and **🏯 Murder Holes**, 10% against a frontier gate. With them no lone City Hall 25 ruler kills a world boss in one battle in their best set, and a full gate that holds against a lone ruler in standard gear still holds in their best set (`npm run sim:balance`).
* **Upkeep and rust.** Every set you hold, at home or on a march, costs its upkeep every harvest, epic first. What the treasury can't pay **rusts back to the stock pile** at once: the sets at home first, then whole marches' sets (the smallest army first; it fights on in standard gear from its next round, and its battle log says so). A herald notice tells you (§14).
* **Scrapping.** Sets you no longer want can be **scrapped** at home: in the Armory tab, **Scrap…** on a set's card, pick how many (or **All**) and confirm. They are broken up for good and **nothing comes back**, and from the next harvest they cost no upkeep. Sets away on a march can be scrapped once they are home.
* **Civilization changes.** Sets are kept by rarity: after a civilization change your sets are your new civilization's sets of the same rarity.
* **In battle.** The battle log names each army's set and what it adds the first round it fights (`☀️ Ser Aldric's troops march in Sunforged Arms (+17% HP, +20% ATK, +34% DEF · +17% ATK while outnumbering the foe)`, with the share that counts against a boss or a gate), and how many of a garrison wear sets. The report's armies list shows each army's set.
* **On the map.** Soldiers carry their set on the rarity ladder: **uncommon** its accent colour on a tabard (a sash over a robe) and a small prop (Valoria's pennant on the spear, a leaf in a Sylvandale hood, a plume on an Ironforge helm, a war-tail on a Drak'mar helm, a star on a Nethergarde hat); **rare** that accent with metal pauldrons and a crest (steel, moonsilver, polished steel, blackiron, riftglass); **epic** rare's trim with a cape behind the back and glowing details (Valoria's sunstone and shield boss, Sylvandale's starlit arrows, Ironforge's axe rune, Drak'mar's red-hot cleaver edge, a shard over Nethergarde's staff, and a gem on the chest). With HD graphics the gear is drawn through the HD model kit (real cloth, steel, gold, iron and gem; the cape trails the walk), riding the chest, head and hands it sits on. An armed soldier uses the shapes the soldiers already use, so an armed march costs the map no new draw. Each set is in the Royal Codex under **Creatures → The Armory**.

### Champions

Server: [championRules.ts](server/src/championRules.ts) (the pure rules: levels, kit unlocks, stat bonus, pools, looks, Renown, wounds, costs, the target shares), [champions.ts](server/src/champions.ts) (the hero, its Renown ledger, wounds, hastening, re-rolls, retraining); champions fight through [combat.ts](server/src/combat.ts) and [warEngine.ts](server/src/warEngine.ts). Client copy of the numbers: [champion.ts](src/utils/champion.ts). Card: [ChampionPanel.tsx](src/components/ui/ChampionPanel.tsx). Model: [heroChampions.ts](src/components/3d/world/heroChampions.ts).

**One hero per kingdom.** Every kingdom has one **champion**, a legendary hero rolled for it once: its name (from its civilization at birth), its look and its Battle Arts. It carries a legendary kit, **2 Instincts, 2 Arts and a Finale**, and it **falls but never dies**.

* **Leading a march.** Every soldier box of the **Muster** (an ⚔️ attack or a 🚩 rally you lead), and the form for joining a rally, has a **🦸 Lead with champion** switch (off by default; one box at a time). The champion leads **one march at a time**: while it is away the switch is greyed out on every other march (*already leads another march*), and it never leads a mercenary band, scouts, gatherers or reinforcements. The march it leads fights with its Battle Arts and a **flat stat bonus: +0.3% HP, ATK and DEF a level** (+9% at level 30), multiplied on top of the civilization's buffs, the ruler's titles, the rally bonus and an Armory set. The battle log names it the first round it fights.
* **A hero's arm.** A champion is one hero in an army, not a band: its Arts strike, its statuses bite and its Instincts lift its army at **13%** of what the same kit carried by a whole band would (the band's Arts strike with the whole band's blow). Its Finale fires as any Finale does (its round, or as a last stand when the army it leads drops under 25%).
* **At home it leads the defence.** While the champion is home and fit, it leads your **home garrison** when your castle is attacked (its kit and bonus on the garrison; stationed reinforcements fight without it). Away on a march or wounded, the garrison defends without it; one that comes home during a battle joins its next round.
* **Falls, never dies.** When the army it leads is wiped out (or the home garrison it leads), the champion **falls, wounded**, and is carried home. It heals by itself in **2 h + 10 min a level** (5 h 20 m at level 20). **Hasten** heals it at once for 150 gold and 4,000 food for every hour still to heal (from level 20 also one gem of your civilization, the Armory's epic gem). A wounded champion can't lead.
* **Levels 1–30, never above the City Hall.** A champion rises on **Renown** from the battles it leads (attacks, rallies and the defences of your castle), and only those: **2 a round** its army fought, **2 × √(enemies its army killed)** (at most 30), **15 for a win**, **60 for a world boss felled**, **40 for a realm felled** (an NPC kingdom or a Voidrift garrison broken, or a ruler's castle set burning) and **20 for every enemy army its Finale broke**. Renown thins through a realm day (`REALM_TIMEZONE`): the first 200 count whole, up to 500 half, past that a quarter. Level `L` needs 20 × (L − 1)² Renown in all (level 10: 1,620; level 20: 7,220; level 30: 16,820). A champion is never above its kingdom's City Hall level: Renown it earns at the cap is **🏦 banked** and counts once the City Hall rises.
* **Its kit unlocks by level**: level 1 its first Instinct, level 5 its first Art, level 10 its second Art, level 15 its second Instinct, level 20 its Finale.

| Slot | Unlocks at | What it can know (rolled once; retrained for gold and iron) |
|---|---|---|
