| Instincts (two different) | 1 and 15 | Thick Hide, Bloodlust, Stoneskin |
| Arts (two different) | 5 and 10 | Fireball, Frost Lance, Venom Volley, Rending Blow, Thunderclap, Shield Wall, War Cry, Armor Break, Second Wind, Ambush, Blackpowder |
| Finale | 20 | Dawnbreaker, Cataclysm, Siren's Requiem, Stinger Storm, Grasping Roots, Fae Glamour, Avalanche, Maelstrom, Sandstorm, Wild Hunt, Dragonfire |

No champion knows a Freeze (Glacial Prison, Stone Gaze, Abyssal Grasp, Petrifying Gaze): a hero's arm can't hold a whole army frozen.

* **Where a hero counts less.** Against the realm's hardest walls and beasts a champion counts only part of its worth (its stat bonus and its Arts' share alike): **🏯 Marked from the Gatehouse**, a tenth against a frontier gate (every crossbow on the walls looks for the hero under the banner), and **🐉 Legends of the Wild**, 30% against an epic or legendary roving pack (beasts and outlaws of legend don't break before a hero's banner). The battle log says so the first round.
* **Your Champion's card.** Your kingdom window's **🦸 Champion** tab (👥 Kingdom → Champion, yours only) shows **how it looks**, in high graphics, with its Idle, March, Battle cry and Strike, then its name, level (and the City Hall's cap), its Renown to the next level, what is banked and what it earned today, where it is (home, leading a march, or wounded with the time left), the bonus it brings, its kit slot by slot (locked slots with the level that opens them), its rolled look, and the **Renown ledger**: every battle's lines (rounds fought, enemies slain, victory, a boss or a realm felled, a Finale's kills), the day's thinning, Renown banked at the cap, and level-ups.
* **Re-roll and retraining.** **Re-roll its look** costs one gem of your civilization (its name, level and Arts stay). **Retraining** swaps one slot of its kit for another from that slot's list (never one it already knows) for 3,000 gold and 20,000 iron; a slot still locked can be retrained ahead of its level. A champion away at the head of a march retrains when it is home.
* **Civilization changes.** The champion stays the same hero: its name, level, Renown and Arts carry over, and its look is kept slot by slot as indices into the part library, so it is re-dressed in the new civilization's parts.
* **Its look, rolled like a collectible.** Eight slots (**head, face, body, armour, cloak, weapon, aura, mark**), four options each in every civilization's part library (65,536 looks per civilization): Valoria's champions wear gilt, steel or white plate under great helms, crowns or winged helms with swords and kite shields, greatswords, spears or war-hammers; Sylvandale's moonsilver and leaf-green plate, circlets of leaves and hoods, leaf-blades with bows, spears and crystal staves; Ironforge's broad bearded smiths in gilt or steel plate with winged, great or horned helms, hammers and axes; Drak'mar's hulking tusked warriors in blackiron and bronze, horned helms, axes, hammers and greatswords; Nethergarde's riftglass and obsidian under glowing hoods and crowns, crystal staves and void greatswords. Every champion carries what makes legendary read at map distance: gilt or rune-lit armour, a glowing mark over its heart (a sun, a leaf, a rune, a star, a skull or an eye), motes circling it, its embers and aura (no beam over a person).
* **On the map.** With HD graphics a champion walks at the head of the march it leads, a little ahead of its soldiers, and fights in front of them: a rigged hero in the same batch as the world bosses and the mercenary bands, so many champions on the map cost no draw more than one. In Normal graphics the march shows its soldiers only. The March Queue marks a march a champion leads (🦸 Lv12). Every civilization's champions are in the Royal Codex under **Creatures → Champions** (four looks each, every slot's options listed; high graphics only).
* **In reports.** A battle report lists the champion with the army it led (*🦸 Ser Corwin the Unbowed (Lv18)*, *fell* when it fell), the champion that led the garrison, and the Renown every champion earned.
* **The weekly story** (§15 *Weekly Chronicle*) tells of champions' Finales that broke the last of an enemy army, and of champions reaching level 10, 20 and 30.
* **Admin.** The treasury ledger reset (Royal Decrees) leaves champions' Renown alone; it resets it only when asked (`{ champions: true }`).

### Balance targets (`npm run sim:balance`)

Every check runs the game's live battle engine with real armies (march capacity × slots, capped by the soldier cap) and must pass (⚖️ BALANCED, exit 0):
* **Losses are the dead**: an elf army's wounded (§13 *Moonwell Healers*) don't count as losses; an elf rally's effective losses (dead + wounded × the hasten's cost) against every world boss stay no lower than the civilization that loses least there; hastening costs 10–60% of raising a soldier; a march wiped out is healed in 2–6 h at every City Hall, sooner than births replace its dead; only elves have the Moonwell, and the client copy of `MOONWELL` matches the server.
* **Voidrift** 2–6 assaults at City Hall 10, elves fastest · **PvP** matchups on the chart above · **rallies** beat the same armies arriving separately (a castle: fewer losses than arriving together or trickling in; a boss: no more tries than the same marches apart, with a rally's 8 rounds) · **bosses**, **NPC kingdoms** and **Overseers** as above · **gathering** worth 50–250% of the harvest income.
* **Armies**: every civ's soldier cap fills at least one march at every City Hall. Births replace a lost City Hall 10 march in ~9 h (orcs), ~11 h (humans), ~22 h (dwarves) or ~26 h (elves); Nethergarde relies on the Void Well and summoning.
* **Plunder**: a City Hall 10 army carries ~7,700 home per win as humans (swarm) and ~6,800 as dwarves (heavy load) — within 1.5× of each other; orcs ~11,100, elves ~1,400.
* **Walls**: tier-5 walls need 1.5–3× the waves of tier 0 (today ×1.7); a full City Hall 20 rally breaches a tier-5 City Hall 25 castle of the civilization it beats.
* **Burning**: City Hall 1–5 castles burn after 1–2 waves of a City Hall 10 march; burning protects a castle for at least 8 h (a swarm camping it can burn it again right after, so at most 3 times a day).
* **Mobs & bandits** (each pack at its spawn tier): common — one City Hall 5 march wins losing < 35%; uncommon — one City Hall 8 march, < 50%; rare — a City Hall 12 ruler with every slot, < 50%; epic — a City Hall 15 ruler with every slot, < 60%, and too strong for a lone City Hall 5 march. Elves, with their tiny armies, count two rulers for rare and epic packs. **A pack's Battle Arts kit makes it no harder to beat**: against the army its tier is meant for, against half of it (a march that only just wins) and under a Blood Moon, no army wins less or loses more than 2 points over the same pack without its kit; and under a Blood Moon every pack still falls to its target army wherever it did without a kit.
* **Seasons** touch only harvest, births and march speed (never combat stats), so the chart holds in every season.
* **Mercenaries**: every civilization's band of every rarity lands on its power (0.9× … 1.4× a full march, Arts included) within ±6% at City Hall 25 and 12; bands take the march slots a ruler's soldiers can't fill (at City Hall 25 the soldier cap fills 3.0 marches for Nethergarde, 2.8 for orcs, 2.5 for humans and dwarves, 1.3 for elves, of 5): a City Hall 25 ruler's own soldiers plus their 2 best bands in the free slots add no more than plain marches worth the bands' power (within 10%, for Arts like Sunder and Paralyze that also spare the soldiers beside them); a lone City Hall 25 ruler never kills a world boss in one battle, with their 2 best bands (landing 30% of their blows) or without (orcs land 50% of theirs: without it their soldiers alone one-shot 3 of the 12 bosses, and with full-strength bands all 12); a band defending its hirer's walls lands on its power there too (within ±10%, Arts included: it adds what it is worth to a castle, no more); battle pay is one harvest's upkeep a fight (client copy matches); today a ruler's bands take a boss in 3.3 battles instead of 3.9 for humans, 2.5 instead of 2.8 for orcs, 2.8 instead of 3.9 for dwarves, 4.2 instead of 5.4 for elves and 2.8 instead of 3.1 for Nethergarde.
* **The Armory**: every civilization's set of every rarity lands on its power (1.08× / 1.16× / 1.25× the same march in standard gear) within ±6% at City Hall 25 and 12; forging a set is worth what its power would cost in extra soldiers (±15%) and its upkeep what they would eat (±25%); the matchup chart holds with every civilization fully equipped in its uncommon and in its epic set (the attacker's marches and the garrison alike). With every ruler in the best set their City Hall forges: every pack still costs the army its tier is meant for at least 60% of its losses in standard gear, epic packs still beat a lone City Hall 5 march, no free realm a lone ruler can't take in one battle at City Hall 15 / 20 / 25 falls to them, every full frontier gate that holds against a lone ruler of any City Hall from 15 to 25 in standard gear still holds (🏯 Murder Holes), a lone City Hall 20 march takes under 25% of a boss, Colossus Hide's City Hall 15 ruler takes at most 30%, and no lone City Hall 25 ruler (their own soldiers and their 2 best bands, or every slot of soldiers, without titles or with every combat title maxed) kills a boss in one battle (🐉 Forged for Armies, and 🗿 Titan's Endurance under it). Client copy matches.
* **Champions**: a max-level champion with the strongest kit it can roll lifts the march it leads at most **+25%** over the same march without it, its kit included (today +22% to +24% by civilization at City Hall 25 and 12: the stat bonus and the kit at a hero's 13% share). With a champion of the ruler's City Hall level leading their first march, in their best set, beside their bands: every pack still costs the army its tier is meant for at least 60% of its losses in standard gear, epic packs still beat a lone City Hall 5 march (🐉 Legends of the Wild), no free realm a lone ruler can't take falls to them, every full frontier gate that holds against a lone ruler of City Hall 15–25 still holds (🏯 Marked from the Gatehouse), and no lone City Hall 25 ruler kills a world boss in one battle with a level-25 champion, their best set and their 2 best bands or every slot of soldiers (🗿 Titan's Endurance). Client copy matches.
* **World bosses — Titan's Endurance and Colossus's Wrath**: no battle takes more than 60% of a boss's max HP, even the strongest lone ruler's (City Hall 25, every combat title maxed, epic set, every slot); a lone ruler who wakes the Wrath loses at least as much as the same armies would without it. Client copy matches.
* **World disasters** (§10): under a Blood Moon the rally of 4 that kills each boss today still kills it, in at most twice its tries +2 (today the worst needs 12, dwarves against the Treant); Tempest Roads never costs a march more than a fifth of its soldiers (worst 16%, Dwarves), and the lost soldiers' mobilization cost stays far below the march's round-trip rations; the schedule rolls 1–3 every week, never two a day, all starting 06:00–22:00 realm time.

### Milestone Titles

Server: [titles.ts](server/src/titles.ts)

Rulers earn **titles** from their Git history and their war record. Each category gives only its highest tier reached (Tier III replaces Tier II). Buffs from different categories **add up**.

**Special Overseers** don't take part (nor does the Archon, whose one title is **Archon Eternal**): they hold one fixed title, **Eternal Overseer of the Voidrift**, with no title buffs (their garrison fights with the Overseer's Resolve instead, see *Special Overseers*).

| Category | Counts | Tier I | Tier II | Tier III | Buff (I / II / III) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Lines of Code** | lines added + deleted over all your commits | 5M 📜 Scribe of the Archive | 15M 🏛️ Grand Code-Architect | 25M 🌌 Eternal Chronicler | HP +2% / +5% / +10% |
| **Commits** | your commits (default branch, §2) | 500 ⚒️ The Diligent Ironforge | 2,000 ⚔️ The Relentless Vanguard | 3,500 🌋 Mythic Warforger | ATK +2% / +5% / +10% |
| **Active Days** | distinct calendar days with a commit (the commit's own date) | 200 🛡️ Steadfast Sentinel | 600 🏰 Unyielding Bulwark | 1,200 💠 Ancient Aegis of Epochs | DEF +2% / +5% / +10% |
| **Repo Holdings** | your buildings (repos) at **Lv 3 or higher**, idle ones included (§4–5) | 5 🏘️ Steward of Five Halls | 10 🏙️ Lord of Many Holdings | 20 🗺️ Architect of Dominions | All stats +1% / +2% / +3% |
| **Legendary Buildings** | your buildings at the **Lv 25 cap** (§5) | 1 🏯 Keeper of a Wonder | 2 🗼 Lord of Twin Wonders | 3 🌟 Architect of Legends | All stats +2% / +4% / +6% |
| **Game Activity** | play days: UTC days with at least 5 game actions (§7 play streak) | 30 🎺 Herald of the Realm | 90 🪙 Steward of the Treasury | 180 👑 Undying Sovereign | Harvest +10% / +20% / +30% (capped, §7) |
| **World Boss Kills** | bosses killed while you shared the bounty (you wounded it that life) | 10 🏹 Beastslayer of the Wilds | 50 ⚡ Colossus Decimator | 100 🐉 Apex Worldbreaker | All stats +1% / +2% / +3% |
| **Kingdom Kills** | enemy units slain in battles against rulers' kingdoms and ruler-held Voidrift slots, attacking or defending | 1,000 🩸 Bloodbound Raider | 10,000 👑 Conqueror of Realms | 100,000 ☠️ Sovereign Executioner | ATK +2% / +5% / +10% |
| **Resources Gathered** | base resources your gatherers carried home (`gathering_haul` ledger entries) | 10,000 🌾 The Earth Harvester | 100,000 💎 Master of Abundance | 1,000,000 🪙 Titan of the Endless Vault | HP +2% / +5% / +10% |
| **Mob & Bandit Kills** | mob and bandit units slain | 1,000 🌲 Shield of the Frontier | 10,000 ⚖️ Scourge of the Lawless | 100,000 🕊️ Immortal Peacekeeper | DEF +2% / +5% / +10% |

* **Commits** are matched to you the same way as for XP (§2), so commits from all history the backfill has read count. Lines are raw line counts, not the "meaningful lines" XP uses.
* **Repo Holdings** counts only repos that became buildings (over the building cap they don't count) and whose building reached Lv 3, so a repo needs real work behind it. Idle and dormant buildings keep their level, so they still count; the count is refreshed on every sync. **Legendary Buildings** counts the same way, but only buildings at the Lv 25 cap (466 XP in one repo): the rarest titles in the realm.
* **Kills are split fairly.** In a battle with several armies, each side's kills (the other side's losses) are shared between its armies by the troops each brought. A defending ruler's home garrison counts as one army next to any reinforcements. NPC void garrisons in open Voidrift slots don't count as kingdom kills.
* **When titles update:** on every Live Sync (startup and every `GIT_SYNC_INTERVAL_HOURS`), right after each battle for everyone who fought in it, when gatherers bring a haul home, and when an admin re-checks a developer's commits. Losing a tier is possible (for example when commits are re-attributed to someone else). A new title is announced in the Chronicle (`🎖️ … earns the title …`).
* Every kingdom carries its `earnedTitles` and summed `titleBuffs` (HP / ATK / DEF; "all stats" adds to all three; `harvestBonus` from Game Activity, which never affects combat). `GET /api/kingdoms/:id/titles` returns the ladder with the ruler's raw totals for progress.
* **Buffs in battle.** Title buffs multiply a ruler's soldier HP, ATK and DEF on top of the civilization buffs and debuffs (a human soldier with +5% HP, +2% ATK, +2% DEF fights at 12.6 HP / 5.1 ATK / 3.06 DEF instead of 12 / 5 / 3). They apply wherever the ruler's own soldiers fight: attacking armies and rallies (each member with their own titles), the home garrison defending a kingdom, stationed reinforcements and gatherers defending a node. Monsters, bosses, NPC kingdoms and void garrisons have no titles. Buffs are read fresh every round, so a title earned mid-battle counts from the next round. An Armory set multiplies on top of them (§13 *The Armory*).
* The battle log names each army's buffs the first round it fights (`🎖️ Ser Aldric's troops fight with +10% ATK (🌋 Mythic Warforger)`). Scout reports of a kingdom show its soldiers' stats with the ruler's titles and Dragon Statue / Mana Spire / Plaza Fountain bonuses included. Those three improvements add to title buffs the same way (§8).
* The **👤 developer profile** shows a **🎖️ Sovereign Titles** section with only the titles the ruler holds, the count (e.g. "3 of 10") and the summed HP / ATK / DEF (and harvest) bonuses. **View all titles →** opens a window with every category: the tier held, a progress bar to the next tier and the whole ladder with **👥 how many rulers hold each tier**. Click a count to see who (earliest holder first); closing that list returns to the titles window, and closing the titles window returns to the profile. Open your own from the 👤 button next to the kingdom picker; open another ruler's by picking them in the picker and pressing 👤, or by clicking their castle and choosing the profile.
* The **kingdom picker** shows a **🏅 N** badge next to every ruler holding titles (hover it for their names). Special Overseers carry the violet **Overseer's medal** instead (a gold starburst behind a violet enamel cross), and their profile shows their one title plus **🔱 Overseer's Resolve**: current stacks (×0 when undefeated this week) and the HP / ATK / DEF bonus they give.

### Not in the game yet

Agreed but not built yet:
* **A world-map announcement** when a civilization place opens, so the move becomes a race (today the War Room lists open places).
* The ⚔️ War pill showing **march slots in use** ("⚔️ 2/3") and a **rally countdown**.
* Map visuals: a **respawn timer on hover** over a fallen pack, and returning armies visibly **carrying loot sacks**.
* A small **gem chance by level** in an NPC kingdom's harvest refill.

Ideas for later: intercepting marches on the road, caravans as marches that can be raided outside safe territory, alliances across civilizations, march-speed items, PvP seasons and leaderboards.

### War Room & reports

The **⚔️ War** pill opens the War Room: your **📯 Notices** of the last 30 days (§14), every army on the road (`outbound`, `acting`, `returning`, with its kind icon), your civilization's **open rallies** (Join / Call off), open civilization places & War Frenzy pairs, peaceful swap proposals, your **scout reports**, and the battle chronicle (*My battles* / *Whole realm*); a badge counts reports you haven't opened, and the **🧹 broom** next to the chronicle tabs marks them all as read. Each report shows both sides, participating stacks ("joined round 2"), losses, wall damage & burning status, plunder carried home, ascension, round-by-round damage and the battle log.

### Interactive Audio & Sound Feedback

Client: [useSound.ts](src/hooks/useSound.ts)

Every sound is synthesized in the browser with the Web Audio API (no audio files); ☰ menu → **⚙️ Game settings** → *Sound* mutes them. The game plays seven cues:
* **Click** — a short tick on buttons, menus, tabs and picking a kingdom.
* **Victory fanfare** (four rising notes) — a march or caravan sent off, an improvement bought, a Royal Proclamation given (settlers, Festival), the garrison set, a void ritual, a civilization changed or swapped, plots swapped, a battle won or a raid on you repelled, and admin and decree actions done.
* **Defeat horn** (three falling notes) — one of your marches was repelled (with the red Chronicle alert).
* **Herald's call** (a rising horn fanfare) — a new herald notice arrives (§14), or an announcement is proclaimed.
* **Spell whoosh** (a sweep of filtered noise) — a Battle Art is cast (§13 *Battle Arts*), in a battle within ~140 units of the camera on the world map and for every cast in the Sparring Grounds; its pitch is the Art's voice (fire low, frost and sand high, thunder long).
* **Spell boom** (a falling thump under a crack, ringing for frost, steel and holy light) — the cast lands, as its burst shows.
* **Finale horn** (three horn notes over the whoosh) — a Finale is cast (a boss rears up in its battle cry).

Many casts at once sound as a few: at most one of each every 0.15 s. The sound library also holds four cues the game doesn't play yet: a harvest coin chime, water bubbles, a footstep and a slime splat. All eleven can be heard in the Royal Codex's **Music & Sounds → Game sounds** tab, next to each civilization's three **Soundtracks** (§10), which are synthesized the same way and play only there for now.

---

## 14. Herald Notices

Server: [broadcasts.ts](server/src/broadcasts.ts) · Client: [HeraldModal.tsx](src/components/ui/HeraldModal.tsx)

Some news should reach a ruler exactly once. When you load the realm with unread notices, a **herald** unrolls them on a parchment scroll, one at a time (`1 of 3`), each closed with a wax seal in the sender's colour and announced by a short horn. **Next** closes the notice in front of you, **Dismiss all** closes the rest.

* **Once per kingdom.** Reads are kept on the server, so a notice you closed never opens again, on any browser or device.
* **Public notices** also appear in the Chronicle ticker at the bottom of the screen.
* **📯 Notices** in the War Room lists everything addressed to you from the last 30 days (unread ones marked); opening one there closes it for the herald too. The herald itself only opens notices from the last 7 days.
* **Signed out**, you see only notices for the whole realm, and closing one is remembered in that browser only.

| Notice | When | Who hears it |
|---|---|---|
| 🐉 **Boss slain** | a world boss dies | the whole realm: *"The Leviathan Kraken Lord has fallen to Ser Aldric's rally"*, with the top three damage dealers of its life and their share |
| ⚔️ **While you were away** | you come back after at least 15 minutes away, and rulers attacked your kingdom meanwhile | you alone: how many attacks, repelled / breached, who attacked, your soldiers lost and theirs, plunder taken, and whether your walls were left burning |
| 📜 **Announcement** | an admin or Special Overseer proclaims one (§15) | the whole realm, one civilization, or the rulers they picked |
| 🎁 **Royal gift** | an admin grants resources (§15) | the rulers who received it, with the gift's message and what it held |
| 🛡️ **Royal Shield** | a kingdom is shielded, or a shield shatters because its holder attacked a ruler (§15) | the whole realm (a short line) and the shielded ruler (what the shield does) |
| ⛈️ **Punishment** | a kingdom is punished, or the punishment is lifted early (§15) | the whole realm (a short line) and the punished ruler (its effects and length); the early lift goes to the ruler only |
| 🔥 **A border falls** | a frontier gate catches fire (§13 *Frontier gates*) | the whole realm: who set it alight, and that its province's cities can be attacked until the walls repair (~8 h) |
| 💀 **Scouts caught** | scouts sent to a ruler's city or an NPC kingdom are caught, by the city's watch, its spymasters or a frontier gate's watch (§13 *Scouting*) | the scouting ruler (*Your scouts never returned*) and, for a ruler's city, that ruler (*Caught 3 scouts from Ser Aldric*) |
| 🦉 **False intel** | your Spymaster's Tower fed lies to scouts sent to your city (§13 *Scouting*) | you alone (*False intel for Ser Aldric*); the scouting ruler is never told |
| 🚧 **Gate duty** | your treasury can't pay for all your troops on gate duty at a harvest | you alone: how many walked home from which gate |
| 🏗️ **Improvements refunded** | an admin resets your improvements, for your kingdom alone or for all kingdoms (§11) | you alone: each improvement taken down and from which tier, and everything returned to your treasury (resources and gems) |
| 🪶 **Deed report** | a manager seals or rejects your pending deed, or revokes a sealed one (§17) | you alone: the deed, its XP and repo, who did it and their note; a rejected deed's XP is free again for that day |
| 🌠 **Omen heeded** | an admin marks your omen read (§11) | you alone: *Your omen was heeded* (or the admin's own words) |
| 🎉 **A Festival** | a ruler declares a Festival (§13 *Royal Proclamations*), or is given one by royal decree (§15 *Proclamations*) | the ruler's own civilization: the lanterns, dancing and fireworks, and its births ×2 and +10% workforce for the day; it goes when the Festival does |
| 🍂 **The season turns** | the first minute of a new season (Sunday 00:00 UTC; §7 *Day, night & seasons*) | the whole realm: the season's story, which civ flourishes and which struggles (harvest, births, march speed), and the season that follows; it goes when the season does |
| ⛈️ **Dark clouds gather** | an hour before a world disaster strikes (§10 *World Disasters*) | the whole realm: an omen, the disaster's realm-time window, what it does, who copes and who suffers; it goes when the disaster does |
| 🌾🌪️🩸☠️🥀 **Disaster strikes** | a world disaster begins | the whole realm: what it does until when, who copes and who suffers; it goes when the disaster does |
| 🌾🌪️🩸☠️🥀 **The realm weathers it** | a world disaster ends | the whole realm: what it did to each civilization (marchers lost, plague deaths, births lost, pool withheld, battle rounds under the red moon) and who coped best and suffered most; kept 2 days |
| 👑 **A legend signs on** | a legendary mercenary is hired at a camp (§13 *Mercenaries*) | the whole realm: who hired whom, where |
| 💀 **Mercenaries fell** | one of your bands is wiped out | you alone: the band, its rarity and where it fell; its place is free |
| 🦸 **Your champion fell** | the army (or home garrison) your champion led is wiped out (§13 *Champions*) | you alone: where it fell and when it is whole again |
| 🦸 **Your champion rises** | your champion reaches a new level | you alone: its level, the bonus it now brings, and what its kit unlocked |
| 🗡️ **Your sets are rusting** | your treasury can't pay the Armory's upkeep at a harvest (§13 *The Armory*) | you alone: how many of which sets rusted back to the stock pile |
| 🪙 **Mercenaries want paying** · 🏃 **deserted** | your treasury can't pay a band's upkeep at a harvest; unpaid again at the next | you alone: what the upkeep was, then that the band left your service |
| 📖 **The Weekly Chronicle** | an admin, Special Overseer or the Archon publishes last week's story (§15 *Weekly Chronicle*) | the whole realm: the story's title and full text with its marks drawn (insignia, icons, callouts, dividers), under a violet seal; it is kept in the Codex's 📜 Chronicles |

**📖 The story's banner.** For 7 days after a weekly story is published, a violet *The Weekly Chronicle* banner with its title sits at the top left of the screen for everyone (signed in or not, under the managers' banners). Clicking it opens the Royal Codex on 📜 Chronicles; its ✕ closes it. Either way it stays closed in that browser until the next story.

Attacks you could watch happen (you were online) are not repeated; they have their own battle report popup. The shielded or punished ruler doesn't get the public line as well: it is marked read for them. An announcement can be **📌 pinned**: it stays at the top of the War Room's Notices (and is kept) for the days chosen.

## 15. Royal Decrees

Server: [decrees.ts](server/src/decrees.ts) · Client: [RoyalDecreesModal.tsx](src/components/ui/RoyalDecreesModal.tsx)

Admins (`ADMIN_USERS`), Special Overseers (`SPECIAL_USERS`) and the Archon (`ARCHON_USERS`) rule the realm and tell its weekly story from the **👑 Royal Decrees** panel (👑 crown button in the top bar, after the 🪶 Scriptorium).

| Decree | Admin | Special Overseer | Archon |
|---|---|---|---|
| 📜 Announcement | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| 🛡️ Shield a kingdom | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| ⛈️ Punish a kingdom | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| 🎉 Proclamations: give a Festival | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| 📖 Weekly Chronicle: publish last week's story | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| 🎁 Give resources | ✅ | — | — |

Every decree asks for confirmation, raises herald notices (§14) and is written to the realm's event log; the panel's **Audit** tab lists who did what and when (shields shattered by an attack are listed too). Decrees don't count as game actions for the play streak (§7).

A **🔭 Watchtower** card at the top of the panel opens the work analytics (§18), which also has its own 🔭 button in the top bar.

### 📜 Announcement

A title (up to 80 characters) and text (plain text and emoji, up to 2,000), heard by **everyone**, **one civilization** or **picked rulers**. A live parchment preview shows how it will look. It can be **pinned in the War Room for 0–30 days**: pinned notices sit at the top of 📯 Notices with a 📌 and are kept at least that long.

### 🎁 Give resources (admins)

* **Form**: pick recipients (search, civilization filter, *Select all*), amounts of gold, iron, stone, lumber, food and any of the 12 scarce gems, and a message. The message starts as a random flavour line (*Grace by the Gods*, *The Treasury Overflows*, *A Caravan from the Sun Court*, …, about 30 of them); **🎲** rerolls it, and it can be edited.
* **Import JSON**: drop or pick a `.json` file, or paste it:
  ```json
  {
    "message": "Grace by the Gods",
    "gifts": [
      { "users": ["jane.doe", "john.roe"], "gold": 5000, "iron": 20000, "gems": { "mana": 10, "diamond": 2 } },
      { "users": ["*"], "food": 50000 }
    ]
  }
  ```
  `users` are Git logins; `"*"` means every ruler who isn't a Special Overseer. Rows that name the same ruler add up. Gem keys: `mana`, `diamond`, `ruby`, `emerald`, `sapphire`, `mithril`, `obsidian`, `aether`, `dragon_scale`, `ancient_rune`, `titan_tablet`, `phoenix_feather`.
* **Check** shows a preview table (what each ruler gets) and every error: unknown logins, Special Overseers (their treasury resets every harvest), unknown gem keys or fields, amounts that aren't whole numbers from 0 to 100,000,000 (gems: 100,000), rows that give nothing. **Nothing is granted until every row is valid.** **Grant** then pays it.
* Each ruler gets ledger lines of type `admin_gift` (one for the base resources, one per gem, reason *🎁 Royal gift: <message>*) and a 🎁 notice listing what they received.

### 🛡️ Shield

Pick a kingdom and **1–14 days** (not the Archon, who is untouchable already). While the shield is up:

* nobody can **attack**, **rally on** or **scout** the kingdom (the march form says why); armies, rallies and scouts already on the road **turn back when they arrive** (scouts bring no report). Reinforcements from allies still come. The shield covers the castle: the ruler's gatherers out in the field can still be attacked where they are exposed.
* **Breaks if the holder attacks a ruler** (checkbox, on by default): marching on another ruler's castle or on their gatherers, or starting or joining a rally against a ruler, shatters the shield at once, with a notice to the realm. Gathering, bosses, mobs, bandits and NPC kingdoms never break it. The march form warns before you send such a march.
* A translucent hex dome covers the castle on the world map; in the city an energy curtain rises round the outer walls, tracing their outline (§5); the castle badge shows 🛡️ and, on hover, `🛡️ Royal Shield — 3d 4h left`, as do the kingdom panels.
* A new shield on the same kingdom replaces the old one; **Lift** ends it early.

### ⛈️ Punish

Pick a mortal kingdom (Special Overseers take nothing from the harvest, so they can't be punished; the Archon is above every decree), **1–14 days**, and any of:

| Option | Effect | World map | City view |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🌧️ No harvest (always on) | nothing from the harvest pool, gem drop, building harvest or workforce (§7) | an epic-class storm (like burning walls): a churning thunderhead over the castle, lit from inside when forked violet lightning strikes, slanted rain splashing on the ground, and a violet curtain of storm light round the castle (a burning castle, a Wonder or the Empyrean keeps its own glow) | the sky darkens, heavy slanted rain, forked lightning strikes the streets and flashes the sky, ragged storm clouds scattered over the countryside |
| 🥀 Low birth rate | births ×0.25 | — | flowers wilt grey, no children in the streets, empty cradles by the houses |
| 🪦 High death rate | civilian deaths ×3 (soldiers never die of age) | crows circle the storm cloud | crows over the plaza, fresh graves outside the walls, grey-green miasma |
| 🌑 Wrath | sends the mythical horror at the kingdom | — | — |

Wrath is not in the game yet (shown greyed out). Status chips in the kingdom panels and the castle's hover card read `⛈️ Punished · no harvest · ▼ births · ▼ life — 2d left`. A new punishment replaces the old one; **Lift** ends it early (the ruler is told).

### 🎉 Proclamations

The **Proclamations** tab gives a kingdom one of the Royal Proclamations (§13) by decree. For now there is one, the **🎉 Festival**: pick a kingdom and it holds a Festival from now for **24 hours**, exactly as if its ruler had declared one (births ×2, workforce +10%, the lanterns, dancing and fireworks). Given by decree it is:
* **free**, and **apart from the ruler's own weekly Festival**: their own cooldown is untouched, so they can still declare theirs once it is over;
* given **even while the ruler is ⚔️ Called to Arms**;
* refused for a kingdom already holding a Festival, for **Nethergarde** and for **Special Overseers** (the picker lists mortal kingdoms only).

The ruler's civilization hears it from the herald (*By royal decree, Ser Aldric holds a Festival*, §14), and the tab lists the Festivals under way with their time left.

### 📖 Weekly Chronicle

Server: [chronicle.ts](server/src/chronicle.ts), [chronicleDetectors.ts](server/src/chronicleDetectors.ts)

Every week the realm gets a story about the week before, told as legend, not stats. The **Weekly Chronicle** tab writes it with any LLM the decree holder likes; the game never calls one itself. Anyone who may proclaim an announcement may publish the story, since it reaches the same people (the whole realm).

1. **Pick the week.** It opens on **last week** (Sunday 00:00 UTC to Sunday, like the other weekly resets). The picker also offers the four weeks before it and *this week so far* (a preview: it can't be published until it ends).
2. **The happenings.** The week's digest, biggest first (at most 24), each one line told as legend. Small detectors read the realm's own logs (battle reports, realm events, the ledgers, titles, deeds):

   | Detector | Finds |
   |---|---|
   | 🌀 Voidrift claims | an open Voidrift slot taken; the **first ever** is told as such |
   | ⚔️ Voidrift persistence | a ruler who attacked the Voidrift (its spires or its Nethergarde rulers) at least **3 times** since their last win there and finally won, with the count |
   | 👑 Voidrift falls | a mortal ruler casting a Nethergarde ruler out and taking their throne |
   | 💎 Rare finds | a **legendary** resource node worked until empty, a legendary chest (the **Relic Hoard**) carried home, legendary gems (Molten Dragon Scale, Titan Tablet, Phoenix Feather) brought home from gathering, battle loot or a boss bounty (the harvest and the workforce don't count: they bring them every day) |
   | 🐉 Boss kills | a **solo kill** (the killer dealt at least **60%** of the damage), a **stolen kill** (the top damage dealer wasn't the killer: by how much and each one's share), or a plain kill |
   | 🏰 Biggest siege | the assault on a ruler's castle with the most soldiers on both sides (a rally is named as one) |
   | 🛡️ Longest defence | the ruler who turned back the most assaults (2 or more, or one held for 4+ rounds), and the most rounds held |
   | 🔥 Castles burned | a ruler's walls left burning by an assault |
   | 🔄 Comebacks | a ruler beaten by another earlier in the week who beat them in turn |
   | 🏅 Titles | milestone titles reached that week (§13; up to 8) |
   | 🤝 Civilization swaps | swaps agreed that week (§9) |
   | 📜 Deeds sealed | the rulers whose deeds the Crown sealed that week (§17; top 5) |
   | 💀 Mercenaries fell | bands wiped out that week, the rarest first (up to 6) |
   | 👑 Legend for hire | every legendary mercenary hired that week, and where |
   | 🎺 A champion's Finale | champions' Finales that broke the last of an enemy army that week, the most armies broken first (up to 4) |
   | 🦸 A champion's renown | champions who reached level 10, 20 or 30 that week (up to 5) |
   | ⛈️ Disasters | each world disaster that ended that week: who coped best, who suffered most, and what it cost each civilization (§10 *World Disasters*) |

   Under them, **the realm's labour**: each civilization's work that week is **translated**, never counted in commits or lines: *The forges of Ironforge rang through 412 nights; 6 smiths hammered out 9,136 ingots of steel* (works become nights, watches, songs, drumbeats or runes; every 10 lines become one stone, ingot, leaf or mote), and the ruler who worked on 5 or more of the 7 days (the most days) is named. A week where no detector finds anything is **quiet**: *No great deeds; the realm rests and its heroes sharpen their blades.*
3. **Copy prompt.** A ready prompt for any LLM: the style rules (medieval and dramatic, 250–450 words, open with the week's greatest happening, the rulers' names exactly as given and no other real names, never commits, code or real-world work, kind enough to read aloud), the **marks** (below), the **saga so far** (the last 3 published stories, so the story goes on), and the digest. It asks for the title on the first line, a blank line, then the story beginning with its insignia.
4. **Paste the reply** into the story box: pasted into an empty form, its first line becomes the title. Edit both as you like (title up to 100 characters, no marks; story up to 6,000). **Preview** draws the chapter exactly as the realm will see it, and lists any mark the game can't draw.
5. **Publish** (after a confirmation). A story with a mark the game can't draw (an unknown key, a callout never closed, a second insignia, too many callouts or dividers) is refused, with what to fix. One story per week, and it can't be taken back. It is kept in the Royal Codex under **📜 Chronicles** (§10), goes to **the whole realm as a herald notice** (§14, the full story), shows the 📖 banner for a week, and is written in the audit (*📖 Published the Weekly Chronicle "…"*). A week already published shows its story instead of the form.

**Marks.** The bard chooses how the story is adorned by writing tags into it, from a list of **68 marks** the prompt gives with their meanings ([chronicleMarks.ts](server/src/chronicleMarks.ts)):

| Tag | Drawn as |
|---|---|
| `[dragon]` | a small line icon in the text, before the word it adorns (*the [dragon] Molten Wyrm*); 4–10 of them |
| `[insignia:dragon]` | once, at the start: a square crest (the mark on gold in a dark wooden frame) beside the opening paragraph; without one the paragraph opens with a red drop cap |
| `[callout:dragon]…[/callout]` | a paragraph of its own framed in gold with the icon at its side, for the week's most striking moment; at most 2 |
| `[divider:swords]` | a line of its own: the icon between two rules; at most 3 |

The marks come in seven groups: *creatures* (dragon (hand-drawn), griffin, phoenix, wolf, serpent, kraken, raven, beast), *war* (swords, sword, axe, bow, shield, siege, castle, burning, banner, skull, bomb, horn, drum, trophy, medal), *the realm* (crown, fallen-crown, throne, void, rift, spire, temple, camp, road, map, pact), *riches* (gem, hoard, relic, key, scroll, book), *omens and nature* (blood-moon, moon, sun, storm, wind, plague, blight, wither, frost, fire, star, mountain, sea, forest, omen, ghost, hourglass), *labour* (hammer, anvil, pickaxe, quill, harvest, bell) and *peoples* (valoria, sylvandale, ironforge, drakmar, nethergarde). Rulers' names are written plain; the game finds them and draws each as a chip with their people's crest, and the chapter's header shows those peoples' crests. The strip of happenings comes from the week's digest, so it needs no marks. Marks are written in square brackets because chat windows treat `<tags>` as HTML and drop them when a reply is copied; the panel still tidies a pasted story (on **Preview** and on **Publish**): `<angle>` tags become square ones, a block mark that lost its brackets at the start of a paragraph (`insignia:dragonWhen the…`) gets them back, a callout left open is closed at its paragraph's end, and the double spaces where a copy dropped an inline mark are closed up. An inline mark the copy dropped can't be brought back: copy the reply as plain text (most chat windows have a copy button) to keep them.

---

## 16. The Hall of Renown (leaderboards)

Server: [leaderboard.ts](server/src/leaderboard.ts), [leaderboardRules.ts](server/src/leaderboardRules.ts) · Client: [HallOfRenownModal.tsx](src/components/ui/HallOfRenownModal.tsx)

The 🏆 trophy button next to the 👤 developer profile button opens the **Hall of Renown**, a ranking of developers that rewards **pace and quality, not totals**: someone with one year at the company shouldn't lose to someone with three just because their sums are bigger.

### Tabs

| Tab | Covers | Changes |
|---|---|---|
| Weekly | the last full week, Monday–Sunday | final once the week is over |
| Monthly | the last full calendar month | final once the month is over |
| Year | the current fiscal year so far (from the 1st of `FISCAL_YEAR_START_MONTH`, default October, to yesterday); shows *N working days so far* | daily |
| All time | each developer's own tenure, from their first meaningful commit to yesterday | daily |

Days are UTC days, counted from each commit's own date as for XP (§3). Today never counts until it is over.

### Three axes

1. **Activeness: showing up.** Days with a meaningful commit (not a merge, at least one counted line) ÷ working days (Mon–Fri) in the period, capped at 100% (weekend work can make up a missed weekday). 20 active of 22 working days ≈ 91%. **All time** measures it over your own tenure and pulls very short tenures toward the realm average: `(active + 20 × realm rate) ÷ (working days + 20)`, so a two-week newcomer with a perfect record doesn't top a steady veteran.
2. **Repo activeness: real work across repos.** Σ over repos of √(XP earned in that repo in the period) — commit, PR and review XP exactly as in §3 (PR and review XP count on repos you committed to or own). Spreading 100 XP over four repos scores 20; putting it all in one scores 10. **All time** divides by tenure in years.
3. **Repo quality: high-impact repos.** Each repo you **earned XP in during the period** that stands at **Lv3+** at the end of the period counts `1 + ln(level ÷ 3)`: Lv3 = 1.0, Lv5 = 1.5, Lv10 = 2.2, Lv25 = 3.1.
   * 1×Lv25 + 10×Lv1 → **3.1**
   * 5×Lv5 + 5×Lv1 → **7.6** (beats the single Lv25)
   * 2×Lv25 + 3×Lv1 → **6.2** (below five Lv5 repos, well above one Lv25)

   **All time** counts every repo and divides by √(tenure years).

All-time divisions treat tenures under **half a year** as half a year.

### Fairness score

Each axis becomes a **percentile** (0–100) among the developers ranked in that tab (ties share the average place), and the fairness score is the weighted average: **Activeness 40% · Repo activeness 30% · Quality 30%**. Ties go to higher activeness.

* Special Overseers and the Archon are not ranked.
* Developers without a meaningful commit in the period are not listed.
* ▲▼ shows places moved since the previous period: the week or month before; for Year and All time, the same ranking a week ago. **NEW** = not ranked then.

### The panel

A top-3 podium (civilization frames), then every ranked ruler: rank, castle level and name, realm, fairness score, three small bars (one per axis, hover for the axis rank, percentile and the numbers behind it) and ▲▼. Your own row is pinned at the bottom while it's scrolled out of view, and clicking a ruler opens their kingdom. ⓘ explains the formula.

### Badges of renown

The top 3 of each tab (#1 gold, #2 silver, #3 bronze) are marked across the realm. The marks are kept discreet, except the All time frame, which is the grand one:

| Tab | Mark |
|---|---|
| Weekly | the castle level badge (`Lv9`) in the kingdom picker becomes a small metal pennant with a notched tail |
| Monthly | the 🏅 next to the title count becomes a gold / silver / bronze medal with the place on it (shown even with no titles yet) |
| Year | the 🏠 next to the building count becomes a house with a metal roof |
| All time | the civilization frame stays, but is crowned: a sunburst of rays behind it (turning slowly for #1), a laurel wreath up both sides, a crown on top (five points for #1, three for #2 and #3) and a place medallion on a ribbon below. Shown in the kingdom picker, on your menu button in the top bar and on the All time podium; gold glints |

Hover any mark for the place it stands for. A legend at the bottom of the kingdom picker says which mark is which tab.

Daily XP per developer and repo is replayed from the stored commits, PRs and reviews when the server starts, on each new UTC day, and within a minute or two of new history arriving (live Git sync, historical backfill, an admin commit re-check or repo delete), so the numbers always match §3 and a fresh database fills in the same day with nothing to run by hand. `npm run sim:leaderboard` checks the worked examples above.

---

## 17. The Scriptorium (project tree & deed reports)

Server: [scriptorium.ts](server/src/scriptorium.ts) · Client: [ScriptoriumModal.tsx](src/components/ui/ScriptoriumModal.tsx), [DeedReviewModal.tsx](src/components/ui/DeedReviewModal.tsx)

The **🪶 feather button** in the top bar (after the 🏆 Hall of Renown and, for managers, the 🔭 Watchtower), open to every ruler, is where the realm's scribes write down what was done: a tree of what the company builds, and **deed reports**, work that isn't a commit (meetings, research, support, deployments, reviews outside Git) logged against a repo for a little XP.

### The tree

```
Division   (root)       e.g. a business unit                      ← admins only
  Client   (sub-root)   a client, tenant, SaaS or in-house app    ← admins, Special Overseers, the Archon
    Project             a project, which may span several repos   ← any developer (own repos)
      repos             Git repositories (each in at most one project)
```

| Who | Division | Client | Project | Put repos in a project |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Developer | — | — | create; edit / delete their own | only **their** repos (owned or committed to), only into a project they created |
| Special Overseer / Archon | — | add / rename / reorder / delete | create, edit, delete any | any repo |
| Admin | add / rename / reorder / delete | add / rename / reorder / delete | create, edit, delete any | any repo |

* A repo sits in **at most one project**. A developer can move their repo between their own projects, but taking it out of someone else's project needs a manager.
* Every level of the tree (Divisions, Clients, projects) is listed **A–Z**, numbers read as numbers ("Team 2" before "Team 10"), in the Scriptorium, the pickers and the Watchtower.
* **⚙ Manage tree** (managers): each Division is a card that folds open, with its Clients under it. ✎ beside a name renames it in place (Enter saves, Esc cancels), **+ Client** opens a row that stays open for the next one, ⇄ moves a Client to another Division, and **+ New Division** (admins) sits at the bottom. A Division or Client can only be deleted when empty: deleting a full one asks, right under it, where its Clients / projects go first (refused when both hold one of the same name).
* **Pickers** for repos, projects and Clients are searchable lists. Projects are listed under their **Division › Client** (two projects may share a name under different Clients), and a picked project shows its whole path. Repos show where they sit (their project, or *Unsorted*). **Developers search repos by name; Admins, Special Overseers and the Archon also by the name or username of anyone who owns or committed to the repo** (the Unsorted list shows those people under each repo for them).
* Deleting a project leaves its repos unsorted; deeds keep their repo and stay valid.
* Every repo name in the Scriptorium (Unsorted and a project's repos) links to the repo on Git (`GIT_URL/owner/name`, in a new tab).
* Repos in no project sit in the **📦 Unsorted** bucket, pinned at the top of the tree: a developer sees their own there, managers every repo with commits, each with a *Move to project* picker.
* The window has two tabs: **📜 Deed reports** (yours: today's and yesterday's allowance, a new deed, your deeds) and **🌳 Project tree** (the tree on the left, as tall as the window, with expand / collapse all and a filter that keeps matching names and the branches above them; the picked Division, Client or project on the right; a project shows its repos and the sealed deeds logged on them). Managers get the **⚙ Manage tree** button over the tree.

### Deed reports

A deed is written for **one repo** you own or have committed to, with a title (up to 80 characters), a report (plain text and emoji, up to 2,000) and an XP claim of **0.5, 1.0 or 1.5**.

| Rule | |
|---|---|
| Daily cap | **5 XP of deeds per developer per day** (the UTC day, as the commit quota). The XP picker only offers what still fits; at 5.0 the button is disabled (*That day's deeds are full (5 / 5 XP)*). The server refuses anything over |
| Auto-seal | **The first 3 XP of a day seal at once** and land on the repo's building. A deed that would take the day's sealed + pending total past 3 waits for a seal, whole (it is never split) |
| Pending | counts towards the cap; a **rejected** deed frees its XP for that day (a revoked one doesn't) |
| Days | today or yesterday only (no back-filling) |
| Who seals | an **Admin, Special Overseer or the Archon**, with an optional note. **Nobody seals their own**; *Seal all* skips them |
| Editing | the author may edit the title and text while it is pending; once sealed only a manager can **revoke** it (the XP comes off, the author is told) |

A sealed deed's XP counts like commit XP: it raises the repo's building level, the castle level and prestige, and it pays the harvest through the past-week XP (§7). A repo you own but never committed to becomes a building once a deed on it is sealed. It doesn't count in the Hall of Renown (§16), which ranks Git work.

Every seal, rejection and revocation is a 🪶 herald notice to the author (§14) and an entry in the realm's event log (`deed_sealed`, `deed_rejected`, `deed_revoked`).

### The seal banner

Admins, Special Overseers and the Archon see a **📜 N deeds await your seal** banner at the top left (under the admin warnings banner) whenever deeds are pending. It opens the review list, oldest first: each row shows the author, XP, repo and project, the day and the author's deed total that day (sealed + awaiting), with a note field, **🪶 Seal** and **Reject**, and **Seal all** at the bottom. The 🪶 button in the top bar carries the count as a badge, and the ☰ menu (*Deeds to seal*) and the Admin Panel's System Validations card show it too.

### Backup (export & import)

Admins keep the Scriptorium and the developer list safe across a data wipe from the Admin Panel's **Realm Backup** card (§11):

* **⬇ Download backup** saves one JSON file (`realm-backup-YYYY-MM-DD.json`): **every developer** (Git login, name, Git email, civilization and kingdom title) with **every commit email linked to them** and their **per-repo matches** of shared emails in 📧 Developer Emails (§1), every Division, Client and project (with its description, who made it and when), which repos sit in each project, and **every deed** with its status, XP, seal and note.
* **⬆ Restore from file** reads such a file back after showing what it holds, then reports how many developers, emails, branches, repos and deeds it restored and what it skipped. It names things the way a fresh harvest recreates them (developers by their Git login, branches by name, repos by `owner/name`), so it fits a new database. It **merges and never removes** anything not in the file:
  * A missing developer is added with the file's name, Git email, civilization and title (the next harvest keeps that civilization); a developer already there keeps everything and only has a blank name or Git email filled in.
  * Each email in the file is linked back to its developer and every commit made under it becomes theirs again, as when an admin matches it by hand; an email another developer now holds moves over. An email that is another developer's own Git email stays with them and is skipped, and so is one matched per repo here.
  * Each per-repo match in the file goes back to its developer, with that email's commits in the repo (a repo the harvest hasn't brought back yet gets them on its next sync); one held by another developer moves over. A match whose email is now linked to someone as a whole is skipped.
  * A branch already there (same name under the same parent) is reused and missing ones are added; each repo in the file goes to the file's project, even if it sits elsewhere now; a deed already there (same author, repo, day, title and time written) is skipped.
  * Restoring the same file twice adds nothing. Sealed deeds and re-linked commits count towards their buildings again at once.
* Older **Scriptorium backups** (`scriptorium-YYYY-MM-DD.json`, from before developers were included) still restore: they bring back the tree, repo placements and deeds. A file with a bad name in it, an unreadable list, or from a newer version of the game is refused whole.


---

## 18. The Watchtower (work analytics)

Server: [watchtower.ts](server/src/watchtower.ts) · Client: [WatchtowerModal.tsx](src/components/ui/WatchtowerModal.tsx), [watchtowerCharts.tsx](src/components/ui/watchtowerCharts.tsx)

For **Admins, Special Overseers and the Archon only**, from the **🔭 telescope button** next to the 🏆 Hall of Renown in the top bar (or the Watchtower card at the top of 👑 Royal Decrees, §15). It is a manager's tool, not a leaderboard: players never see it, and the server refuses its routes to everyone else. Every view but the last has **Week / Month** sub-tabs with ◀ ▶ and a picker (ISO week, Monday–Sunday; calendar month). Days are the commits' own dates, as for XP (§3).

**The roster** is every developer (Special Overseers and the Archon left out) with any commit, PR, review or deed in the 90 days up to the period's end. A developer on the roster with **no commit, PR, review or deed in the period is quiet**.

| Tab | Shows |
|---|---|
| **At a glance** | Tiles: active and quiet developers, meaningful commits and lines, deeds sealed (with their XP and how many await a seal), PRs merged, reviews, and the deed share of all XP. **Who is quiet** (days since their last activity and what it was). **Git XP vs deed XP** per developer (stacked bars: who works through Git and who through the Scriptorium). A **daily activity** heatmap (developers × days). **Commits by hour** on the author's own clock. A table of every developer's numbers |
| **Divisions & projects** | The Scriptorium tree (§17) with the period rolled up per Division / Client / project / repo: repos, commits (▲▼ against the period before), lines, deed XP, PRs merged, developers and stale repos; repos with work but no project under 📦 Unsorted. **Top movers** (biggest change in commits against the period before) and **stale repos** (in a project, with no commit, merged PR or sealed deed in the period) |
| **People** | Pick a developer: commits and active days, median lines per commit, the share of tiny commits (under 10 lines), PRs opened / merged, reviews given, deeds sealed against rejected, weekend commits, repos and projects touched; XP over the last 12 weeks or months by source (commits, PRs, reviews, deeds), commits per day and active hours |
| **Did the game change how we commit?** | The days since the game started (`GAME_LAUNCH_DATE`, §12, or the realm's first harvest) against the same number of days before it (4 weeks to a year): developers committing, commits per developer per working day, median lines per commit, tiny commits, commits per active day, PRs and reviews per developer, for the team and for each developer with 5+ commits on both sides, plus both trends week by week with the launch marked. Diffs are only analysed for the last 30 days, so both sides count raw changed lines. **The tell for gaming** is commits up while lines per commit fall: a developer whose commits per working day rose 25%+ while their median lines fell 25%+ is marked *⚠ splitting?* |

Numbers are computed on the server and cached (5 minutes for the running period, an hour for past ones; a deed sealed or rejected clears the cache).
